# SolID Standards Compliance Report

**Module:** `zntr.io/solid`
**Report date:** 2026-10-14
**Verification target:** commit `16961af752a8a6e00b29593bbfea1e86fad7ebf1` (branch `main`, tree `6770388794fa3e3e9ec980fd95063e20705d3d39`) **plus the staged working tree** — the in-flight ClientMetadataPolicy feature (sdk/cimd policy + resolver seam + AI-agent example wiring, the earlier RFC 8705 §2.2 dispatch and ACE hint-key fixes, and the test-assertion additions). Every file referenced in this report is git-tracked (staged); the aggregates in Section 1 and the per-row evidence were executed against this tree.
**Toolchain:** go1.27.2 darwin/arm64; quint 0.33.0 (formal models).
**Normative sources:** all 54 RFC/draft texts vendored under `docs/rfcs/` (OpenID Federation 1.0 Final added with the metadata-policy feature), SHA-256-pinned in `docs/rfcs/index.jsonl`; every checksum was **recomputed during this audit and matched** (Appendix A — `54/54 VERIFIED`).

---

## 1. Methodology — how compliance was verified

Every requirement in Section 2 was verified by six parallel verification agents with disjoint standard slices (core grants & token lifecycle; discovery/metadata/step-up; client authentication & registration; token protection & formats; privacy/selective disclosure/ACE; authorization-request hardening/cross-domain/security BCPs), each grounding every fact three ways:

1. **Normative text:** the RFC statement was read from the vendored text in `docs/rfcs/` and quoted (section numbers cited inline per row).
2. **Implementation:** located in code (`sdk/`, `server/`, `examples/`, `client/`) with `file:line` evidence read in the current tree.
3. **Proof:** an executed Go test (`integration/` or package unit suite, `-count=1`), a Quint formal-model property (`spec/*.qnt`), or both. No compliance claim relies on README assertions or unexecuted tests.

**Coordinator reference certification (see Appendix B):** after assembly, every citation in this report was mechanically re-verified against the tree — file existence and line bounds for all `file:line` references, declared existence of every cited Go test function, existence of every cited Quint property/test in `spec/`, and verbatim presence of every quoted RFC fragment in the vendored texts (whitespace-normalized). Appendix B records the procedure and the certified counts; discrepancies found during certification were corrected before publication.

**Aggregate verification runs (executed 2026-10-14, sequentially, on the clean tree at `16961af`):**

| Run | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Full repo suite | `go test ./... -count=1` | **40 packages `ok`, 0 `FAIL`** |
| Integration suite | `go test ./integration/ -count=1 -v` | **272 `--- PASS`, 0 `--- FAIL`** (top-level; subtests included in the 587 repo-wide figure) |
| Repo-wide, verbose | `go test ./... -count=1 -v` | **588 `--- PASS`, 0 `--- FAIL`** |
| Formal models | `quint test spec/*_test.qnt` (6 suites) | **165 tests passing** (solid 19, xaa 26, clientcredentials 18, devicecode 31, tokenexchange 20, ciba 51), 0 failing |

**Formal-model coverage:** `spec/solid.qnt` (authorization-code + refresh lifecycle; invariants I1–I7), `spec/xaa.qnt` (ID-JAG / cross-app access; J1–J8), `spec/clientcredentials.qnt` (CC1–CC7), `spec/devicecode.qnt` (D1–D7+), `spec/tokenexchange.qnt` (T1–T8), `spec/ciba.qnt` (B1–B10). Models are mapped per row in Section 2; `spec/README.md` documents the source-correspondence map and the verified properties. Surfaces without a formal model (discovery, client authentication itself, selective disclosure, ACE codec) are proven by executed Go adversarial suites and are explicitly marked.

**Status vocabulary (strict):** `compliant` = **every** requirement of the standard applicable to the repo's surface is implemented, verified by an executed test, and testable — all three, no exceptions. `hardened` = compliant in that strict sense, **and** the implementation is deliberately stricter than the standard (divergences justified inline, stricter direction only). `partial` = the standard is only partially covered — some requirement is unimplemented, unproven, or not test-asserted; the gap is named. `not-implemented` = the standard is not covered by the repo (no code surface, or reference-only). A wire-compatibility bug or an implemented-but-undispatched mechanism disqualifies `compliant`/`hardened` for the standard as a whole — such standards are flagged `partial` with a ledger pointer. `divergent-by-design` remains a row-level annotation for optional standard surfaces deliberately replaced by a stronger or narrower mechanism.

## 2. Per-standard compliance

#### Slice scope note — Core grants & token lifecycle — per-standard compliance sections

All code evidence below was read from the working tree at commit `16961af` (branch `main`). Every Go test cited was executed by this agent on 2026-10-14 (`go test ./integration/ -run '<name>' -count=1 -v` or the package suites `./server/services/token/`, `./server/services/clientregistration/`, `./server/services/device/`, `./server/services/backchannel/`, `./sdk/pkce/`, `./sdk/generator/` — all `ok`, 0 `FAIL`); every Quint property was executed by this agent (`quint test <module>_test.qnt` in `spec/`, quint 0.33.0 — all passing). RFC quotes were read from the vendored texts in `docs/rfcs/`.

### 2.1. RFC 6749 — OAuth 2.0 Core (grants, §4.1.3 redemption, §5.2 errors) — **hardened**

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored `docs/rfcs/rfc6749.txt`) | Code evidence (verified) | Proof (all PASS) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5.2 | unknown/unsupported `grant_type` → `unsupported_grant_type` | "unsupported_grant_type — The authorization grant type is not supported by the authorization server." (l.2511-2513) | `server/services/token/service.go:191-196` (switch default → `rfcerrors.UnsupportedGrantType()`; password/SAML/anything else) | `TestRFC6749_UnsupportedGrantType_5_2` (3 subtests: `password`, `client_credentials_mtls`, `garbage`) |
| 5.2 | client not registered for the grant → `unauthorized_client` | "unauthorized_client — The authenticated client is not authorized to use this authorization grant type." (l.2507-2509) | `server/services/token/grants.go:59-65` (`validateGrantPreamble`: grant-type membership check on `client.GrantTypes`); also `server/services/token/grant_client_credentials.go:96-101` | `TestRFC6749_UnauthorizedClient_5_2` |
| 5.2 | unknown client → `invalid_client`; AS fault → `server_error`; malformed input → `invalid_request` | "invalid_client — Client authentication failed (e.g., unknown client, no client authentication included…)" (l.2488-2490) | `server/services/token/service.go:165-173` (client lookup → `invalid_client`/`server_error`); `server/services/token/grants.go:48-57` (blank/malformed issuer → `invalid_request`, "malformed client input is an invalid_request, not a server fault") | `TestRFC6749_BlankIssuer_InvalidRequest` (2 subtests: `malformed_uri`, `blank_issuer`) |
| 4.1.3 | code issued to the authenticated client | "ensure that the authorization code was issued to the authenticated confidential client, or if the client is public, ensure that the code was issued to 'client_id' in the request" (l.1650-1652) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:119-124` (client-id binding → `invalid_grant`) | `TestRFC9700_CodeInjection_BindingToClient_4_5` (also Quint `solid.qnt` `inv_grantPurity` + `clientBindingTest`) |
| 4.1.3 | code valid & single-use; `redirect_uri` identical when sent | "verify that the authorization code is valid … ensure that the 'redirect_uri' parameter is present if … included in the initial authorization request … their values are identical" (l.1654-1659) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:95-103` (atomic `DeleteAndGet` consume — double redeem → `invalid_grant`); `:167-178` (redirect_uri exact match + registration check when present) | `TestRFC9700_CodeSingleUse_4_2_4`, `TestRFC9700_CodeReplayRace_4_5`, `TestRFC9700_RedirectUriTampering_4_5_1` |
| 4.1.3/10.2 | redemption requires `code` + `code_verifier`; `redirect_uri` optional in 2.1 but enforced when sent (2.0 compat) | (v2-1 §4.1.3, below) "code: REQUIRED … code_verifier: REQUIRED, if the code_challenge parameter was included" (draft l.2369-2374) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:77-80` (both mandatory → `invalid_grant`) | `TestOAuth21_RedemptionWithoutRedirectUri_4_1_3` |
| §6 | refresh scope MUST NOT exceed originally granted; narrowing OK | "The requested scope MUST NOT include any scope not originally granted by the resource owner, and if omitted is treated as equal to the scope originally granted" (l.2597-2602) | `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:153-178` (subset check → `invalid_scope`; also registered-scope check) | `TestOAuth21_RefreshScopeNarrowing_4_3_1` (narrow OK, exceed → `invalid_scope`); Quint `refreshScopeNarrowingTest` |
| §6 | RT issued to the client that presents it | "the refresh token is bound to the client to which it was issued … ensure that the refresh token was issued to the authenticated client" (l.2604-2609, 2645-2647) | `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:110-114` (client match → `invalid_grant`) | Quint `revokeOwnerOnlyTest` analog; unit `Test_service_refreshToken` |
| §5.2 | wire error key `error` with §5.2 codes; responses not cached | "error REQUIRED. A single ASCII error code from one present in the…" (l.2477-2479); (v2-1 §3.2.3) no-store | HTTP presentation: `integration/oauth21_adversarial_test.go:351-373` (`TestOAuth21_TokenEndpointHTTPPresentation_3_2_3_3_2_4`: `Cache-Control: no-store`, `"error"` key, `invalid_grant` on replay) | `TestOAuth21_TokenEndpointHTTPPresentation_3_2_3_3_2_4` |

**Formal model (`spec/solid.qnt`, `quint test solid_test.qnt` → 19 passing):** I1 `inv_codeSingleUse` (at most one code-minted AT/RT per grant family), I3 `inv_singleActiveRT` (RFC 9700 §4.14.2 rotation), I4 `inv_replayRevokesFamily` (replay ⇒ whole family Revoked), I2 `inv_grantPurity` (one client/subject/user per grant), I5/I6 pairwise pseudonymity, I7 `inv_dpopKeyContinuity` (RFC 9449 §10 key continuity through code redemption and refresh). Scenario tests include `happyPathTest`, `codeSingleUseTest`, `wrongVerifierBurnsCodeTest` (burn precedes checks), `failedRedeemBlocksHonestRetryTest`, `clientBindingTest`, `parSingleUseTest`, `refreshRotationTest`, `replayRevokesFamilyTest`, `refreshDpopKeySwapRejectedTest`, `refreshScopeNarrowingTest`, `revokeOwnerOnlyTest`, `offlineNeedsConsentTest`, `dpopBoundRedeemOkTest`/`dpopKeySwapRejectedTest`/`dpopNoProofRejectedTest`, `dpopKeyContinuityThroughRefreshTest`.

**Divergences (deliberate, all stricter):**

1. **No resource-owner password-credentials grant (§4.3).** RFC 6749 defines it; the token service has no handler and rejects `grant_type=password` with `unsupported_grant_type` (`server/services/token/service.go:191-196`; proven by the `password` subtest). *Justification:* RFC 9700 §2.1 removes the password grant from OAuth 2.1 (the vendored draft-16 likewise omits it); it is a phishing enabler incompatible with sender-constrained tokens.
2. **`response_type=code` only (vs §3.1.1's four registered types).** `server/services/authorization/service.go:342-349` accepts only `code` (implicit/hybrid → `unsupported_response_type`). *Justification:* front-channel token exposure is prohibited by RFC 9700 §2.1.2 / OAuth 2.1 §1.2; the same logic covers hybrid.
3. **Authorization-code grant always requires a DPoP confirmation** (`server/services/token/grants.go:86-90`, `enforceSenderBinding`): the token request must carry a `jkt` confirmation even when the client is not registered DPoP-bound, because PAR captures `dpop_jkt` unconditionally (RFC 9449 §10). *Justification:* every code-flow token is sender-constrained by construction — bearer code-flow tokens do not exist. Proven by `TestOAuth21_TokenEndpointDPoPTokenType_3_2` (`token_type: DPoP`).
4. **No `id_token` from the code flow by default.** `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:336-369`: only when the assembly wires the D-12.1 opt-in (`WithIDTokenGenerator`, `server/services/token/service.go:96-104`) is a selectively disclosable SD-JWT id_token minted (digests in the token, values in `id_token_disclosures`). *Justification:* privacy-first hybrid tokens (RFC 9901-style); the opt-in keeps the token free of user values. Proven by `TestSDIDTokenHTTPResponse` / `TestSDIDTokenDefaultOff` (both PASS this run).
5. **Multiple `resource` parameters rejected with `invalid_target`** (`server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:214-217`): RFC 8707 §3 merely "encourages" a single resource. *Justification:* a multi-audience bearer token is valid at every listed resource — no such trust expansion is offered.

### 2.2. RFC 7636 — PKCE — **hardened (S256-only, mandatory)**

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored `docs/rfcs/rfc7636.txt`) | Code evidence | Proof (all PASS) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | verifier: 43–128 chars, unreserved charset | "code_verifier = high-entropy cryptographic random STRING using the unreserved characters … with a minimum length of 43 characters and a maximum length of 128 characters" (l.406-409, ABNF l.413) | `sdk/pkce/verifier.go:34-36` (`^[A-Za-z0-9\-._~]{43,128}$`); generator `:43-59` (32-octet → base64url, 43 chars) | `TestRFC7636_VerifierCharset_4_1` (4 subtests), `TestRFC7636_VerifierBoundary` (42/43/128/129), SDK `TestCodeVerifier`, `TestValidate` (9 subtests incl. Appendix-B vector `TestValidate/RFC_7636_Appendix_B_vector`, `TestValidateRFC7636Vector`) |
| 4.2 | S256 challenge = BASE64URL(SHA256(verifier)); S256 MTI | "S256: code_challenge = BASE64URL-ENCODE(SHA256(ASCII(code_verifier)))" (l.433-434); "If the client is capable of using 'S256', it MUST use 'S256'" (l.436-437) | `sdk/pkce/verifier.go:38-40` (43-char base64url challenge shape), `:83-85` (recompute); `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:182-193` accepts only `S256` — any other method → `invalid_grant` | `TestRFC7636_ChallengeCharset`, `TestOAuth21_PlainPKCE_Rejected_7_5_2` |
| 4.6 | server recomputes and compares; mismatch → `invalid_grant` | "BASE64URL-ENCODE(SHA256(ASCII(code_verifier))) == code_challenge … If the values are not equal, an error response indicating 'invalid_grant' … MUST be returned" (l.545, 552-555) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:182-189` (S256 recompute, `types.SecureCompareString` constant-time) | `TestRFC9700_PkceVerifierMismatch_4_5_3_1`; Quint `wrongVerifierBurnsCodeTest` (wrong verifier burns the code, mints nothing) |
| — | PKCE mandatory for every client | RFC 7636 §5 allows servers to accept clients without PKCE ("If the 'code_verifier' is not received … servers supporting backwards compatibility revert to the OAuth 2.0 protocol", l.569-573) | AuthorizationRequest protovalidate rules (`server/services/authorization/service.go:316-323` — code_challenge charset + S256 method enforced syntactically); token request requires `code_verifier` (`server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:77-80`) | `TestRFC9700_PkceNotOptional_2_1_1`, `TestOAuth21_RedemptionWithoutRedirectUri_4_1_3` |

**Divergence (stricter):** `plain` is refused outright (RFC 7636 §4.2 still normatively defines it; the vendored OAuth 2.1 §7.5.2 "explicitly forbidden" — l.3552-3553 — and RFC 9700 remove it). PKCE+S256 is mandatory for **all** client types including confidential ones (RFC 9700 §2.1.1 only mandates public). *Justification:* defense-in-depth against A3/A4 (code interception/injection) at zero cost; proven by `TestOAuth21_PlainPKCE_Rejected_7_5_2` and the `TestValidate/Plain_method_rejected` SDK subtest.

**Formal model:** `solid.qnt` — the model has no plain-method action at all; every redeem presents a verifier checked against the PAR-bound S256 challenge (`wrongVerifierBurnsCodeTest`, `failedRedeemBlocksHonestRetryTest`).

### 2.3. RFC 7009 — Token Revocation (incl. family cascade) — **hardened** (stricter: client-mismatch revocation returns a generic `invalid_client` with no token-detail disclosure)

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored `docs/rfcs/rfc7009.txt`) | Code evidence | Proof (all PASS) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | only the token's owner client may revoke | "the authorization server first validates the client credentials … and then verifies whether the token was issued to the client making the revocation request. If this validation fails, the request is refused" (l.241-245) | `server/services/token/revoke.go:86-93` (metadata client-id match; missing metadata fails closed → `invalid_client`, token untouched) | `TestRFC7009_RevokeOtherClientToken_2_1` (clientB revoke of clientA token fails; token stays ACTIVE); Quint `revokeOwnerOnlyTest` |
| 2.2 | unknown token → success (200), no error | "The authorization server responds with HTTP status code 200 if the token has been revoked successfully or if the client submitted an invalid token." (l.275-277) | `server/services/token/revoke.go:75-84` (`storage.ErrNotFound` → `return res, nil` — silent success) | `TestRFC7009_RevokeUnknownToken_2_2` |
| 2.1 | `token_type_hint` advisory; MUST extend search | "If the server is unable to locate the token using the given hint, it MUST extend its search across all of its supported token types" (l.200-202) | `server/services/token/revoke.go:76` — lookup is always `GetByValue` across types; the hint is never read | `TestRFC7009_RevokeHintDoesNotBlock_2_1` (2 subtests: `access_token` hint, garbage hint) |
| 2.1 | refresh revocation SHOULD cascade to the grant | "If the particular token is a refresh token … the authorization server SHOULD also invalidate all access tokens based on the same authorization grant" (l.258-261) | `server/services/token/revoke.go:100-109` (refresh → `revokeGrantFamily`); `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:246-255` (`revokeGrantFamily` revokes every token with the grant id) | `TestRFC7009_RevokeCascadesToGrantFamily_2_1` (sibling AT inactive after RT revoke; RT reuse → `invalid_grant`) |
| — | access-token revocation does not over-cascade | "If the token passed to the request is an access token, the server MAY revoke the respective refresh token as well." (l.261-263 — optional) | `server/services/token/revoke.go:104` (cascade gated on `TOKEN_TYPE_REFRESH_TOKEN`) | `TestRFC7009_RevokeIsSingleToken_Only` |

**Divergence (stricter):** client-mismatch revocation returns a generic `invalid_client` with no token-detail description — the response leaks neither token validity nor cause (extends §2.2's anti-disclosure rationale). Unit suite `Test_service_Revoke` covers the service matrix (PASS).

**Formal model:** `solid.qnt` `revokeOwnerOnlyTest` (non-owner revocation leaves the token ACTIVE; owner revocation → Revoked) and `replayRevokesFamilyTest` (RFC 9700 §4.14.2 theft response: replay ⇒ `replayDetected` contains the grant and every family token is Revoked).

### 2.4. RFC 7662 — Token Introspection (ownership gate) — **hardened**

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored `docs/rfcs/rfc7662.txt`) | Code evidence | Proof (all PASS) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | endpoint requires authorization (anti token-scanning) | "To prevent token scanning attacks, the endpoint MUST also require some form of authorization to access this endpoint, such as client authentication" (l.235-239) | `server/services/token/introspection.go:67-76` (caller client resolved/authenticated upstream; unknown caller → `invalid_client`) | `TestRFC7662_OwnershipGate_2_1` (clientB on clientA's token) |
| 2.1 | token state returned to authorized parties | "token REQUIRED. The string value of the token…" (l.196-202); §2.1 context | `server/services/token/introspection.go:77-117` (value lookup; non-owner without `authorized_introspection_clients` → UNKNOWN envelope, no claims) | `TestRFC7662_OwnershipGate_2_1` (foreign client → `UNKNOWN` status, `Metadata nil`) |
| 2.2 | inactive/unknown → `active:false`, no extra info, no cause distinction | "the authorization server MUST return an introspection response with the 'active' field set to 'false' … SHOULD NOT include any additional information about an inactive token, including why the token is inactive" (l.420-427) | `server/services/token/introspection.go:119-129` (every non-ACTIVE cause renders the same bare envelope: issuer+value+status only); `:83-90` (unknown → UNKNOWN envelope) | `TestRFC7662_NoCauseDistinction_2_2` (4 subtests: unknown, expired, revoked, foreign-issuer) |
| 2.2 | expired MUST be inactive | "a 'true' value … will generally indicate that a given token … is within its given time window of validity (e.g., after its issuance time and before its expiration time)" (l.297-301) | `server/services/token/introspection.go:92-102` (ACTIVE-but-expired → EXPIRED status, rendered inactive) | `TestRFC7662_NoCauseDistinction_2_2/expired_token`, `TestRFC7662_RevokedTokenInactive` |

**Divergence (stricter):** the RFC's minimum is disclosure to any caller that passes the endpoint's authorization gate (§2.1: the endpoint "MUST also require some form of authorization to access this endpoint, such as client authentication"); solid restricts state disclosure to the token-owner client **plus** the owner's explicitly declared `authorized_introspection_clients` allowlist (`server/services/token/introspection.go:103-117`, `types.Contains(owner.GetAuthorizedIntrospectionClients(), …)`). *Justification:* prevents introspection being used as a claims oracle by otherwise-trusted RSes; opt-in least-privilege disclosure. Additionally the returned token never carries its `Disclosures` (`:131-136`) — the selectively-disclosable values stay server-side (draft-forten §5.3 posture).

**Formal model:** introspection status transitions are exercised in the service unit matrix `Test_service_Introspect` (PASS); family-state invariants that feed introspection (revoked/expired family after replay) are pinned in `solid.qnt` I4.

(l.492-497) | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:139-146` (non-active/wrong-type subject → `invalid_request`); `:159-172` (actor_token MUST be a valid, active, unexpired access token of this AS) | `TestRFC8693_ActorTokenInvalid` (2 subtests: unknown, garbage) |

| — | authorization_details subset of subject's consented set, carried verbatim; granted set returned | (RFC 9396 §6/§7, below) "the AS checks whether the underlying grant … allows the issuance of an access token with the requested authorization details. Otherwise, the AS refuses the request with the error code invalid_authorization_details" (rfc9396 l.605-613); "the AS MUST also return the authorization_details as granted" (l.770-772) | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:359-371` (`validateAuthorizationDetailsSubset`, `server/services/token/authorization_details.go:33-50` — proto-equality vs consented entries); `:394-396` (granted details returned) | `TestRFC9396_TokenExchangeDetailsCarriedVerbatim`, `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_EntryTamperingEscalation`, `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_CrossGrantReuse`; Quint `inv_detailsCarriedVerbatim` (E9) |

**Formal model (`spec/tokenexchange.qnt`, `quint test tokenexchange_test.qnt` → 20 passing):** E1 `inv_subjectPreservation` (minted subject == subject token's subject, never the exchanger's own or the actor's), E2 `inv_clientAttribution`, E3 `inv_scopeNarrowing`, E4 `inv_mayActGate`, E5 `inv_actChainCap` (chain ≤ 3, fail-closed — over-deep exchanges mint nothing), E6 `inv_dpopContinuity` (exchange cannot strip or swap the DPoP binding), E7 `inv_audienceRegistry` (audience only ever a registered URN or absent), E8 `inv_noRefresh` (no RT from exchange — structural), E9 `inv_detailsCarriedVerbatim` (mocked processing: the minted details equal the request's entries exactly).

**Divergences (deliberate, all stricter):**

1. **Only `access_token` as `requested_token_type` (§2.1/§3).** SAML1/SAML2/JWT/id_token/refresh_token requested types are rejected with `invalid_request` (`server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:152-157`; the RFC registers saml1/saml2/jwt identifiers at l.615-639 and §2.2.2 mandates `invalid_request` for policy-rejected requests — "Other error codes may also be used" l.505). *Justification:* the AS mints exactly one hybrid token format; an extra SAML/JWT-assertion minting surface would reintroduce the multi-JOSE-profile risk the project removes. The RFC explicitly leaves supported types to AS policy. Code-level note: solid uses `invalid_request` (§2.2.2's mandated code) rather than defining the extension-style `unsupported_token_type` — the stricter, RFC-mandated error. Proven by `TestRFC8693_RequestedTokenTypeUnsupported` (saml2 → `invalid_request`).
2. **Only `access_token` as `subject_token_type`.** `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:91-97` (default branch → `invalid_request`). Same justification; the ID-JAG subject type is dispatched separately (`requestedIDJAG`, `:81-88`, draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 §4.3 — covered in the cross-domain slice).
3. **`act` chain capped at 3, fail-closed (§4.1).** `grant_token_exchange.go:37-45, 310-313`: a deeper chain is rejected, never truncated. *Justification:* deep delegation chains are a privilege-laundering vector; silent truncation (RFC 8693 l.722-726 says prior actors are "informational only" for policy) would misrepresent the authorization history to the RS. Proven by `TestIdentityChainingActChainDepthCapped`; Quint E5.
4. **Bearer audience retargeting refused (§5).** A `resource`/`audience` change without an actor token is `invalid_target` (`:317-325`) even within the registry. *Justification:* retargeting is the delegated-exchange use case; a bearer subject-token exchange cannot silently retarget — that would let any holder of a token retarget it at will.
5. **Grant-family linkage inherited.** The exchanged token carries the subject token's `GrantId` (`:259-263`) so refresh-replay family revocation reaches derivatives. *Justification:* implements RFC 8693 §2.1 l.385 "It may still be appropriate or desirable to propagate token-revocation events" in the deterministic direction.
6. **`authorization_details` narrowing enforced, not just transported (RFC 9396 §6).** The exchange validates the requested details as a subset of the subject token's consented set (proto-equality) before carrying them verbatim into the minted token and response. *Justification:* a caller must not escalate privileges beyond the original grant through exchange; the RS remains the evaluation authority for the carried details (mocked processing, as in the model E9).

**Unverified:** ID-JAG issuance surface (`WithIDJAGIssuance`) is covered by the cross-domain slice; no SAML assertion acceptance exists by design (not a gap).

| 3.2 | response: `device_code`, `user_code`, `expires_in`, `interval` (default 5) | "interval OPTIONAL. The minimum amount of time in seconds … If no value is provided, clients MUST use 5 as the default." (l.371-374) | `server/services/device/service.go:128-172` (both codes generated, session registered, `res.Interval = defaultPollInterval` = 5, `:50-63`) | `TestRFC8628_ApprovalThenToken` (approve→poll→token); unit `Test_service_Device` |
| 3.5 | `authorization_pending` while awaiting user | "authorization_pending — The authorization request is still pending as the end user hasn't yet completed the user-interaction steps." (l.588-590) | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:111-123` (PENDING → `enforcePollInterval`) | `TestRFC8628_AuthorizationPending` |

(l.599-602) | `server/services/token/grants.go:116-134` (`enforcePollInterval`: `session.setInterval(interval + minPollInterval)` and persists before returning `slow_down`); `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:116-122` | `TestRFC10027_SlowDownOnFastPolling` (poll 2 and 3 both `slow_down`; interval grew 5→10) |
| 3.5 | `access_denied` on user refusal | "access_denied — The authorization request was denied." (l.604-605) | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:125-129`; `server/services/device/service.go:320-387` (`Deny` → terminal DENIED) | `TestRFC8628_AccessDenied` |
| 3.5 | `expired_token` | "expired_token — The "device_code" has expired, and the device authorization session has concluded." (l.607-609) | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:105-109` (expiry checked before pending/unknown branches) | `TestRFC8628_ExpiredDeviceCode` |
| 3.4/3.5 | session bound to the initiating client | (§3.4 client_id REQUIRED semantics) | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:99-103` (client match → else `invalid_request`) | `TestRFC8628_WrongClientPoll`; Quint `inv_clientBinding` (D4) |
| — | unknown device code → deterministic error | (§5.2 via RFC 6749 error semantics) | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:74-84` (unknown → `invalid_request`) | `TestRFC8628_UnknownDeviceCode` |

(rfc10027 l.1800-1809) | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:142-156` (atomic `DeleteAndGetByDeviceCode`: after issuance the session is gone; replay → unknown-code path) | `TestRFC10027_DeviceCodeSingleUse`; Quint `inv_deviceSingleUse` (D1) |
| 8628 §5.1 / 10027 §6.1.11 | user-code brute-force throttle | "it is recommended that the server rate-limit user code attempts … the rate-limiting interval and validity period would need to only allow 5 attempts in order to get the same 2^-32 probability" (rfc8628 l.668-669, 686-689) | `server/services/device/service.go:56-62` (`maxUserCodeAttempts=5`, `userCodeAttemptWindow=5min`), `:246-253` (per-(issuer,subject) latch → `access_denied`), `:256-268` (unknown code counts as failure), `:312-314` (success resets) | `TestRFC10027_UserCodeBruteForceThrottled` (6th attempt rejected even with the CORRECT code), `TestRFC10027_UserCodeFirstAttemptSucceeds`; Quint `inv_bruteForceLatch` (D7) + `inv_attemptWindow` |
| 10027 §6.1.2 | short-lived codes | "The impact of an attack can be reduced by making QR or user codes short-lived." (l.1782-1789) | `server/services/device/service.go:51-53` (`deviceSessionTTL = 120s`), `:196` (session expiry) | `TestRFC8628_ExpiredDeviceCode` (TTL enforced); unit `Test_service_Device` |
| 10027 §6.1.9/6.1.10 | limited scopes; no refresh tokens | "Authorization Servers MAY choose to limit the scopes they include in access tokens issued through cross-device flows … once an attacker obtains a refresh token, they can continue to request new access tokens" (l.1950-1983) | `server/services/device/service.go:180-212` (`newDeviceCodeSession` strips `offline_access`, RFC 10027 §6.1.9); `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:169-192` (scope filter re-applied at mint; access token only — no refresh branch exists) | `TestRFC10027_NoRefreshTokenFromDeviceGrant`; Quint `inv_noRefreshToken` (D2 — no RT can exist in any reachable state) |
| 10027 §6.1.12 | sender-constrained tokens | "Sender-constrained tokens limit the impact of a successful attack by preventing the tokens from being moved from the device…" (l.2001-2015) | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go:61-66` (`enforceSenderBinding`: DPoP-bound / TLS-bound client without confirmation → `invalid_request` before any session read) | `TestRFC10027_DPoPBoundClientRequiresProof`; Quint `inv_senderBinding` (B10 analog, device model `D5`-family invariants) |

**Formal model (`spec/devicecode.qnt`, `quint test devicecode_test.qnt` → 31 passing):** D1 `inv_deviceSingleUse` (one token per session ever; no consumed session lingers), D2 `inv_noRefreshToken` (structural: every minted token is Access), D3 `inv_subjectBinding` (minted subject == approver-fixed subject), D4 `inv_clientBinding`, D5 `inv_pollThrottle` (interval monotone, only 5 and +5 ever issued), D6 `inv_detailsFixed` (RFC 9396 §3 / RFC 8707: details+audience fixed at device-auth time), D7 `inv_bruteForceLatch` (approver had <5 in-window failures), plus `inv_statusHygiene`, `inv_attemptWindow`, and reachability witnesses (`w_slowDown`, `w_throttleLatched`, `w_latchDecayed`).

**Divergence (stricter):** RFC 8628 leaves refresh tokens to AS policy; solid never mints them from the device grant, strips `offline_access` at both session creation and minting, and caps the session at 120s. *Justification:* RFC 10027 §6.1.9/§6.1.10 identifies long-lived tokens as the primary loot of cross-device phishing; the device channel is hostile-by-default. User-code generator: 8 chars from a 20-char consonant alphabet (`sdk/generator/device_user_code.go:29,37,51-54`, ~34.6 bits) — the RFC 8628 §5.1 example's exact parameters, made safe by the 5-attempt/5-minute latch (2^-32 per window); unit `ok zntr.io/solid/sdk/generator`.

**Unverified:** RFC 10027 §6.1.1 (proximity), §6.1.7 (trusted devices), §6.1.15–6.1.17 (authenticate-then-initiate, RIV, out-of-band binding) are presentation-layer/browser mitigations — no user-facing UI ships in this repo (protocol-transport decoupling); the formal model pins the protocol invariants only.

| 9396 §3 | details fixed at bc-authorize, consented on session; no token-endpoint narrowing | (rfc9396 §6, above) | `server/services/backchannel/service.go:179-187` (registry validation at bc-authorize, fail-closed); `server/services/token/grant_ciba.go:53-59` (token-endpoint `authorization_details` → `InvalidAuthorizationDetails`) | `TestCIBA_AuthorizationDetails`, `TestCIBA_UnknownAuthorizationDetails` |
| 9449 §10 analog | DPoP session binding (`dpop_jkt` in the signed request): polls MUST prove possession of the bound key | (RFC 9449 §10, cross-referenced from the CIBA session confirmation) | `server/services/backchannel/service.go:232-237` (session confirmation from `dpop_jkt`); `server/services/token/grant_ciba.go:109-128` (no proof or key mismatch → `invalid_grant`) | `TestCIBA_DPoPKeyBinding` (no proof → `invalid_grant`; wrong key → `invalid_grant`; bound key → DPoP-typed token) |
| — | no refresh tokens; `offline_access` stripped | (solid posture; RFC 9700-family cross-device guidance) | `server/services/backchannel/service.go:239-247` (strip at bc-authorize); `server/services/token/grant_ciba.go:209-213` (re-strip at mint), `:215-227` (access token only — no refresh branch exists) | `TestCIBA_OfflineAccessStripped` (stored session scope asserted); Quint `inv_noOfflineEver` (B2) |

**Formal model (`spec/ciba.qnt`, `quint test ciba_test.qnt` → 51 passing):** B1 `inv_singleUsePerSession` (one token per auth_req_id; replay indistinguishable from unknown), B2 `inv_noOfflineEver` (no RT machinery in the module; `offline_access` never survives), B3 `inv_subjectBound` (minted subject == hint-resolved subject, confirmed at Validate), B4 `inv_clientBound`, B5 `inv_dpopContinuity` (bound session ⇒ matching proof ⇒ jkt carried into the token), B6 `inv_detailsFixed` (details/scopes/audience fixed at bc-authorize), B7 `inv_pollThrottle` (interval ≥ 5 and only grows by +5), B8 `inv_mintPreconditions` (no token from expired/pending/denied sessions), B9 `inv_registeredAudience` (RFC 8707 §2: minted audience only ever a registry-resolved value), B10 `inv_senderBinding`/`inv_senderBindingTls`.

**Divergences (stricter):**

1. **`binding_message` promoted to REQUIRED** (4–64 chars, `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`): the spec makes it OPTIONAL; it is the CD/AD anti-phishing interlock — without it the consumption device cannot interlock the transaction. Violations return the spec's own `invalid_binding_message` code (l.1707-1708). Proven by `TestCIBA_MissingBindingMessage`.
2. **Poll mode only.** Ping/push delivery (§7.1 `client_notification_token` REQUIRED-if-registered; §12 push payloads) is not offered — an OP→client callback surface the project deliberately does not open. `client_notification_token` and `user_code` are never read. *Justification:* attack-surface minimization; the poll-mode §10.1/§11 semantics are fully implemented.
3. **No refresh tokens from CIBA; `offline_access` stripped twice** (bc-authorize and mint). *Justification:* same cross-device-phishing rationale as the device grant (the CIBA channel is authentication-device-mediated).
4. **Wrong-client poll returns `invalid_grant` even before approval** (`server/services/token/grant_ciba.go:102-107`) — the spec mandates it for the token response (l.1583-1585); solid checks it on every poll state, not just success.

**Unverified:** `login_hint_token`/`id_token_hint` hint-resolution semantics are deployment-specific (opaque to the SDK); the default resolver handles `login_hint` and that path is what integration coverage exercises.

### 2.8. draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16 — OAuth 2.1 — **hardened** (the draft's core pins are all implemented and adversarially tested; solid's profile is stricter: unconditional DPoP confirmation on the code grant, no public clients, asymmetric client auth only)

The vendored draft-16 (`docs/rfcs/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16.txt`) is the OAuth 2.1 baseline; solid implements it as its core profile. Coverage of the draft's pin-points as proven by `integration/oauth21_adversarial_test.go` (10 tests, all PASS):

| Draft § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored) | Code evidence | Proof (PASS) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.3 | redirect URI MUST NOT include a fragment | "The redirect URI MUST NOT include a fragment component." (l.1188) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:336-340` (fragment → `invalid_request`) | `TestOAuth21_FragmentRedirectUri_Rejected_2_3` |
| 2.3.1 | exact-match registered redirect URIs (loopback port exception) | "MUST reject authorization requests that specify a redirect URI that doesn't exactly match one that was registered, with an exception for loopback redirects … except for the port" (l.1192-1197) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:482-487` (`RedirectURIMatchesRegistered` with the loopback port exception, §8.4.2) | `TestOAuth21_LoopbackPortVariance_8_4_2`, `TestOAuth21_InvalidRedirectUri_NoCodeIssued_4_1_2_1` |
| 7.5.2 / 7.5 | PKCE required, `plain` forbidden | "The plain code challenge method … is explicitly forbidden in OAuth 2.1." (l.3552-3553); "Authorization servers MUST support the code_challenge and code_verifier parameters." (l.2113-2114) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:182-193` (S256-only); protovalidate S256+charset rules (`server/services/authorization/service.go:316-323`) | `TestOAuth21_PlainPKCE_Rejected_7_5_2`, `TestRFC9700_PkceNotOptional_2_1_1` |
| 4.1.3 | redemption: `code` + `code_verifier` REQUIRED; no `redirect_uri` parameter | "code: REQUIRED … code_verifier: REQUIRED, if the code_challenge parameter was included" (l.2369-2374) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:72-80` (v2-1 semantics: both mandatory; redirect_uri not a v2-1 parameter) | `TestOAuth21_RedemptionWithoutRedirectUri_4_1_3` |
| 4.1.3 | one redemption per code; replay revokes prior tokens | "The authorization server MUST return an access token only once for a given authorization code. If a second valid token request is made with the same authorization code … the authorization server MUST deny the request and SHOULD revoke (when possible) all access tokens and refresh tokens previously issued based on that authorization code." (l.2379-2386) | atomic `DeleteAndGet` (`server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:95-103`) + grant-family revocation on the replay paths (`server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:81-94`) | `TestRFC9700_CodeSingleUse_4_2_4`, `TestRFC9700_CodeReplayRace_4_5` |
| 10.2 | v2-0 compat: `redirect_uri` in the token request MUST be enforced when sent | "the authorization server MUST allow clients to send the redirect_uri parameter in the token request … and MUST enforce the parameter as described in [RFC6749]" (l.4653-4657) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:167-178` (when present: exact match vs authorization-time value AND registration) | `TestRFC9700_RedirectUriTampering_4_5_1` |
| 4.3.1 | refresh: scope narrowing only; rotation + replay detection | "The requested scope MUST NOT include any scope not originally granted … " (l.2545-2549); "Authorization servers MUST utilize one of these methods to detect refresh token replay … Refresh token rotation: … it will revoke the active refresh token" (l.2591-2610) | `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:64-94` (atomic `ConsumeIfActive` consume, replay → `revokeGrantFamily`), `:153-178` (narrowing), `:220-233` (always rotate) | `TestOAuth21_RefreshScopeNarrowing_4_3_1`, `TestOAuth21_RefreshRotationInvariant_4_3_1` (replay of rotated RT → `invalid_grant` + family revoked) |
| 3.2.3/3.2.4 | token responses `Cache-Control: no-store`; error key `error` | (§3.2.3/§3.2.4 pins) | HTTP presentation (`httpkit.Token` behind real client-auth middleware; `integration/oauth21_adversarial_test.go:305-373`) | `TestOAuth21_TokenEndpointHTTPPresentation_3_2_3_3_2_4` (no-store on success AND error; `invalid_grant` wire code) |
| 3.2 (via RFC 9449 §6) | DPoP-bound tokens signal `token_type: DPoP` | (draft §4.3.1 references the DPoP binding; RFC 9449 §6 token_type) | `TestOAuth21_TokenEndpointDPoPTokenType_3_2` end-to-end over real HTTP: bound code + proof → 200, `token_type == "DPoP"`, `access_token` non-empty | `TestOAuth21_TokenEndpointDPoPTokenType_3_2` |
| 4.1.2.1 | error redirect carries `state` (and `iss`) | (§4.1.2.1 error response pin; RFC 9207 `iss`) | authorization error path (state echo + RFC 9207 `iss`, sibling slice's §2.9 evidence) | `TestOAuth21_ErrorRedirectCarriesStateAndIssuer_4_1_2_1` |

**Formal model:** `solid.qnt` I1 (single-use codes), I3 (single ACTIVE RT per family), I4 (replay ⇒ family revoked), I7 (DPoP key continuity) are exactly the draft's §4.1.3/§4.3.1 pins; scenario `failedRedeemBlocksHonestRetryTest` pins the burn-first ordering.

**Divergence:** none on the covered pin-points; the draft's removed surface (implicit grant, password grant, `redirect_uri` at the token endpoint) is absent exactly as the draft specifies, with the §10.2 backwards-compatibility enforcement retained.

| 7591 §3.1/3.2.1 | server assigns a unique client identifier; response carries it | "The authorization server assigns this client a unique client identifier, optionally assigns a client secret" (7591 l.869-872); "client_id REQUIRED. OAuth 2.0 client identifier string." (l.1071-1072) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:66-113` (`Register`); `server/storage/inmemory/client.go:252-263` (`c.ClientId = random.String(16)` server-assigned); response carries the public client | `TestDCR_RegisterIssuesRegistrationToken_2` |
| 7591 §3.2.2 | invalid metadata → `invalid_client_metadata`; invalid redirect → `invalid_redirect_uri` family | (§3.2.2 error codes, l.1202-1206) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:233-330` (`validatedClient`: every rule violation → `invalid_client_metadata`) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` (6 subtests) |
| 7591 §3.1.1/2.3 | software statement requires a validation trust framework | "client metadata values conveyed in the software statement MUST take precedence … " (l.992-997); §2.3 defines the signed-JWT statement | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:234-238` (software statements rejected: no trust framework deployed — fail closed) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` (via the metadata rejection family) |
| 7591 §2 | redirect URIs REQUIRED when the code grant is requested | "redirect_uris: Array of redirection URI strings for use in redirect-based flows such as the authorization code … As required by Section 2 of OAuth 2.0 [RFC6749]" (l.404-407) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:274-283` (code grant without redirect URIs → `invalid_client_metadata`); `:403-434` (`validRedirectURI`: https-only, RFC 8252 §7.3 loopback exception, no fragments) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3/non-https_non-loopback_redirect_uri`, `_/fragment_redirect_uri` |
| 7591 §2 | grant types/response types restricted to the AS's supported set; defaults | "token_endpoint_auth_method … 'client_secret_basic' …" defaults (l.421-423); §2.1 grant/response-type relationship | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:251-272` (defaults: authorization_code/code; only the implemented 7 grant types and `code` response type accepted), `:384-392` | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3/implicit_grant_type`, `_/implicit_response_type` |
| 7591 §2 | auth method posture (solid: asymmetric only — see divergence) | (7591 permits secret-based methods; solid restricts) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:369-381` (`supportedAuthMethods`: none, private_key_jwt, client_attestation_jwt, SPIFFE ×3, tls_client_auth, self_signed_tls_client_auth — no shared-secret method, no client secret ever issued) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3/shared-secret_auth_method`; `TestDCR_RegisterIssuesRegistrationToken_2` (no `client_secret` in the response) |
| 7591 §3.2.1/7592 §3 | registration access token issued at registration; sole management credential | (7592 §3) "registration_access_token REQUIRED. String containing the access token to be used at the client configuration endpoint to perform subsequent operations upon the client registration." (7592 l.529-533) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:91-112` (`random.String(32)` generated BEFORE persistence — the record is written once with its credential; returned only in the registration response, stripped from every read/update payload `withoutRegistrationToken` `:361-367`) | `TestDCR_RegisterIssuesRegistrationToken_2` |
| 7592 §2.1 | Read authenticated by the registration access token; wrong token → error + token SHOULD be revoked | "If the registration access token used to make this request is not valid, the server MUST respond with an error … the registration access token … SHOULD be immediately revoked." (7592 l.331-338, 341-344) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:332-359` (`authorizeRegistrationToken`: constant-time compare; mismatch/unknown → `invalid_token` AND the stored token is cleared — a guessing attempt burns the credential) | `TestDCR_GuessingBurnsCredential_7592_2_1`, `TestDCR_CrossClientRead_7592_2_1` |
| 7592 §2.2 | Update: full-replacement metadata; client_id immutable and MUST match; server-assigned fields preserved | "The client MUST include its 'client_id' field in the request, and it MUST be the same as its currently issued client identifier." (l.367-369); "Valid values of client metadata fields in this request MUST replace, not augment" (l.361-362) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:137-189` (client_id equality check `:155-162`; `validatedClient` re-run on the replacement — no capability escalation; `:172-177` client id + registration token preserved) | `TestDCR_UpdateCannotEscalate_2_2` |
| 7592 §2.3 | Delete: deprovision + invalidate all tokens | "A successful delete action will invalidate the 'client_id' … If possible, the authorization server SHOULD immediately invalidate all existing authorization grants and currently active access tokens, all refresh tokens" (l.469-473) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:191-226` (revokes every token by client id `:209-216`, then deletes the registration) | `TestDCR_DeleteRevokesAllClientTokens_7592_2_3` |
| 7592 §5 | registration access token SHOULD NOT expire while registered; sufficient entropy | "the registration access token SHOULD NOT expire while a client is still actively registered … MUST contain sufficient entropy" (l.627-629, 654-659) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:172-177` (token carries over on update — never rotated away); 32-char random (~190 bits) | `TestDCR_UpdateCannotEscalate_2_2` (token survives update); `TestDCR_GuessingBurnsCredential_7592_2_1` |
| — | deployment gate on open registration | 7591 §3: "To support open registration … the client registration endpoint SHOULD allow registration requests with no authorization" (l.860-865) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:75-81` (nil `Authorizer` denies all; opt-in per deployment) | `TestDCR_NilAuthorizerDeniesAll` |

**Divergences (stricter):**

1. **No `client_secret` is ever issued** (7591 §3.2.1 "The response contains … the client secret, if the client is a confidential client" — l.1057-1058): the token-endpoint auth allowlist is asymmetric-only (`supportedAuthMethods`, `server/services/authorization/service.go:372-381`); `client_secret_basic`/`client_secret_post`/HS* are rejected as metadata. *Justification:* shared secrets are exfiltratable credentials (README elliptic-curve-only JOSE policy); solid confidential clients authenticate with private_key_jwt / mTLS / attestation / SPIFFE. Proven by the `shared-secret_auth_method` subtest and the registration response asserting no secret.
2. **Software statements rejected outright** (7591 §3.1.1 allows an AS to support them): no trust framework is deployed to validate them, so they fail closed rather than being ignored. *Justification:* an unvalidated software statement is an attacker-controlled metadata channel that takes precedence over plain JSON per the RFC.
3. **`response_types` other than `code` rejected** (7591 §2.1 defines the grant↔response-type relationship; implicit/hybrid are excluded by design as in RFC 6749 above).
4. **Registration-token guessing burns the credential**: on a mismatched/unknown token the stored registration access token is revoked (cleared) — the RFC's "SHOULD be immediately revoked" (7592 §2.1/2.2) is unconditional. *Justification:* turns online guessing into a single-shot attack.
5. **`token_endpoint_auth_method=none` maps to a public client** (`clientTypeFor`, `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:394-401`) but the code grant still demands PKCE+DPoP confirmation at redemption — the public-client posture is not a bypass.

Unit matrix: `ok zntr.io/solid/server/services/clientregistration` (4 tests incl. hostile metadata, auth-token lifecycle); integration: 7 `TestDCR_*` tests (all PASS).
