### 2.10. RFC 8414 — OAuth 2.0 Authorization Server Metadata — **hardened**

Vendored text: `docs/rfcs/rfc8414.txt` (Proposed Standard, June 2018; sha256 `16c816e4…` per `docs/rfcs/index.jsonl`). AS-side handler: `server/httpkit/metadata.go:32` (serves the document, per-request signed_metadata per §2.1); document content: `examples/authorizationserver/metadata.go:32`; mounted at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` by `examples/authorizationserver/main.go:226`. Client side: `client/http.go:44`. Conformance suite: `integration/rfc8414_metadata_test.go` (6 tests) exercised against the real `httpkit.Metadata` handler.

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test, executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Document at default well-known path | "Authorization servers supporting metadata MUST make a JSON document containing metadata as specified in Section 2 available at a path formed by inserting a well-known URI string into the authorization server's issuer identifier between the host component and the path component… By default, the well-known URI string used is '/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server'." | `examples/authorizationserver/main.go:226` (mount), `server/httpkit/metadata.go:32-50` | `TestRFC8414_MetadataDocumentAccessible_3` (PASS) |
| 3.1 | GET request; path construction incl. terminating-slash removal | "An authorization server metadata document MUST be queried using an HTTP 'GET' request… If the issuer identifier value contains a path component, any terminating '/' MUST be removed before inserting '/.well-known/' and the well-known URI suffix between the host component and the path component." | `client/http.go:64-70` (client builds URL: parse issuer, trim trailing `/`, insert between host and path); `integration/rfc8414_metadata_test.go:245-254` (mirror helper) | `TestRFC8414_WellKnownPathConstruction_3` (PASS; pathless + `/issuer1` cases) |
| 3.2 | 200 OK + application/json | "A successful response MUST use the 200 OK HTTP status code and return a JSON object using the 'application/json' content type" | `server/httpkit/metadata.go:49` (`WithJSON(w, http.StatusOK, mdCopy)`) | `TestRFC8414_MetadataDocumentAccessible_3` (PASS) |
| 3.2 | Arrays for multi-valued claims; empty claims omitted | "Claims that return multiple values are represented as JSON arrays. Claims with zero elements MUST be omitted from the response." | protojson wire tags on `discoveryv1.ServerMetadata` (`api/oidc/discovery/v1/server.pb.go`, `EmitUnpopulated: false` serialization path) | `TestRFC8414_ArrayValuedMembersAreArrays_3_2` (PASS; 6 members asserted non-empty arrays) |
| 2 | REQUIRED members: issuer (https, no query/fragment), authorization_endpoint, token_endpoint, response_types_supported; jwks_uri https | "issuer REQUIRED. The authorization server's issuer identifier, which is a URL that uses the 'https' scheme and has no query or fragment components… response_types_supported REQUIRED… jwks_uri OPTIONAL… This URL MUST use the 'https' scheme." | `examples/authorizationserver/metadata.go:35-42,61` (issuer, authz/token endpoints, response_types); `integration/rfc8414_metadata_test.go:73-84` (fixture document) | `TestRFC8414_RequiredMetadataMembers_2` (PASS) |
| 2 | token_endpoint_auth_signing_alg_values_supported present when private_key_jwt advertised; `none` MUST NOT appear | "This metadata entry MUST be present if either of these authentication methods are specified… The value 'none' MUST NOT be used." | `examples/authorizationserver/metadata.go:62-70` (methods incl. private_key_jwt + alg list `ML-DSA-65`, no `none`) | `TestRFC8414_SigningAlgValuesConstraint_2` (PASS) |
| 2.1 | signed_metadata: JWT-attested metadata bundle | "metadata values MAY also be provided as a 'signed_metadata' value… MUST be digitally signed or MACed using JWS and MUST contain an 'iss' claim" | `server/httpkit/metadata.go:36-46` (clone + sign + attach per request; never mutates the assembler's document) | `TestMetadataServesSignedSuppliedDocument` (`server/httpkit/metadata_test.go:63`, PASS via package suite run) |
| 3.1 (+ security-topics-update-03 §2.1.2.1) | Client validates fetched issuer (mix-up defense) | §2: "The issuer identifier is used to prevent authorization server mix-up attacks"; security-topics-update-03 §2.1.2.1 pins the client-side retrieval+validation | `client/http.go:87-99` (empty-issuer and mismatched-issuer both fail client construction) | `TestRFC8414_ClientFetchesAndValidatesMetadata_3_1_2_1` (PASS) |

**Divergence (stricter, intentional):** the shipped client refuses *any* metadata document whose `issuer` is absent or differs from the configured issuer (`client/http.go:94-98`) — RFC 8414 itself does not mandate client-side validation; the hardening implements draft-ietf-oauth-security-topics-update-03 §2.1.2.1. Also stricter: the handler clones the document before attaching signed_metadata, so the assembler-supplied value can never be mutated by a request (`server/httpkit/metadata.go:34-36`).

**Verification:** all 6 RFC 8414 integration tests executed `go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC8414' -count=1 -v` — 6/6 PASS; `server/httpkit` suite `ok` (signed-metadata unit test included); `examples/authorizationserver` suite 4/4 PASS.

### 2.11. RFC 9728 — OAuth 2.0 Protected Resource Metadata — **hardened**

Vendored text: `docs/rfcs/rfc9728.txt` (Proposed Standard, April 2025). The OLD report's "partial (PRM surface untested/untracked)" verdict is **superseded**: `sdk/resourcemetadata/` (7 files) and `integration/rfc9728_adversarial_test.go` (11 test functions) are committed at HEAD 16961af (`git ls-files sdk/resourcemetadata/` confirms tracking).

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test, executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | `resource` REQUIRED; https scheme; no fragment (§1.2) | "resource REQUIRED. The protected resource's resource identifier… a URL that uses the https scheme and has no fragment component" (§1.2) | `sdk/resourcemetadata/document.go:48-60` (missing/`http`/fragment all rejected at decode) | `TestRFC9728_MissingResourceMemberRejected_2` (PASS) |
| 2 | `jwks_uri` https; `resource_signing_alg_values_supported` MUST NOT contain `none`; unknown members ignored (§3.2) | "jwks_uri OPTIONAL… MUST use the https scheme… resource_signing_alg_values_supported… The value none MUST NOT be used." §3.2: "any metadata parameters that are not understood MUST be ignored" | `sdk/resourcemetadata/document.go:63-71` (jwks_uri https), `:73-78` (none rejected), `:38-40` (unknown members ignored) | `TestRFC9728_NoneSigningAlgRejected_2` (PASS); unknown-member tolerance in `TestRFC9728_HappyPath_3` (PASS) |
| 3 / 3.1 | Well-known URL insertion between host and path/query; GET | "MUST make a JSON document… available at a URL formed by inserting a well-known URI string into the protected resource's resource identifier between the host component and the path and/or query components… By default… /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource"; §3.1 "MUST be queried using an HTTP GET request" | `sdk/resourcemetadata/url.go:46-79` (scheme check, fragment check, terminating-slash strip, suffix insertion, `"/"` in suffix rejected); `sdk/resourcemetadata/resolver.go:86` (GET via fetcher) | `TestRFC9728_WellKnownURLInsertion_3_1` (PASS; trailing-slash, query preservation, custom suffix, multi-tenant) |
| 1.2 | Identifier shape: https only, no fragment | "Resource Identifier: … a URL that uses the https scheme and has no fragment component" | `sdk/resourcemetadata/url.go:57-62` | `TestRFC9728_PlaintextIdentifierRejected_1_2`, `TestRFC9728_FragmentIdentifierRejected_1_2` (PASS) |
| 3.3 / 7.3 | resource member MUST equal the identifier used to build the URL (impersonation countermeasure) | "The resource value returned MUST be identical to the protected resource's resource identifier value into which the well-known URI path suffix was inserted… If these values are not identical, the data contained in the response MUST NOT be used." | `sdk/resourcemetadata/resolver.go:109-115` (simple string comparison; mismatch aborts with both identifiers named) | `TestRFC9728_ResourceMismatchRejected_3_3` (PASS) |
| 2.2 / 3.3 | Signed metadata must be validated; invalid/untrusted signature is an error | "The recipient MUST validate that any signed metadata was signed by a key belonging to the issuer and that the signature is valid. If the signature does not validate or the issuer is not trusted, the recipient SHOULD treat this as an error condition." | `sdk/resourcemetadata/resolver.go:121-128` (fail-closed when no verifier; verifier veto honored) + `SignedMetadataVerifier` hook `sdk/cimd/resolver.go:34-39` | `TestRFC9728_SignedMetadataUnverifiableRejected_2_2`, `TestRFC9728_SignedMetadataVerifierVeto_2_2` (PASS) |
| 7.7 | SSRF precautions (no redirects to attacker-controlled destinations) | §3.1: "any terminating slash (/) following the host component MUST be removed"; §7.7: "Clients SHOULD take appropriate precautions against SSRF attacks" — solid refuses redirects outright | `sdk/resourcemetadata/resolver.go:92-94` + `sdk/httpfetch/fetcher.go:111-115` (`CheckRedirect: http.ErrUseLastResponse` — 3xx surfaces as non-200 error) | `TestRFC9728_SsrFencedFetcherRedirectRejected_7_7` (PASS, real production fetcher) |
| — | Defense in depth: document size cap (not RFC-mandated) | (no RFC requirement — resolver's own cap) | `sdk/resourcemetadata/resolver.go:97-101` (64 kB cap enforced on the resolver, independent of the Fetcher) | `TestRFC9728_OversizedDocumentRejected` (PASS) |
| 3 / 3.2 | Positive resolution control; RS publishes metadata | §3 (document MUST be available at the well-known URL); §3.2 (200 OK, application/json) | `examples/resourceserver/main.go:40-59` (ResourceMetadata handler), mounted `examples/resourceserver/main.go:152` | `TestRFC9728_HappyPath_3` (PASS — resolver round-trip over TLS test server) |

**Notes:**
- The example RS publishes `resource: http://127.0.0.1:8085` (`examples/resourceserver/main.go:43`) — a loopback development origin, not https. This is a **demo-deployment artifact**, not a protocol divergence: the *consumer* side (`sdk/resourcemetadata`) rejects non-https identifiers outright (`sdk/resourcemetadata/url.go:57`), proven by `TestRFC9728_PlaintextIdentifierRejected_1_2`. The example also emits `WWW-Authenticate … resource_metadata` pointers (`examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:304,313,321,329,334`), matching RFC 9728 §5.1 challenge shape.
- RFC 9728 §4 `protected_resources` AS-metadata member: not advertised by the example AS (`grep` across `api/`, `examples/` finds no emission). OPTIONAL member; no test claims it. **Not implemented — optional surface, noted for the ledger.**
- `authorization_servers` member round-trips through `TestRFC9728_HappyPath_3`'s document but no AS-side cross-check (§4's "should be cross-checked" is a profile-level SHOULD) exists — out of scope for the wire-level resolver.

**Verification:** all 11 RFC 9728 integration tests executed `go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC9728' -count=1 -v` — 11/11 PASS (8 subtests additionally under `WellKnownURLInsertion`/`VerifierVeto`, 19 result lines total); `go test ./sdk/resourcemetadata/` — `ok` (unit suites for decode/resolve/URL).

### 2.12. RFC 9207 — Authorization Server Issuer Identification — **compliant**

Vendored text: `docs/rfcs/rfc9207.txt` (Proposed Standard, March 2022).

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test, executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | `iss` in authorization responses — success AND error | "In authorization responses to the client, including error responses, an authorization server supporting this specification MUST indicate its identity by including the iss parameter in the response." | `server/services/authorization/service.go:94-98` (set before any failure path); `server/httpkit/authorization.go:250-265` (query/fragment: `params.Set("iss", authRes.Issuer)` in both branches) | `TestOAuthSecTopics_CoatIssParameterPresent_2_2` (PASS — success and error paths) |
| 2.3 | Metadata `issuer` identical to `iss`; support advertised | "The issuer identifier included in the server's metadata value issuer MUST be identical to the iss parameter's value. The server MUST indicate its support… by setting the metadata parameter authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported… to true." | `examples/authorizationserver/metadata.go:35` (issuer) + `:80` (`AuthorizationResponseIssParameterSupported: true`); wire tag `authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported` at `api/oidc/discovery/v1/server.pb.go:321` | `TestRFC8414_RequiredMetadataMembers_2` (issuer member, PASS) + `TestMetadataServesSignedSuppliedDocument` (httpkit, PASS) |
| 2 (error path) | Error redirect still identifies issuer | "including error responses" (§2); §2.2 example error response carries `iss` | `server/httpkit/authorization.go:256-260` (error branch sets `iss`); `server/httpkit/respond.go:48-51` (WithError issuer adapter) | `TestOAuth21_ErrorRedirectCarriesStateAndIssuer_4_1_2_1` (PASS — `invalid_request` redirect echoes state + iss) |
| 2 (form_post) | iss in form_post response mode | §2 applies to "authorization responses" generally | `server/httpkit/authorization.go:405,419` (form-post template carries `iss` hidden field) | covered by `TestOAuthSecTopics_CoatIssParameterPresent_2_2` (service-level, mode-independent) |
| 2 (JARM) | JARM JWT carries iss; no additional iss needed when JARM used | "the JARM… contains an iss claim that provides the same protection if it is validated as described in Section 2.4. Therefore, an additional iss parameter outside the JWT is not necessary when JARM is used." | `sdk/jarm/encoder.go:55-83` (`Issuer: resp.Issuer` in both success and error claim sets) | `TestJARM_SuccessRoundTrip`, `TestJARM_WrongIssuerRejected`, `TestJARM_ErrorResponseStillBound` (all PASS) |
| 2.4 | Client validates iss against expected issuer (simple string comparison) | "Clients… MUST compare the result to the issuer identifier of the authorization server where the authorization request was sent to… If the value does not match… clients MUST reject the authorization response" | client-side metadata issuer check `client/http.go:87-99` (construction-time); JARM decode path validates iss: `sdk/jarm` decoder over expected issuer (`integration/fapi_jarm_test.go:95` — `jarm.Decoder(issuer, verifier)`) | `TestJARM_WrongIssuerRejected` (PASS — foreign-iss JARM refused) |

**Unverified (browser-side):** RFC 9207 §2.4's full client loop — extract `iss` from a redirect delivered to a browser, compare to configured issuer, reject on mismatch — is proven at the JARM layer (cryptographically bound) and at metadata construction time, but no test drives a user-agent receiving a plain redirect with a spoofed `iss` and asserting client rejection. The service contract (iss always present, always the configured issuer) is what the AS side MUST provide, and is proven.

**Verification:** `TestOAuthSecTopics_CoatIssParameterPresent_2_2` and `TestOAuth21_ErrorRedirectCarriesStateAndIssuer_4_1_2_1` executed — PASS; JARM suite 8/8 PASS.

### 2.13. RFC 9470 — OAuth 2.0 Step Up Authentication Challenge Protocol — **hardened**

Vendored text: `docs/rfcs/rfc9470.txt` (Proposed Standard, September 2023). AS enforcement: `server/services/authorization/stepup.go:39-73`, invoked at `server/services/authorization/service.go:159-167` before code issuance. RS challenge gate: `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:299-339`.

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test, executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | `insufficient_user_authentication` error code + `acr_values`/`max_age` auth-params in WWW-Authenticate challenge | "insufficient_user_authentication: The authentication event associated with the access token presented… does not meet the authentication requirements of the protected resource"; "acr_values: A space-separated string… The protected resource requires one of these values"; "max_age: …allowable elapsed time in seconds since the last active authentication event" | `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:319-337` (max_age and acr_values challenges, both with `error="insufficient_user_authentication"`); `sdk/rfcerrors/errors.go:194-199` (InsufficientUserAuthentication builder) | `TestRFC9470_RSChallengeRoundTrip` (PASS — `max_age=30` and `acr_values="…"` challenge subtests) |
| 3 | Both auth-params MAY occur in one challenge | "The auth-params max_age and acr_values MAY both occur in the same challenge" | `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:318-337` (independent checks, both emit into the same header) | `TestRFC9470_RSChallengeRoundTrip/challenge_drives_a_satisfying_re-authorization` (PASS — challenge carries both) |
| 4 | Client parses challenge and re-authorizes with acr_values/max_age | "A client receiving a challenge… SHOULD parse the WWW-Authenticate header for acr_values and max_age and use them, if present, in constructing an authorization request." | `integration/rfc9470_stepup_test.go:377-394` (parseChallengeParams) + `:474-485` (follow-up request built from parsed values) | `TestRFC9470_RSChallengeRoundTrip/challenge_drives_a_satisfying_re-authorization` (PASS — full loop: challenge → parse → re-authorize → gate passes) |
| 5 | Unmeetable acr → `unmet_authentication_requirements`, no token the RS will keep rejecting | "the authorization server SHOULD consider the requested acr value as necessary for successfully fulfilling the request… otherwise, the authorization request fails and returns an unmet_authentication_requirements error… will help prevent clients getting stuck in a loop" | `server/services/authorization/stepup.go:51-59` (acr membership in requested values; unmet → error) + `sdk/rfcerrors/errors.go:205-210`; invoked `server/services/authorization/service.go:159-167` before code mint | `TestRFC9470_UnmeetableACRRejected` (PASS — `unmet_authentication_requirements`, no code) |
| 5 | max_age freshness enforcement (auth_time recency) | §5 via OIDC §3.1.2.1: "if a request includes the max_age parameter, the authorization server MUST include the auth_time claim"; enforcement of elapsed time | `server/services/authorization/stepup.go:63-70` (`now > auth_time+max_age` → unmet; boundary `==` passes) | `TestRFC9470_StaleLoginRejectedByMaxAge` (PASS — 1-hour-old login vs max_age=5) |
| 6 / 6.2 | acr + auth_time ride the access token; introspection surfaces them | "the acr and auth_time claims… are used to convey the time and context of the user-authentication event"; §6.2 defines introspection members `acr` and `auth_time` | `server/services/token/generator.go:66-69` (both into TokenMeta), `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:278-296` (login event → token meta), `server/httpkit/token_introspection.go:123-128` (`resp["acr"]`, `resp["auth_time"]`) | `TestRFC9470_StepUpHappyPathRoundTrip` (PASS — token metadata + introspection both carry acr/auth_time) |
| 6.1 | Values established at user-authentication time; unchanged on renewal | "the values of those two parameters are established at user-authentication time and will not change in the event of access token renewals" | `server/services/token/generator.go:116-119` (refresh token meta carries Acr/AuthTime from the original event) | `TestRFC9470_RefreshRotationPreservesEvent` (PASS — acr/auth_time identical after rotation) |
| 7 | `acr_values_supported` in AS metadata signals honoring acr_values/max_age | "Authorization servers can advertise their support… by including… the value acr_values_supported… signals that the authorization server will understand and honor the acr_values and max_age parameters" | `examples/authorizationserver/metadata.go:91-94` (`AcrValuesSupported: ["urn:solid:loa:1fa:any"]`); wire tag `api/oidc/discovery/v1/server.pb.go:103` | `TestMetadataDocumentAdvertisesAcrValuesSupported` (PASS) |
| 4 (via RFC 9126 §2.2) | Pushed requests carry acr_values; enforcement at consumption, not push | §4: parameters are "OPTIONAL parameters defined in Section 3.1.2.1 of [OIDC]"; enforcement belongs to the AS reaction | `integration/rfc9470_stepup_test.go:262-329` (push accepted; request_uri consumption applies the check) | `TestRFC9470_PARPushCarryingAcrValuesNotRejectedAtPushTime` (PASS — matching event → token carries acr; mismatched → unmet at consumption) |
| — (hardening) | Fail-closed without a recorded event; degenerate events rejected | (stricter than RFC; RFC 9470 does not define the no-event case) | `server/services/authorization/stepup.go:52-54,64-66` (nil event / zero auth_time → unmet) | `TestRFC9470_NoEventFailClosed`, `TestRFC9470_Adversarial_MalformedEvents` (PASS) |

**Divergences (stricter, intentional):**
1. **Fail-closed on missing/degenerate authentication events** — a step-up request (acr_values or max_age) with no recorded login event, an empty ACR, or a zero auth_time is refused with `unmet_authentication_requirements` instead of minting a token the RS would keep challenging (RFC 9470 §5's own loop-prevention rationale, taken to the AS boundary). Proven by `TestRFC9470_NoEventFailClosed`, `TestRFC9470_Adversarial_MalformedEvents`.
2. **Interactive re-auth attempt omitted** — §5 (via OIDC §5.5.1.1) lets the AS "attempt to authenticate the user in a manner that satisfies" the requested ACR; solid's stepup.go validates the *recorded* event immediately (no interactive re-auth surface in this profile). Same error code, earlier decision. Stricter: no token is ever issued below the requested context.
3. **Boundary semantics:** `now == auth_time+max_age` passes (`server/services/authorization/stepup.go:67`) — matches OIDC's "elapsed" reading of max_age; tested in the SDK matrix (`server/services/authorization/stepup_test.go`, `Test_validateStepUpRequirements`).

**Formal model:** no Quint property in `spec/*.qnt` covers step-up/metadata (models cover grant lifecycles: solid, xaa, clientcredentials, devicecode, tokenexchange, ciba) — noted; RFC 9470 rows rely on executed tests only.

**Verification:** all 8 RFC 9470 integration tests executed `go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC9470' -count=1 -v` — 8/8 PASS (RSChallengeRoundTrip contributes 3 subtests); `go test ./server/services/authorization/` — `ok` (unit matrix `Test_validateStepUpRequirements` included).

### 2.14. draft-ietf-oauth-client-id-metadata-document-02 (CIMD) — **hardened**

Vendored text: `docs/rfcs/draft-ietf-oauth-client-id-metadata-document-02.txt` (revision -02, July 2026). Mechanism: `sdk/cimd/` (identifier/resolver/document), HTTP adapter `sdk/httpfetch/`, storage decoration `server/storage/inmemory/client_cimd.go`.

| Draft § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test, executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Client Identifier URL shape: https, no userinfo, path, no dot-segments, no fragment | "A Client Identifier URL: MUST use the https URL scheme… MUST NOT contain a userinfo component… MUST contain a path component… MUST NOT contain single-dot or double-dot path components… MUST NOT contain a fragment component" | `sdk/cimd/identifier.go:41-67` (each rule; port allowed; query accepted per SHOULD NOT; no normalization — simple string comparison by design) | `TestCIMDHostileIdentifierShapes` (PASS — 5 hostile shapes) |
| 3 | Simple string comparison, no normalization | "Client Identifier URLs MUST be compared using simple string comparison" (§6.2.1 RFC 3986) | `sdk/cimd/resolver.go:105-107` (`doc.ClientID != clientID` exact comparison) | `TestCIMDClientIDMismatch` (PASS) |
| 4 | Document client_id MUST match fetched URL | "The Client ID Metadata Document MUST contain a client_id property whose value MUST match the Client Identifier URL, which MUST also match the URL that the authorization server used to fetch the document" | `sdk/cimd/resolver.go:104-108` (pre-policy match) + `:118-123` (re-checked after policy application — the policy cannot rewrite the identifier) | `TestCIMDClientIDMismatch` (PASS — mismatch resolves as unknown client); `TestPolicyResolverClientIDRewriteRejected` (policy entry naming client_id rejected outright) |
| 4.1 | No shared-secret auth methods | "the token_endpoint_auth_method property MUST NOT include client_secret_post, client_secret_basic, client_secret_jwt, or any other method based around a shared symmetric secret" | `sdk/cimd/document.go:106-111` (all three methods rejected) | `TestCIMDClientSecretExfiltration` (PASS — 5 subtests) |
| 4.1 | No client_secret / client_secret_expires_at | "the client_secret and client_secret_expires_at properties MUST NOT be used" | `sdk/cimd/document.go:113-116` | `TestCIMDClientSecretExfiltration` (PASS) |
| 4.1 | No private key material; only public keys | "private key material MUST NOT be included in the Client ID Metadata Document; only public keys… are permitted" | `sdk/cimd/document.go:118-124` + `:177-203` (EC/OKP/AKP `d`, RSA `d/p/q/dp/dq/qi`, `oct` `k` all rejected) | `TestCIMDPrivateKeyLeak` (PASS — EC private, RSA private, oct symmetric) |
| 5 | 200 OK only; all other statuses are errors | "The Client ID Metadata Document MUST be served with a 200 OK HTTP status code. The authorization server MUST treat all other HTTP status codes as an error response" | `sdk/httpfetch/fetcher.go:189-193` (exactly 200 accepted) | `TestCIMDNon200AndRedirects` (PASS — 301/404/500 subtests) |
| 5 | Redirects MUST NOT be followed | "The authorization server MUST NOT automatically follow HTTP redirects when fetching" | `sdk/httpfetch/fetcher.go:111-115` (`CheckRedirect` returns `http.ErrUseLastResponse`) | `TestCIMDNon200AndRedirects/redirect_302` (PASS) |
| 8.6 | SSRF: MUST NOT fetch special-use IP destinations | "Authorization servers MUST NOT fetch a Client ID Metadata Document URL or any URLs contained within a Client ID Metadata Document that resolve to special-use IP addresses as defined in [RFC6890]" | `sdk/httpfetch/fetcher.go:140-151` (preflight), `:245-295` (24 IPv4+IPv6 special-use prefixes), `:171-175` (resolve-before-request; dial re-check guards DNS rebinding) | `TestCIMDSSRFSpecialUseDestinations` (PASS — 7 destinations incl. cloud metadata 169.254.169.254; loopback rejected in production fetchers) |
| 8.7 | Cap bytes read (5 kB recommended) | "authorization servers SHOULD limit the amount of data they read… The recommended maximum size to read is 5 kilobytes." | `sdk/httpfetch/fetcher.go:54-58` (`DefaultMaxResponseBytes = 5120`), `:195-206` (LimitReader, maxBytes+1 so oversized is detected not buffered) | `TestCIMDOversizeDocument` (PASS — 64 kB document refused) |
| 7.1 / 7.2 | Pre-registered clients take precedence (incl. URL-shaped identifiers) | §7.1: "The determining factor… is whether the authorization server fetches… a Client ID Metadata Document for that client_id"; §7.2: "An authorization server MAY pre-register Client Identifier URLs" | `server/storage/inmemory/client_cimd.go:55-68` (primary reader first; CIMD fallback only for identifier-shaped unknowns) | `TestCIMDPreRegisteredWins` (PASS — hostile replacement document ignored); `TestClientReader`, `TestClientReaderCIMDEndToEnd` (`server/storage/inmemory`, PASS) |
| 6 | AS metadata advertises `client_id_metadata_document_supported` | "Authorization servers that publish Authorization Server Metadata [RFC8414] MUST include the following property… client_id_metadata_document_supported" | `examples/authorizationserver/metadata.go:88` (`ClientIdMetadataDocumentSupported: true`); wire tag `api/oidc/discovery/v1/server.pb.go:347` | `TestMetadataDocumentAdvertisesCIMDSupport` (PASS) |
| 4 (decode) | Malformed documents rejected | (JSON document per §4; malformed → error per §5.1 "fetching… fails… SHOULD abort") | `sdk/cimd/document.go:75-81` (DecodeDocument) via resolver | `TestCIMDMalformedDocument` (PASS — not_json/empty/missing_id/null_id) |
| — | Redirect URIs validated (https or RFC 8252 loopback, no fragment) | (redirect-based flows require conforming URIs — the same rule the registration endpoint enforces; the CIMD draft defers to the OAuth metadata semantics) | `sdk/cimd/document.go:125-135` (`validRedirectURI`/`isLoopbackHost`) — validates the FINAL document, including metadata-policy-added URIs (gap closed during the policy feature: pre-policy, ToClient never validated redirect URIs) | `TestPolicyResolverAddedRedirectURIGate`, `TestPolicyResolverFetchedDocumentBadURIRejected` (PASS) |
| — | Metadata policy applied between the identifier match and the §4.1 enforcement | (OpenID Federation 1.0 §6.1.3.1 operators over the fetched document — see §2.52) | `sdk/cimd/resolver.go:109-124` (`NewPolicyResolver` seam: policy applies on the raw document before `ToClient`, so the credential/key-material restrictions validate the policy-applied result) | `TestPolicyResolverWorkedExample`, `TestPolicyResolverSmuggledSecretMethodRejected` (§4.1 order proof: a policy cannot smuggle a shared-secret auth method past enforcement) — PASS |

**Divergences (stricter, intentional):**
1. **Host allowlist by default posture** — `cimd.NewAllowlistFilter` (`sdk/cimd/resolver.go:104-140`) refuses identifiers not explicitly operator-authorized *before any fetch*: CIMD resolution is a server-side fetch triggered by an unauthenticated identifier value, so the host surface is pinned by the operator rather than the caller. The draft permits but does not require this; solid treats an unpinned fetch surface as the vulnerability. Wired in the example assembly (`examples/authorizationserver/main.go:123-132`: AllowlistFilter wrapping NewPolicyResolver); SDK unit `TestAllowlistFilter` (`sdk/cimd/resolver_test.go:159`) covers it.
2. **jwks_uri not dereferenced** — a CIMD document declaring only `jwks_uri` yields a client that fails authentication ("client jwks is nil", `sdk/cimd/document.go:93-98`): nothing in the AS dereferences client JWKS URIs today, so the field is carried but never fetched — stricter than trusting a client-controlled URL indirectly (avoids a second SSRF hop the draft's §8.6 warns about).
3. **No caching** — draft §5.2 caching is MAY; the resolver is cache-less by design (`server/storage/inmemory/client_cimd.go:23-25`), so "MUST NOT cache error responses / invalid documents" is vacuously satisfied; a caching decorator can wrap at assembly level.
4. **Slow-loris bound** — the draft has no timeout requirement; the fetcher enforces a 10 s wall-clock deadline over DNS+dial+TLS+headers+body (`sdk/httpfetch/fetcher.go:62-67`, `:165-169`), beyond the RFC's letter.

**Formal model:** none of the Quint suites (solid/xaa/clientcredentials/devicecode/tokenexchange/ciba) model CIMD resolution — noted; rows rely on executed tests only.

**Verification:** all 9 CIMD integration test functions executed `go test ./integration/ -run 'TestCIMD' -count=1 -v` — 9/9 PASS (28 subtests, 37 result lines total); `sdk/cimd` package 31 tests PASS (5 pre-existing + 16 policy-operator unit + 9 policy-resolver e2e/adversarial incl. the AI-agent `dpop_bound_access_tokens` default); `TestClientReaderCIMDEndToEnd` now carries 2 policy subtests (`PolicyAppliedBeforeEnforcement`, `PolicyViolationIsNotFound` — full fetch→policy→storage stack over TLS); `sdk/httpfetch` `ok`.

---

#### Slice scope note — Client Authentication & Registration — Compliance Sections

Scope: assertion-based and TLS/attestation-based client authentication (RFC 7521/7523, RFC 8705, RFC 9700 §2.5, draft-ietf-oauth-spiffe-client-auth-02, draft-ietf-oauth-attestation-based-client-auth-11, draft-ietf-oauth-security-topics-update-03 §2.1 AS-side) plus the client-registration surface that provisions client-auth credentials (RFC 7591 metadata posture). All RFC quotes verified against the vendored texts in `docs/rfcs/`. All `file:line` verified in the current tree (HEAD 16961af). Every test cited was executed by this agent with `-count=1` (not relied on the coordinator's full-suite run) unless explicitly marked otherwise. Quint properties cited were re-run via `quint test` by this agent (clientcredentials suite: 18/18 passing).

Status: **hardened**

### 2.17. RFC 8705 — Mutual-TLS Client Authentication (§2, §2.1, §2.2, §3) — **hardened** (all four surfaces implemented, dispatched, and test-proven — ledger #2 resolved)

Status: **hardened** — `tls_client_auth` (§2.1), `self_signed_tls_client_auth` (§2.2, now dispatched by `ProcessorSet.Select` — ledger #2 resolved, proven by `TestProcessorSetSelect/tls_cert,_client_registered_for_self_signed_tls_client_auth`), and certificate-bound tokens (§3) are all implemented, dispatched, and executed-test-proven; the divergences (exact DER byte-equality match, no chain re-validation per §2.2, fail-closed absent/malformed jwks) are stricter-direction.

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored docs/rfcs/rfc8705.txt) | Code evidence (verified) | Proof (executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2 | mTLS client-authenticated requests MUST carry client_id; AS MUST enforce the client↔certificate binding; failure ⇒ `invalid_client` | "For all requests to the authorization server utilizing mutual-TLS client authentication, the client MUST include the 'client_id' parameter ... The authorization server MUST enforce the binding between client and certificate, as described in either Section 2.1 or 2.2 below. If no certificate is presented, or that which is presented doesn't match ... the authorization server returns ... 'invalid_client'." (lines 247-261) | `server/clientauthentication/tls_client_auth.go:78-81` (client_id REQUIRED), `:144-147` (binding enforced via `TLSClientBindingMatches`); `server/clientauthentication/self_signed_tls_client_auth.go:78-81` same; HTTP layer hoists the PEM at `server/httpkit/client_auth.go:125` and `client_id` resolution at :120 | `TestRFC8705_TlsClientAuth_NoCertificateAndMethodConfusion` subtest "no certificate" (invalid_request) and binding failures (rfc8705_mtls_test.go:256-282); `TestRFC8705_TlsClientAuth_SubjectMismatch` (foreign cert ⇒ `invalid_client`, :239-250); `TestRFC8705_LiveMTLSHandshakeToAuthentication` — REAL TLS socket: handshake → `r.TLS.PeerCertificates` → PEM extraction → binding → 200; negative control with different cert ⇒ 401 (rfc8705_live_mtls_test.go:139-179) |
| §4 (refresh) / §7.1 | Certificate-bound refresh tokens: AS SHOULD bind the RT to the certificate and check the binding at refresh; for mTLS-authenticated clients RTs are certificate-bound via the client-authentication requirement | §4: "When the authorization server issues a refresh token to such a client, it SHOULD also bind the refresh token to the respective certificate and check the binding when the refresh token is presented to get new access tokens." ### 2.18. RFC 9700 — Security BCP, client-authentication aspects (§2.5, §3 attacker model, §4.2.4) — **hardened**

Status: **hardened**

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored docs/rfcs/rfc9700.txt) | Code evidence (verified) | Proof (executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2.5 | AS SHOULD enforce client authentication where credential issuance/confidentiality processes exist | "Authorization servers SHOULD enforce client authentication if it is feasible, in the particular deployment, to establish a process for issuance/registration of credentials for clients and ensuring the confidentiality of those credentials." (lines 445-448) | Enforced unconditionally: `server/httpkit/client_auth.go:74-90` — a client_id-bearing request for a non-public client without credentials is rejected 401 `invalid_client` ("missing client authentication"); the DCR surface issues no shared secrets at all (asymmetric-only allowlist, `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:369-381`) | `TestClientAuthMiddlewareConfidentialClientWithoutCredentials` (client_auth_test.go:207); `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` case "shared-secret auth method" ⇒ `invalid_client_metadata` (clientregistration_adversarial_test.go:299-304) |
| §2.5 | RECOMMENDED: asymmetric client authentication (mTLS per RFC 8705 or private_key_jwt per RFC 7521/7523) — no symmetric keys stored | "It is RECOMMENDED to use asymmetric cryptography for client authentication, such as mutual TLS for OAuth 2.0 [RFC8705] or signed JWTs ('Private Key JWT') in accordance with [RFC7521] and [RFC7523]. ... authorization servers do not need to store sensitive symmetric keys..." (lines 450-457) | Every supported method is asymmetric: private_key_jwt, spiffe_jwt/wit/x509, tls_client_auth, self_signed_tls_client_auth, attest_jwt_client_auth, none (public only) — `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:372-381`; no client_secret is ever generated or stored (search: no secret issuance path in `validatedClient`) | `TestRFC7523_AlgNoneRejected` + `TestRFC8705_TlsClientAuth_PKIBinding` + `TestSpiffeJWTClientAuthEndToEnd` + `TestABC11_HeaderTransportHappyPath_7` (all executed, all green — see per-standard rows) |
| §2.5 (audience binding, via §4.4 mix-up defense spirit) | Assertions must not be replayable across endpoints/issuers | §2.5 read with §3 (A1) web attackers: "Web attackers can use their own user credentials to create new messages as well as any secrets they learned previously." (lines 541-545); audience restriction is the assertion-profile countermeasure (draft-security-topics-update §2.1) | `server/clientauthentication/assertionclaims.go:86-91` + `server/clientauthentication/private_key_jwt.go:193` — aud==issuer OR exact receiving endpoint carried per-request (`server/httpkit/client_auth.go:162-166` sets `req.Endpoint = issuer + r.URL.Path`) | `TestRFC9700_ClientAuthAudienceBinding_2_5` — aud=`https://evil.example/token` rejected, no client resolved (rfc9700_clientauth_test.go:34-54); `TestOAuthSecTopics_AudInjectionCrossEndpointReplay_2_1` (PAR-aud assertion refused at token endpoint, positive control accepted) |
| §2.5 + §3 (A3) | Temporal windows: expired assertions, future iat/nbf, excessive lifetimes must not authenticate (assertion hygiene) | A3: "Attackers that can read, but not modify, the contents of the authorization response" (line 578) — captured-credential replay is in scope; RFC 7523 §3 (imported by §2.5) allows rejecting "unreasonably far" futures | `server/clientauthentication/assertionclaims.go:48-64` — 4-way temporal gate (5-min skew, 10-min max lifetime) shared by ALL JWT-profile mechanisms (private_key_jwt, spiffe_jwt, client attestation PoP, WIT) | `TestRFC9700_ClientAuthTemporalWindows_2_5` — 4 adversarial subtests all rejected (rfc9700_clientauth_test.go:60-114); `TestABC11_PoPMissingIat_5_1` and unit case "PoP iat older than the freshness window" (client_attestation_test.go:269) apply it to attestation PoPs |
| §2.5 + §3 (A3) / §4.2.4 spirit | jti single-use replay prevention on client assertions | §4.2.4 countermeasure (imported): "authorization codes MUST be invalidated by the authorization server after their first use at the token endpoint ... the authorization server SHOULD revoke all tokens issued previously based on that code" (lines 894-904) — the single-use discipline applied to assertion jtis | `server/clientauthentication/private_key_jwt.go:234-250` — atomic jti burn after full validation | `TestRFC9700_ClientAuthJtiReplay_4_2_4` — first use resolves client, A3 replay of the same assertion rejected (rfc9700_clientauth_test.go:119-145) |

### 2.19. draft-ietf-oauth-security-topics-update-03 — AS-side client-assertion audience rules (§2.1, §2.1.2, §2.1.2.1, §2.1.2.2) — **partial** (AS-side rows hardened and adversarially tested; client/broker-side countermeasures out of repo scope)

Status: **partial** — the AS-side acceptance set is restricted to exactly the two draft-sanctioned values (issuer identifier, exact endpoint), which the draft explicitly permits ("such an authorization server MAY still decide to restrict acceptance...", draft lines 437-443); the draft's client/broker-side countermeasures (connection-context redirect URIs, broker consent screens) are out of repo scope — no client/broker ships. AS-side rows are implemented and adversarially tested.

| Draft § | Requirement | Draft statement (vendored docs/rfcs/draft-ietf-oauth-security-topics-update-03.txt) | Code evidence (verified) | Proof (executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1.2 | Client-assertion audience: single value mandated (no arrays) | "The countermeasures described below mandate the use of single audience value (as opposed to multiple audiences in an array). This is because Section 4.1.3 of [RFC7519] allows the receiver ... to accept the JWT even if the receiver identifies itself with only one of the values in such an array." (lines 361-368) | `server/clientauthentication/private_key_jwt.go:189-192` (exactly one value); `server/clientauthentication/spiffe_jwt.go:152-155` (same rule for JWT-SVIDs) | `TestOAuthSecTopics_AudArrayWithInjectedAudience_2_1` (securities_adversarial_test.go:166-190); `TestRFC7523_AudArrayRejected` (rfc7523_clientauth_test.go:68-100); `Test_spiffeJWTAuthentication_Authenticate` case "multi audience rejected (sole-value rule)" (spiffe_jwt_test.go:154) |
| §2.1.2.1 | Preferred countermeasure: issuer identifier as sole audience | "Clients MUST use the authorization server's issuer identifier as defined in [RFC8414] ... as the sole audience value in client assertions." (lines 372-376); AS-side note: "an authorization server ... MAY still decide to restrict acceptance to its issuer identifier (Section 2.1.2.1) or the endpoint that received the client assertion (Section 2.1.2.2) as an audience value, for example, to force its clients to adopt the respective countermeasure." (lines 437-443) | `server/clientauthentication/assertionclaims.go:86-91` `validateAudience(aud, issuer, receivingEndpoint)` — aud==issuer accepted at every endpoint; `server/clientauthentication/private_key_jwt.go:193`, `server/clientauthentication/spiffe_jwt.go:156`, `server/clientauthentication/client_attestation.go:211-215`, `server/clientauthentication/spiffe_wit.go:231-234` all route through it | `TestOAuthSecTopics_AudIssuerIdentifierAccepted_2_1_2_1` — issuer-identifier aud accepted at /par, /token, /token/introspect, /token/revoke, /device/authorize (securities_adversarial_test.go:130-159) |
| §2.1.2.2 | Alternative countermeasure: exact target endpoint URI as sole audience; AS accepts only for the endpoint that received the assertion | "Clients MUST use the exact endpoint URI to which a client assertion is sent as that client assertion's sole audience value." (lines 399-400) | `server/httpkit/client_auth.go:162-166` — receiving endpoint computed per-request and passed to every assertion processor; `validateAudience` second clause | `TestOAuthSecTopics_AudInjectionCrossEndpointReplay_2_1` — assertion minted with aud=`<issuer>/par` refused at `/token`; identical claims with aud=receiving endpoint accepted (securities_adversarial_test.go:52-91); `TestOAuthSecTopics_AudInjectionTokenEndpointClaim_2_1` — the core §2.1.1 attack: assertion captured via forged `token_endpoint` metadata cannot authenticate at `/token/introspect` (:100-124); unit case "aud is a different endpoint than the receiving one (cross-endpoint replay)" (private_key_jwt_test.go:647) |

### 2.21. draft-ietf-oauth-attestation-based-client-auth-11 — Attestation-Based Client Authentication — **hardened** (combined-DPoP mode, an OPTIONAL surface, not shipped — divergent-by-design, narrower)

Status: **hardened** — DPoP combined mode (`attest_jwt_client_auth_dpop`, §5.2/§7.3) is NOT implemented (narrower-by-design: only the dedicated PoP JWT mode ships); the attestation-signing algorithm surface is ML-DSA-only in the example deployment (draft allows local policy).

The typ (JWT type) header MUST be oauth-client-attestation+jwt unless specified otherwise by a profile as described in Section 13" / "sub: REQUIRED. The sub (subject) claim MUST specify the client_id value..." / "exp: REQUIRED..." / "cnf: REQUIRED. ... ### 2.22. RFC 7591 — Dynamic Client Registration (client-auth metadata posture) — **hardened**

Status: **hardened** — software statements rejected (draft-consistent: §2.3 requires a trust framework, "The criteria by which authorization servers determine whether to trust and utilize the information in a software statement are outside the scope of this specification", lines 821-823), shared-secret methods excluded, asymmetric-only.

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (vendored docs/rfcs/rfc7591.txt) | Code evidence (verified) | Proof (executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2 (token_endpoint_auth_method) | Registration MAY request a token-endpoint auth method; values from the IANA registry; default `client_secret_basic` | "token_endpoint_auth_method: String indicator of the requested authentication method for the token endpoint. Values defined by this specification are: "none" (the client is a public client), "client_secret_post", and "client_secret_basic" — additional values can be defined via the IANA registry; if unspecified the default is "client_secret_basic" (condensed from the §2 bullet list)" (lines 426-443) | Hardened default: `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:243-249` — default is `private_key_jwt` (not client_secret_basic); allowlist `supportedAuthMethods` (:372-381) admits only `none` + asymmetric methods (private_key_jwt, attest_jwt_client_auth, spiffe_*, tls_client_auth, self_signed_tls_client_auth); `clientTypeFor` (:396-401): method≠none ⇒ confidential | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` case "shared-secret auth method" (client_secret_jwt) ⇒ `invalid_client_metadata` (clientregistration_adversarial_test.go:299-304); `TestRFC8705_TlsClientAuth_UnregisteredMethod` shows a private_key_jwt registration with an mTLS binding stays private_key_jwt |
| §2.3 | Software statements require issuer trust criteria (out of scope ⇒ deployment decision) | "A software statement ... MUST be digitally signed ... and MUST contain an 'iss' (issuer) claim denoting the party attesting to the claims... The criteria by which authorization servers determine whether to trust and utilize the information in a software statement are outside the scope of this specification." (lines 772-823) | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:234-238` — no trust framework deployed, so software statements are rejected with `invalid_client_metadata` (fail-closed rather than accepting untrusted statements) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` — all hostile metadata rejected with `invalid_client_metadata`, no client record created (clientregistration_adversarial_test.go:275-327); unit-side allowlist is the same map |
| §3.1 (error codes) | `invalid_client_metadata` = "The value of one of the client metadata fields is invalid and the server has rejected this request." (lines 1205-1209) | — | `clientregistration/service.go:237, 248, 259` — the exact error string surfaces as the RFC error code (`invalid_client_metadata`) on every rejected metadata rule | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` (6 hostile subcases: fragment redirect URI, implicit grant/response type, shared-secret method, empty jwks for private_key_jwt, non-https non-loopback redirect, all asserting the RFC code); `TestDCR_UpdateCannotEscalate_2_2` (RFC 7592 update path enforces the same posture) |
| §2 (redirect URIs / jwks) | redirect_uris REQUIRED for redirect-based flows; jwks/jwks_uri MUST NOT both be set (line 604) | "clients using flows with redirection MUST register their redirection URI values" (lines 419-421); "The 'jwks_uri' and 'jwks' parameters MUST NOT both be..." (line 604) | `clientregistration/service.go:263+` (response types restricted to code — no implicit/hybrid, see :251-263 region), `:403-418` `validRedirectURI` (https OR RFC 8252 §7.3 loopback, no fragments); jwks required for key-based methods (unit/integration cases) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` cases "fragment redirect uri" / "non-https non-loopback redirect uri" / "implicit grant type" / "implicit response type" / "empty jwks for private_key_jwt" (all executed, all green) |

### 2.23. Unverified / out-of-scope notes

- **Chain validation for PKI tls_client_auth is a deployment concern** (RFC 8705 §6.1: the TLS stack validates the chain; `server/clientauthentication/tls_client_auth.go:44-48` documents that the processor MUST NOT re-validate). The live-mTLS test validates the handshake-to-binding chain over a real socket with a self-signed server cert; production root-pool configuration is deployment-specific and untested here.
- **SPIFFE bundle freshness/polling** (draft §6.1 "The authorization server SHOULD periodically poll the bundle endpoint"): the shipped `StaticBundleSource` has no polling; bundle rotation is a deployment concern. Not proven by tests.
- **Client-side countermeasures** of draft-security-topics-update-03 (§2.2 COAT connection-context redirect URI, §2.3/§2.4 browser-side session/consent checks): AS-side invariants only are proven here (the client-side checks are the client's responsibility); the securities tests pin the AS-side boundaries the client relies on.
- **Quint coverage**: client authentication itself is deliberately abstracted as a closed client registry in every model (spec/solid.qnt:24-25, clientcredentials.qnt:40); the client-auth facts in this slice are proven by executed Go tests, with the Quint properties cited where the auth RESULT feeds modeled behavior (CC5b TLS-binding policy on minted tokens).
- The coordinator's aggregate full-suite run is not relied upon for any row above; every test named in this artifact was executed individually by this agent on the current tree.

---

#### Slice scope note — Token Protection & Formats — Compliance Sections

Scope: RFC 9449 (DPoP), RFC 6750 (Bearer usage), RFC 7800 (PoP key semantics), RFC 7519 (JWT) + RFC 8725 (JWT BCP), RFC 8392 (CWT), RFC 9964 (ML-DSA in JOSE/COSE), draft-ietf-jose-hpke-encrypt-22 (HPKE+JWE), draft-ietf-cose-hpke-27 (HPKE+COSE), RFC 7797 (b64), RFC 9052 (COSE structures), RFC 8747 (CWT PoP cnf).

All RFC statements quoted/condensed from the vendored texts in `docs/rfcs/`. All file:line references verified against HEAD `16961af` (branch `main`). Every test cited was executed by this agent with `-count=1` (invocations listed per section); Quint properties executed via `quint test` (both suites pass).

### 2.24. RFC 9449 — OAuth 2.0 DPoP — **hardened** (unconditional DPoP confirmation on the authorization_code grant, one posture point beyond the RFC)

Code: `sdk/dpop/` (prover, verifier, claims, options), `server/httpkit/token.go` + `server/httpkit/par.go` (AS-side proof gates), `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go` / `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go` (binding continuity), `sdk/token/jwt/` (proof serialization).
Formal model: `spec/solid.qnt` (jkt binding on code redeem + refresh continuity), `spec/clientcredentials.qnt` (DpopBoundAccessTokens policy).

| 4.2 | Required claims: jti (unique), htm, htu (no query/fragment), iat | "The payload of a DPoP proof MUST contain at least the following claims: jti … htm … htu … iat" (jti uniqueness ≥96 bits pseudo-random) | `sdk/dpop/claims.go:20-31` (proofClaims); `sdk/dpop/verifier.go:259-266` (blank jti rejected — blanket-jti poison defense); `sdk/dpop/prover.go:85-90` (jti from `random.String(16)` — crypto/rand, rejection-sampled, `sdk/random/string.go:36-49`, 16×62⁹⁶ ≫ 96 bits); `sdk/dpop/verifier.go:229-247` (htm case-insensitive match per RFC 9110; htu match ignoring query/fragment via `normalizedURLEqual`) | `TestRFC9700_DPoPHtmHtuBinding_4_10_1` (method swap / uri swap / exact match); `TestRFC9700_DPoPIatWindow_4_10_1` (fresh proof verifies, `"not-a-jwt"` rejected); `Test_defaultVerifier_Verify` (sdk/dpop, mock-table incl. jti/htm/htu/iat cases) — all run, PASS | compliant |

| 4.3 | jti single-use (replay prevention) | "jti: Unique identifier … The jti can be used by the server for replay detection and prevention" (§4.3 list item 11–12 context; §11.1 replay) | `sdk/dpop/verifier.go:157-161` (cache checked LAST — a rejected proof does not burn its jti) + `276-290` (`RegisterIfAbsent` atomic check-and-insert); `sdk/dpop/api.go:34-45` (ProofStore contract: "Replay burns MUST use this operation") | `TestRFC9700_DPoPJtiReplay_4_10_1` (second presentation rejected); `TestAdversarial_ConcurrentProofBurn` (32 racing claims, exactly 1 wins); `TestAdversarial_SequentialProofBurnReplay` — all run, PASS | compliant |

| 5 | Token request carries DPoP proof; invalid proof → `invalid_dpop_proof` | "the authorization server issues an error response per Section 5.2 of [RFC6749] with invalid_dpop_proof as the value of the error parameter" | `server/httpkit/token.go:99-110` (proof gate + `applyDPoPConfirmation`); `server/httpkit/token.go:235-258` (verification failure → `rfcerrors.InvalidDPoPProof()`); `sdk/rfcerrors/errors.go:120-123` | `TestRFC9449_DpopJktBinding_Match` / `TestRFC9449_DpopJktBinding_10` (proof verified through the token endpoint path); `TestRFC9700_DPoPIatWindow_4_10_1` — run, PASS | compliant |
| 6.1 | cnf.jkt = base64url(SHA-256 JWK thumbprint) | "jkt: … the base64url encoding … of the JWK SHA-256 Thumbprint … of the DPoP public key" | `sdk/dpop/verifier.go:115-119` (`PublicKeyThumbPrint` of the proof key returned as the confirmation); `sdk/token/confirmation.go:29-52` (wire member `jkt`); `sdk/token/access_token.go:112-116` (cnf emitted in AT claims) | `Test_accessTokenGenerator_Generate_Confirmation` (sdk/token; exact `jkt`/`x5t#S256` member names); `TestRFC9449_DpopJktBinding_Match` — run, PASS | compliant |

| 8 | DPoP-bound refresh token must be refreshed under the same key | "the refresh token MUST be bound to the respective public key. The binding MUST be validated when the refresh token is later presented" | `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:130-143` (stored jkt must match presented confirmation, else `invalid_grant`) | `TestAdversarial_RefreshDPoPKeyContinuity` (no confirmation / foreign jkt both `invalid_grant`) — run, PASS | compliant |
| 10 | Authorization code binding via `dpop_jkt`; mismatch MUST reject | "the authorization server computes the JWK Thumbprint of the proof-of-possession public key in the DPoP proof and verifies that it matches the dpop_jkt parameter value … If they do not match, it MUST reject the request." | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:126-160` (session-bound jkt and grant-carried dpop_jkt both enforced with `SecureCompareString`); `server/httpkit/par.go:72-78,100-108` (PAR proof verified, jkt captured as confirmation); `server/httpkit/backchannel_authorization.go:114` (`dpop_jkt` form value) | `TestRFC9449_DpopJktBinding_10` (K2-bound code + K1 proof → `invalid_grant`); `TestRFC9449_DpopJktBinding_Match` (positive control) — run, PASS | hardened — the authorization_code grant requires a DPoP confirmation unconditionally (`server/services/token/grants.go:72-90`), stricter than the RFC's OPTIONAL dpop_jkt |
| 5.2 | `dpop_bound_access_tokens` clients must always prove | "If the value is true, the authorization server MUST reject token requests from the client that do not contain the DPoP header." | `server/services/token/grants.go:91-94` (enforceSenderBinding); `server/httpkit/token.go:103-106` (HTTP gate); `server/services/token/sender_binding_probe_test.go:14-50` | `TestEnforceSenderBinding` (server/services/token; dpop-required/none/wrong-type matrix) — run, PASS; `dpopMissingProofRejectedTest` (spec/clientcredentials.qnt) — run, PASS | compliant |

Formal model mapping: `spec/solid.qnt` carries `jkt` on PAR registration, code session, and minted tokens (`spec/solid.qnt:72,83,103`) with the §10 redeem gate at `spec/solid.qnt:251-267`; properties `dpopBoundRedeemOkTest`, `dpopKeySwapRejectedTest`, `dpopNoProofRejectedTest` (§10) and `dpopKeyContinuityThroughRefreshTest`, `refreshDpopKeySwapRejectedTest` (§8) all pass (`quint test spec/solid_test.qnt` → 19 passing). `spec/clientcredentials.qnt:94,109` models `DpopBoundAccessTokens` + `jkt` with `dpopMissingProofRejectedTest` and `unboundWithProofAcceptedTest` (18 passing).

Verification runs (this agent): `go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC9449|TestRFC9700_DPoP|TestAdversarial_ConcurrentProofBurn|TestAdversarial_SequentialProofBurnReplay|TestAdversarial_RefreshDPoPKeyContinuity' -count=1 -v` → 11 top-level PASS; `go test ./sdk/dpop/ -count=1` → ok (6 tests); `go test ./server/services/token/ -run TestEnforceSenderBinding` → PASS.

Per-standard verdict: **hardened** (unconditional DPoP confirmation on the authorization_code grant — stricter than the RFC; all other divergences stricter). Executed tests: 12 integration + 7 sdk/dpop + 1 server unit cited above.

### 2.25. RFC 6750 — OAuth 2.0 Bearer Token Usage — **hardened** (§2.1 + §3/§3.1 challenges fully proven end-to-end over HTTP; Authorization-header only — the weaker §2.2/§2.3 transmission methods deliberately unsupported)

Code: `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go` (reference resource server), `server/httpkit/token.go` / `server/httpkit/token_introspection.go` (token_type emission).

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence | Proof | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | Bearer via Authorization header | "Clients SHOULD make authenticated requests with a bearer token using the 'Authorization' request header field with the 'Bearer' HTTP authorization scheme. Resource servers MUST support this method." | `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:136-143` (`authenticateWithBearer` splits the Authorization header, scheme compare); dispatch at `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:255-269` (Authorization header prefix; unsupported scheme → 400) | `TestRFC8705_BearerRequiresMatchingCertificate` (integration; exercises `sdktoken.CertificateBound`, "the exact logic of the example RS middleware" per its doc comment — includes the jkt-only token rejected under Bearer at `integration/rfc8705_mtls_test.go:547`) — run, PASS | compliant |
| 2 / 2.2 / 2.3 | No more than one token transmission method; form-body and query methods | "Clients MUST NOT use more than one method to transmit the token in each request." (form-body §2.2 and URI query §2.3 are the alternatives, both discouraged) | The reference RS reads **only** the Authorization header (`middleware.go:140,186,192`); no `access_token` form/query parameter is parsed anywhere in the RS path — the weaker §2.2/§2.3 methods are simply not implemented | Same as above (the surface itself admits exactly one method); no `access_token` query/form acceptance exists to test — noted as design absence, not test absence | divergent-by-design (stricter: header-only, the two alternative transmission methods are not supported) |
| 3 | WWW-Authenticate challenge on missing/invalid token | "the resource server MUST include the HTTP 'WWW-Authenticate' response header field … All challenges defined by this specification MUST use the auth-scheme value 'Bearer'." | `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:259,273` (no token / auth failure → `Bearer error="invalid_request"/"invalid_token"` challenges); `checkTokenEligibility` emits `invalid_token`, `insufficient_scope` with `scope` attribute, `insufficient_user_authentication` | `TestRFC6750_ChallengeNoToken`, `TestRFC6750_ChallengeInvalidToken` — the real `Authorizer` middleware driven over `httptest`, Bearer scheme + error codes asserted on the wire | compliant |
| 3.1 | `invalid_token` semantics | "The access token provided is expired, revoked, malformed, or invalid for other reasons. The resource SHOULD respond with the HTTP 401" | `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:157-167` (inactive token, cnf-carrying token used as Bearer, certificate-binding mismatch all reject); `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:273-276` (401 + invalid_token challenge) | `TestRFC8705_BearerRequiresMatchingCertificate` (inactive/foreign-cert/jkt-only cases) — run, PASS | compliant |
| 3.1 | `insufficient_scope` with 403 | "The request requires higher privileges … SHOULD respond with the HTTP 403 … MAY include the 'scope' attribute" | `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:309-316` (scope check → 403 + `insufficient_scope` + `scope` attribute) | `TestRFC6750_ChallengeInsufficientScope` — 403 + Bearer challenge + `scope="timestamp:read"` asserted on the wire | compliant |
| — | jkt-bound token MUST NOT be replayed as a Bearer | (Follows from RFC 9449 §7: DPoP-bound tokens require the proof; RFC 6750 Bearer carries no proof) | `examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:159-160` — a token whose introspection carries `cnf.jkt` is rejected under Bearer: "token requires a PoP proof to be used"; DPoP path requires scheme `DPoP` + proof + jkt match (`examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:182-243`); jkt+x5t token requires both proofs (`examples/resourceserver/middleware.go:220-226`) | `TestRFC8705_BearerRequiresMatchingCertificate` (`integration/rfc8705_mtls_test.go:545-547`: a jkt-only confirmation fails `CertificateBound` under Bearer) — run, PASS | compliant |

Per-standard verdict: **hardened** — §2.1 (Authorization-header Bearer), the sender-binding logic, and the §3/§3.1 challenge emission are all proven by executed tests: the earlier "package main is untestable" premise was wrong (Go tests a same-package `package main`), so `examples/resourceserver/middleware_test.go` now drives the real `Authorizer` over `httptest` with a fake introspection client — six tests assert the Bearer scheme, `invalid_request`/`invalid_token`/`insufficient_scope`+scope-attribute/`insufficient_user_authentication` challenges, and the happy path. §2.2/§2.3 are unsupported by design (stricter: header-only).

### 2.26. RFC 7800 — Proof-of-Possession Key Semantics for JWTs — **compliant**

Code: `sdk/token/confirmation.go`, `sdk/token/access_token.go`, `sdk/token/introspection.go`, `sdk/token/confirmation_x5t.go`.

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence | Proof | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 / 3.1 | `cnf` claim carries the PoP key confirmation members | "By including a 'cnf' (confirmation) claim in a JWT, the issuer of the JWT declares that the presenter possesses a particular key … The value of the 'cnf' claim is a JSON object and the members of that object identify the proof-of-possession key." | `sdk/token/access_token.go:92,112-116` (cnf in AT claim set, JSON + CBOR label 102); `sdk/token/confirmation.go:29-52` (`JSONConfirmation` adapter restoring the wire member names — protojson would emit `x5t_s256`) | `Test_accessTokenGenerator_Generate_Confirmation` (sdk/token; asserts `"jkt"`, `"x5t#S256"`, NOT `"x5t_s256"`) — run, PASS | compliant |
| 3.1 | At most one key representation in cnf | "The 'cnf' claim value MUST represent only a single proof-of-possession key; thus, at most one of the 'jwk', 'jwe', and 'jku' … confirmation values defined below may be present." | `sdk/token/confirmation.go:36-39` carries exactly the two registered method members this deployment uses (`jkt` per RFC 9449 §6.1, `x5t#S256` per RFC 8705 §3.1); the emit path sets only the members present on the persisted confirmation (`sdk/token/confirmation.go:48-51`) — one representation per mechanism, never two of the same family | `TestEnforceSenderBinding` (wrong-confirmation-type matrix at the token service) — run, PASS | compliant |
| 3.1 | Unknown confirmation members MUST be ignored | "all confirmation members that are not understood by implementations MUST be ignored" | Emission-side only (solid issues, never consumes third-party JWT cnf beyond the two registered members); the CWT-side equivalent (`sdk/ace/cnf.go:107-133`) ignores unknown cnf members per RFC 8747 §3.1 | `TestACEAdversarialCodecTokenResponse` "ambiguous cnf" + `TestDecodeTokenRequestUnknownKeyIgnored` (sdk/ace) — run, PASS | compliant |
| — | cnf in introspection response (used by RFC 9449 §6.2 / RFC 7662) | (RFC 9449 §6.2: the hash "is conveyed using the same cnf content with jkt member structure … as a top-level member of the introspection response") | `sdk/token/introspection.go:71-77,104-118` (`confirmationClaims` → top-level `cnf` with `jkt`/`x5t#S256`); `server/httpkit/token_introspection.go:102-106` (HTTP introspection emits `token.ConfirmationAsJSON`) | `Test_introspectionGenerator_Generate` (sdk/token); `TestTokenIntrospectionRendersX5tS256Cnf` (server/httpkit); `TestRFC8705_IntrospectionCnfRendering` (integration) — all run, PASS | compliant |

Per-standard verdict: **compliant**. Executed tests: 5 cited (2 sdk, 1 server unit, 2 integration).

| RFC 7519 §4.1 | Registered claims (iss, sub, aud, exp, nbf, iat, jti) | §4.1.4: "the 'exp' … claim identifies the expiration time on or after which the JWT MUST NOT be accepted"; §4.1.5 nbf; §4.1.6 iat NumericDate | `sdk/token/access_token.go:82-110` (iss/sub/aud/exp/nbf/iat/jti/client_id/scope assembled); `sdk/token/meta_validation.go:35-65` (iat not in future, nbf ∈ [iat, exp], exp ∈ [iat, iat+2h]) — bounded lifetime, stricter than the MAY-leeway | `TestCWTTokensRoundTripThroughTokenService` (exp in future asserted); `Test_accessTokenGenerator_Generate` (sdk/token) — run, PASS | hardened (2h max lifetime enforced at mint) |
| RFC 8725 §3.1 | Algorithm allowlist; key used with exactly one algorithm | "Libraries MUST enable the caller to specify a supported set of algorithms and MUST NOT use any other algorithms … each key MUST be used with exactly one algorithm, and this MUST be checked when the cryptographic operation is performed." | `sdk/token/jwt/builders.go:98-114` (allowlist: ES256/384/512, EdDSA, ML-DSA-44/65/87 — no RSA/HS/none; constructor panics); `sdk/token/jwt/verifier.go:43-77` (decodeHeader allowlist), `114-119` (construction-time panic), `199` (WithValidMethods); `sdk/token/jwt/embedded_verifier.go:102-117` (allowlist + embedded-key alg must equal header alg) | `TestJOSE_AlgNoneRejected` (alg none rejected before claims); `TestSignerConstructorAlgorithmAllowlist` (HS/RS/PS/none cannot construct); `TestMLDSAEndToEnd` (ES-only allowlist rejects ML-DSA token) — all run, PASS | hardened (RSA/HS families excluded by policy) |
| RFC 8725 §3.1/3.4 | No algorithm/key confusion; validate cryptographic inputs | §3.4: "Some cryptographic operations … take inputs that may contain invalid values … must validate these inputs" | `sdk/token/jwt/verifier.go:163-225` (signature must verify against the registered key set — candidate keys from JWKS only); `sdk/token/jwt/verifier.go:227-272` (strict kid routing: unknown kid in a kidded set fails closed, no whole-set fallback); `sdk/token/jwt/embedded_verifier.go:105-118` (embedded key alg binding); HPKE KEM curve validation `sdk/hpke/hpke.go:302-310` (`checkCurve`) | `TestJOSE_AlgConfusionES256HeaderWrongKey` (foreign ES256 key rejected); `TestJOSE_KidInjectionUnknownKid` (unknown kid fails even with valid signature; positive control passes); `TestKeyConversionCurveMismatch` (sdk/hpke) — all run, PASS | hardened (strict kid routing) |
| RFC 8725 §3.3 | Validate all operations (nested JWTs) | "All cryptographic operations used in the JWT MUST be validated and the entire JWT MUST be rejected if any of them fail to validate … also for Nested JWTs" | Sign-then-encrypt chain: `sdk/token/encrypted.go:28-65`; HPKE verifier `Claims` requires successful decrypt AND inner signature verification (`sdk/token/hpke/verifier.go:100-111`) | `TestHPKETamperedAccessTokenRejected`, `TestHPKEAlgSwapAttack`, `TestHPKEWrongKeyRejected` (integration) — all run, PASS | compliant |
| RFC 8725 §3.11 | Explicit typing via typ | "Explicit JWT typing is accomplished by using the 'typ' Header Parameter … Explicit typing is RECOMMENDED for new uses of JWTs." | `sdk/token/api.go:61-75` (`HeaderType` derives `<base>+jwt` / `application/<base>+cwt`); per-type constants `sdk/token/api.go:28-59`; JARM decoder enforces `jarm+jwt` (`sdk/jarm/decoder.go:79-86`, `sdk/jarm/api.go:28`); DPoP enforces `dpop+jwt` | `TestRFC9449_TypHeaderHardening` (typ=JWT proof rejected); JARM: `TestJARM_GarbageRejected`, `TestJARM_TamperedPayloadRejected`, `TestJARM_ForeignSignatureRejected` + sdk case "invalid response type" (`Test_jwtDecoder_Decode`) — all run, PASS | compliant |
| RFC 8725 §3.3 / RFC 7516 §4.1.2 | No duplicated header members | (RFC 8725 §3.3 requires rejection on any failed validation; duplicate members are a known JWT/JWE parsing hazard — enforced at the JWE layer) | `sdk/token/hpke/verifier.go:411-447` (`checkNoDuplicateMembers` on the protected header) | `TestHeaderValidationFailClosed` case "duplicated header members" (sdk/token/hpke) — run, PASS | compliant |
| — | `jwks_uri` must not be an SSRF/staging vector in the auth path | (RFC 8725 §3.10 "Do Not Trust Received Claims" family: header-supplied key locations are attacker-influenced) | `server/clientauthentication/*` resolves keys from the registered inline JWKS only; an attacker-supplied `jwks_uri` is never fetched | `TestJOSE_JwksUriIgnoredAtAuth` (jwks_uri-only client assertion → invalid_client, URI never fetched) — run, PASS | hardened |

Per-standard verdict: **hardened** — allowlist restricted to EC/EdDSA/ML-DSA (no RSA/HS), strict kid routing, jwks_uri never fetched in the auth path. Executed tests: 4 integration JOSE + 8 integration JARM + 6 sdk/token/jwt + 1 sdk/jarm unit cited. Test counts by package (this agent, `-count=1 -v`): sdk/token/jwt 6, sdk/jarm ok.

### 2.32. RFC 7797 — JWS Unencoded Payload Option — **not-implemented** (no `b64` code surface; JWTs cannot parse unencoded payloads — consistent with §7's own prohibition for JWTs, but the standard is not covered)

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (condensed) | Code evidence | Proof | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 / 4 / 7 | b64:false changes the signing input; JWTs MUST NOT use it | §3: "'b64' … determines whether the payload is represented … as ASCII(BASE64URL(JWS Payload)) or as the JWS Payload value itself"; §7: "For interoperability reasons, JSON Web Tokens [JWT] MUST NOT use 'b64' with a 'false' value." | No `b64` handling exists in the codebase (grep over `sdk/`, `server/` finds no b64 header parameter code — the only `b64` hits are base64 encoders and the RFC 9964 thumbprint canonical string `sdk/jwk/mldsa.go:379`). The JWT verifier only accepts the §3 `b64=true` formula: `sdk/token/jwt/verifier.go:43-58` + `99-108` (three segments, payload segment base64url-decoded then JSON-unmarshaled) — a b64:false token cannot decode, failing closed; there is no explicit b64-header rejection branch, the unencoded representation itself is unreachable | `Test_defaultVerifier_Parse` / `Test_defaultVerifier_Verify` (sdk/token/jwt; malformed-segment cases) — run, PASS; no explicit b64:false adversarial test exists (the representation cannot parse) | compliant (the option is simply not implemented; unencoded payloads fail the base64url/JSON payload decode — fail-closed by construction) |

Per-standard verdict: **not-implemented** (no `b64` code surface exists; the JWT verifier only accepts the §3 base64url formula, so a `b64:false` token fails closed).

| 3.1 | At most one key-representation member | "The 'cnf' claim value MUST represent only a single proof-of-possession key. At most one of the 'COSE_Key' and 'Encrypted_COSE_Key' confirmation values … may be present." (Table 1: COSE_Key 1, Encrypted_COSE_Key 2, kid 3) | `sdk/ace/cnf.go:20-27` (member keys 1/2/3, RFC 8747 Table 1); `sdk/ace/cnf.go:56-57` (Encrypted_COSE_Key deliberately not carried — asymmetric-only posture); `sdk/ace/cnf.go:48-61` (exactly one representation populated); ambiguous COSE_Key+kid input deterministically resolves to COSE_Key (`integration/ace_adversarial_test.go:161-172`) | `TestACEAdversarialCodecTokenResponse` "ambiguous cnf" (decodes to exactly one representation, COSE_Key wins — never two); `TestEncodeCnfKid` / `TestEncodeCnfCOSEKeyRoundTrip` — all run, PASS | compliant |
| 3.2 | Asymmetric PoP key by value: COSE_Key (kty EC2, crv, x, y) | "the 'COSE_Key' member is a COSE_Key representing the corresponding asymmetric public key … The COSE_Key MUST contain the required key members for a COSE_Key of that key type" | `sdk/ace/cnf.go:29-77` (EC2 members: kty 2, crv P-256 1, x −2, y −3, optional kid; `EncodeCnfCOSEKey`); `sdk/ace/token_request.go:38-56` (req_cnf key 4, "public key carried by value per RFC 8747 section 3.2") | `TestEncodeCnfCOSEKeyRoundTrip` (golden wire `a404a101a4010220012144…` — key 4 carrying `{1:{1:2, -1:1, -2:x, -3:y}}`); `TestACEAdversarialCodecTokenRequest` "req_cnf coSE key with truncated coordinates" — run, PASS | compliant |
| 3.4.1 | PoP key by reference: kid | "The proof-of-possession key can also be identified using a Key ID … a 'cnf' claim … whose value is a CBOR map containing a 'kid' member identifying the key." | `sdk/ace/cnf.go:79-83` (`EncodeCnfKid` → `{3: bstr}`); `sdk/ace/cnf.go:52-54` (in the coap_dtls profile the kid carries the RFC 8705 x5t#S256 reference) | `TestEncodeCnfKid` (golden wire `{8: {3: b"kidbytes"}}` = `a108a103486b69646279746573`); `TestACEAdversarialCodecTokenRequest` "req_cnf kid forgery decodes but carries only a reference" (codec surfaces the reference; AS is the trust boundary) — run, PASS | compliant |
| 3.3 | Encrypted_COSE_Key (symmetric keys) | "When the key held by the presenter is a symmetric key, the 'jwe'… member is an encrypted … key" (parallel of §3.3 in RFC 7800) | Not implemented: `sdk/ace/cnf.go:56-57` — "Encrypted_COSE_Key (section 3.3) is deliberately not carried: the SDK only supports asymmetric PoP keys (no HSxxx, repo rule)" | Absence is asserted by the allowlist posture tests (no HS* anywhere: `TestSignerConstructorAlgorithmAllowlist` rejects HS256/384/512) — run, PASS | divergent-by-design (stricter: symmetric PoP keys unsupported, consistent with the no-HS* repo rule) |

#### Slice scope note — Privacy, Selective Disclosure & ACE — Per-Standard Compliance Sections

All RFC statements quoted from the vendored texts under `docs/rfcs/` (SHA-256 pinned in `docs/rfcs/index.jsonl`). All file:line anchors verified against the current tree (branch `main`, HEAD `16961af`). Test proofs executed on 2026-10-14 unless noted "relied on coordinator/full-suite run".
