### 2.35. RFC 9901 — Selective Disclosure for JWTs (SD-JWT) — **hardened** (sha-256-only, KB required by default)

RFC 9901 (November 2025, Standards Track) defines selective disclosure for JWT claims via salted-digest disclosures. Implementation: `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/` (Issuer/Holder/Verifier roles), core walk/process in `sdk/sdtoken/` (format-agnostic), adversarial proof in `integration/sdjwt_adversarial_test.go` (15 subtests), role-level SDK proof in `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/sdjwt_test.go` + fuzz + bench.

**Status: hardened**

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (vendored text) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §4.2.3 | Digest over base64url disclosure string | "The digest MUST be computed over the US-ASCII bytes of the base64url-encoded value that is the Disclosure… The input to the hash function MUST be the base64url-encoded Disclosure, not the bytes encoded by the base64url string" | `sdk/sdtoken/digest.go:30-37` (`Digest` = SHA-256 over wire bytes; only sha-256 supported — defensive posture) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/tampered_disclosure_claim_value` — tampering a disclosure value breaks the digest match and is rejected (executed) |
| §4.2.4.1 | `_sd` arrays must hide claim order | "The Issuer MUST hide the original order of the claims in the array. To ensure this, it is RECOMMENDED to shuffle the array of hashes, e.g., by sorting it alphanumerically or randomly" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/issuer.go:87-95` — every `_sd` array sorted lexicographically (`sort.Strings`) before write-back | `TestSDJWTAdversarial` positive-control fixtures + `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/sdjwt_test.go` (36 tests pass in `./sdk/sdtoken/...`, executed) |
| §4.2.5 | Decoy digests from CSPRNG, same hash | "It is RECOMMENDED to create the decoy digests by hashing over a cryptographically secure random number… The same digest function as for the Disclosures MUST be used" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/issuer.go:79-85` + `addJSONDecoys` `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/issuer.go:207-227` — bare digests over `base64url(crypto/rand)` salts, no disclosure object (RFC 9901 form) | `TestSDATAdversarial/jwt/positive_control_full_flow` issues with `WithDecoyDigests(2)` and verifies (executed); `integration/sd_idtoken_http_test.go:85` harness uses `WithDecoyDigests(2)` (executed in `TestSDIDTokenHTTPResponse`) |
| §4.2.6 / §7.1 | Recursive disclosures processed children-first | "all Disclosures are valid and correspond to a respective digest value in the Issuer-signed JWT (directly in the payload or recursively included in the contents of other Disclosures)" | `sdk/sdtoken/walk.go:113` (inner markers before outer ones); `sdk/sdtoken/process.go:84` catches recursive "child without parent" | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/recursive_disclosure_child_without_parent` — nested `street_address` disclosure without its enclosing `address` disclosure is rejected (executed) |
| §4.2.3 / §9.3 | Salt ≥128 bits, cryptographically random, per claim | "each salt MUST be created in such a manner that it is cryptographically random, sufficiently long… The RECOMMENDED minimum length of the randomly generated portion of the salt is 128 bits" | `sdk/sdtoken/digest.go:26-28` (`saltLengthBytes = 16` = 128 bits) + `sdk/sdtoken/digest.go:47-56` (`NewSalt` from `crypto/rand`) | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/sdjwt_test.go` issuance round-trips (salt length pinned; 36 tests pass executed); override only via `WithSaltFactory` for test vectors |
| §9.2 | Verifier must check disclosure digests against signed SD-JWT | "The Verifier MUST check the Disclosures to ensure that the values of the claims are correct, i.e., the digests of the Disclosures are actually present in the signed SD-JWT" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:82-94` — every disclosure processed through `sdtoken.Process` with digest-site matching; unsolicited/duplicate disclosures rejected (`sdk/sdtoken/process.go:27-28,137-153`) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/extra_disclosure_not_in_sd-jwt`, `/same_disclosure_presented_twice`, `/forged_disclosure_colliding_with_plaintext_claim` (all executed) |
| §9.5 / §7.3 | Key binding: holder proves possession; verifier must not decide KB policy from presence | "Without Key Binding, a Verifier only gets the proof that the credential was issued by a particular Issuer, but the credential itself can be replayed by anyone"; §7.3 step 1: "This decision MUST NOT be based on whether or not a Key Binding JWT is provided" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:61-64` — KB required by default (`ErrKeyBindingRequired`) regardless of presence; `sdk/dpop/verifier.go:118-137` KB validation (signature, typ, claims) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/kb_required_but_absent` (no-KB presentation rejected), `/forged_kb-jwt_with_different_holder_key`, `/kb_nonce_replay`, `/kb_iat_outside_freshness_window` (executed) |
| §4.3.1 | sd_hash over the exact SD-JWT part incl. trailing `~` | "The sd_hash value MUST be computed over the US-ASCII bytes of the encoded SD-JWT, i.e., the Issuer-signed JWT, a tilde character, and zero or more Disclosures… each followed by a tilde character" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/holder.go:125-129` (`sdHashOf(presentation)` over JWT~D~…~D~ with trailing `~`); enforced `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:251-269` (`checkKBSDHash`) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/sd_hash_over_different_disclosure_subset` — KB-JWT spliced onto a different disclosure subset fails sd_hash (executed); `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/kb_over_different_disclosure_subset` (executed) |
| §4.3.2 / §7.3 step 5d | KB-JWT typ MUST be `kb+jwt` | "Check that the typ of the Key Binding JWT is kb+jwt" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:131-134` (`checkTyp(..., TypeKeyBinding, exact=true)`); issuer typ policy `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:271-275` (non-empty, ends `+sd-jwt`) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/invalid_typ_values` (both issuer typ and kb typ rejection), `TestSDATAdversarial/jwt/kb_typ_not_kb+jwt` (executed) |
| §7.1 step 2d / §4.1.1 | `_sd_alg` understood; unsupported rejected | "Check that the _sd_alg claim value is understood and the hash algorithm is deemed secure" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/holder.go:166-182` (`checkSDAlg`: absent defaults sha-256, anything ≠ sha-256 rejected); `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:77-80` | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/unsupported__sd_alg` — hand-signed JWT with `_sd_alg: sha-512` rejected (executed) |
| §7.1 step 2a | `alg: none` MUST NOT be accepted | "Ensure that the used signing algorithm was deemed secure… The 'none' algorithm MUST NOT be accepted" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:66-70` — issuer JWT verified via injected `token.Verifier` (alg allowlist enforced at construction, `jwt.SupportedSignAlgorithms()`) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/unsigned_issuer_jwt` — `alg: none` unsigned issuer JWT rejected (executed) |
| §9.11 | Explicit typing recommended; verifier checks typ | "it is RECOMMENDED that application profiles of SD-JWT specify an explicit type by including the typ header parameter… and that Verifiers check this value" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:72-75` (issuer typ `+sd-jwt` suffix enforced), `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/issuer.go:37-40` (RawTypedSigner `vc+sd-jwt`); draft-forten profile keeps `at+jwt` per its §3 (see §3 below) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/invalid_typ_values` (executed) |
| §7.1 step 3c (format) | Unmatched digests ignored; trailing `~` terminates | "If no such Disclosure can be found, the digest MUST be ignored"; §4 compact serialization `JWT~D~…~D~` | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:54-59` (Parse) + `sdk/sdtoken/process.go:116-117` (unmatched sites tolerated — decoys) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/truncated_trailing_tilde` (dropped `~` rejected), `/malformed_split` (trailing garbage rejected) (executed) |
| §7.2 | Holder processing: every digest must have a disclosure | the Holder must hold a disclosure for every redacted claim it wants to present (§3.1/§7 framing — paraphrase) | `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/holder.go:51-111` — `Present` verifies issuer signature, holder-semantics processing (`ProcessOptions{HolderSemantics: true}`, `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/holder.go:82`), duplicate-free subset check (`sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/holder.go:86-101`) | `TestSDJWTAdversarial/same_disclosure_presented_twice` (holder rejects duplicate at present time) — executed |

**Divergences / hardening notes (all stricter-direction):**
1. **sha-256-only** (`sdk/sdtoken/digest.go:33`): RFC 9901 §4.1.1 permits registering other hash algorithms; the repo pins sha-256 in both profiles and rejects everything else — hardening per OR-3 (defensive single option).
2. **KB required by default at the Verifier** (`sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:35-41`): RFC 9901 leaves KB policy to the verifier; the default posture requires KB (5-min freshness window + 60s leeway) — stricter.
3. **Audience + nonce validation mandatory** on KB (`sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/options.go` config used in `checkKBAudience`/`checkKBNonce`, `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:199-232`) — stricter than the RFC's example minimums.

**Unverified:** none for RFC 9901 — every row above is backed by an executed Go test or the executed `./sdk/sdtoken/...` suite (36 passing).

### 2.36. draft-ietf-spice-sd-cwt-08 — Selective Disclosure for CBOR Web Tokens (SD-CWT) — **partial** (encrypted disclosures §12 out of scope — ledger #13; all covered §s hardened)

Draft-08 (expires 2026-12-03) maps SD-JWT semantics onto COSE_Sign1/CWT with CBOR-specific constraints. Implementation: `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/` (Issuer/Holder/Verifier, KBT), CBOR-constraint checks in `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go`, adversarial proof in `integration/sdcwt_adversarial_test.go` (15 subtests). The core walk/process is shared with the JWT side (`sdk/sdtoken/`).

**Status: hardened**

| Draft § | Requirement (short) | Draft statement (vendored text) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §5.1 | Reject indefinite-length CBOR | "Encoders of SD-CWT and KBT MUST NOT send indefinite length CBOR. Decoders… MUST reject any SD-CWT or KBT received containing indefinite length CBOR" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go` + `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/definite.go` (`checkDefiniteLength` invoked at `sdk/dpop/verifier.go:98-101` on every KBT) | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/indefinite-length_cbor` — `0x9f…0xff` payload rejected (executed) |
| §5.2 | Date claims finite, no NaN/Inf, \|v\| ≤ 2^53 | "The standard CWT claims exp, nbf, and iat MUST be finite numbers… use of floating point numbers smaller than -(2^53) and larger than 2^53 is FORBIDDEN" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:36-38` (`maxNumericDateAbs = 1<<53`), `checkDateClaims` `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:117-138`, `isNaNOrInf` `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:140-142` | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/exp_claims_with_invalid_float_values` — NaN, +Inf, 2^54 rejected (executed) |
| §5.3 | Map keys: int or text ≤255 octets (+ simple 59 / pre-issuance tags) | "exclusively contain map keys… exclusively contain map keys with the following map key types: unsigned integers; negative integers; text strings with a length no greater than 255 octets" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:33-34` (`maxTextKeyLen = 255`), `checkClaimKey` `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:93-115` (no bstr/float keys, no nested tags) | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/invalid_map_key_types` — bstr key and 256-byte text key rejected (executed) |
| §5.4 | Duplicate map keys MUST be rejected | "Implementations MUST NOT send multiple map keys inside the same CBOR map having the same CBOR Preferred Encoding… This applies to any map anywhere in an SD-CWT or a KBT" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:40-52` (`dupMapKeyDecMode` with duplicate rejection), `enforceDuplicateMapKeys` `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:151-159` | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/duplicate_map_keys` — `a2 01 61 61 01 61 62` re-signed payload rejected at holder or verifier stage (executed) |
| §5.5 | Depth limit (MAY reject >16) | "Issuers, Holders, and Verifiers MAY reject SD-CWT Claims Sets exceeding a depth of 16 levels" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:29-31` (`maxClaimsDepth = 16`, enforced defensively — MAY made MUST) | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/claims_depth_17` — 17-level nested map rejected (executed) |
| §6.1 | Per-disclosure salt ≥128 bits | "The Issuer MUST generate a unique cryptographically random salt with at least 128-bits of entropy for each Salted Disclosed Claim" | `sdk/sdtoken/digest.go:26-28` (128-bit salt, shared constant with RFC 9901) + `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/issuer.go:97-99` (default `sdtoken.NewSalt`) | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/sdcwt_test.go` issuance tests (suite executed: `./sdk/sdtoken/...` 36 passing) |
| §8.1 | KBT: typ 294, aud MUST, iss/sub MUST NOT, iat or cti | "The protected header of the KBT MUST include the typ header parameter with the unsigned integer value of 294… The aud claim MUST be included… the sub and iss of a KBT are implied from the cnf claim… and MUST NOT be present in the KBT" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/api.go:30-36` (`MediaTypeSdCWT=293`, `MediaTypeKbCWT=294`); `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:108-111` (`checkKBTType`), forbidden iss in `verifyKBTBinding`; `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:134-137` | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/audience_mismatch_and_forbidden_iss_sub` (KBT carrying iss rejected), `/wrong_typ_values` (SD-CWT with KBT typ 294 and KBT with 293 both rejected) (executed) |
| §9 intro | cnonce SHOULD be required by verifier | "To protect against replay attacks, the Verifier SHOULD provide a nonce, and reject requests that do not include an acceptable nonce (cnonce) in the KBT" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:51-57` — `WithAudience` + `WithCnonceValidator` are **mandatory options** (SHOULD made MUST); `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:78-81` | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/cnonce_replay_and_absence` — second presentation with the same cnonce rejected (executed) |
| §9 step 2 | Empty sd_claims array invalid | "Verifiers MUST treat an sd_claims or sd_aead_encrypted_claims unprotected Header Parameter with an empty array as invalid" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:203-209` (`processSDCWTDisclosures`) — decodes sd_claims (key 17) and rejects empty | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/empty_sd_claims_array` — empty array `{17: []}` rejected (executed) |
| §9 step 7 | KBT/SD-CWT time-claim inequality chain | "exp in the KBT <= exp in the SD-CWT… iat in the KBT >= iat in the SD-CWT… nbf in the KBT < exp in the SD-CWT (if both exist)" (full list §9 step 7) | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:84-88` → `checkKBTTimeConstraints` (full chain incl. KBT-exp ≤ SD-CWT-exp, KBT-iat ≥ SD-CWT-iat) | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/time_constraint_violations` — KBT exp beyond SD-CWT exp and KBT iat before SD-CWT iat both rejected (executed) |
| §9 step 8 | Unsolicited disclosure invalid; any order processable | "If there are any disclosures that do not have a corresponding Redacted Claim Hash, the entire SD-CWT is invalid… A Verifier MUST be prepared to process disclosures in any order" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:91` (`processSDCWTDisclosures` over `sdk/sdtoken.Process` digest-keyed lookup — order-independent) | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/disclosure_without_matching_redacted_claim_hash` (forged disclosure rejected) and `/positive_control_with_shuffled_disclosures` (reversed order still validates) (executed) |
| §10 | Decoy digests: digest + 1-element decoy disclosure, unique salts | "The list of disclosures sent by the Issuer to the Holder contains one disclosure for each decoy digest. Each disclosure contains a single element array with a per-decoy salt. Each salt MUST be unique" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/issuer.go:76-83` + `addCBORDecoys` `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/issuer.go:290-330` — decoy digest + 1-element `[salt]` disclosure per decoy, fresh CSPRNG salt each | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/sdcwt_test.go` (decoy round-trips; suite executed); holder decodes 1-element arrays as `IsDecoy` (`sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/codec.go:71-98`) |
| §9 step 5 / §8.1 | KBT signature verified with SD-CWT cnf key | "Using the confirmation key, the Verifier validates the KBT as described in Section 7.2 of [RFC8392]" | `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:134-137` (`verifyKBTBinding` extracts cnf key, verifies KBT COSE signature) | `TestSDCWTAdversarial/kbt_signed_by_wrong_holder_key` — attacker-key KBT rejected (executed) |

**Divergences / hardening notes:**
1. **Depth-16 rejection enforced, not optional** (`sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/cborcheck.go:31`): draft says MAY reject; repo always rejects — stricter.
2. **cnonce + audience verification mandatory** (`sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/verifier.go:51-57`): draft SHOULD → repo MUST (missing option = construction error).
3. **sd_alg = sha-256 only**: draft permits COSE hash algorithms (registry values; e.g. sd_alg -16 for SHA-256, others registered); the repo rejects anything but SHA-256 (`sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/holder.go:365` `checkSDAlg`, pinned via `sdk/sdtoken/digest.go:33`). Stricter.
4. **Encrypted disclosures (§12) not implemented** — the draft's AEAD-encrypted-disclosure mechanism is out of scope of the repo's SD-CWT surface. Partial-surface note, not a divergence against a MUST the repo claims.

**Unverified:** none — all rows executed.

### 2.37. draft-forten-oauth-sd-jwt-access-token-00 — SD for JWT Access Tokens Without Changing the Token — **hardened**

Experimental draft (expires 2027-04-06): RFC 9068 JWT access tokens whose selectively disclosable claims travel in a `disclosures` token-response parameter and two Structured-Fields HTTP fields, with DPoP as the binding mechanism. Implementation: profile factory `sdk/sdtoken/` (`sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/access_token.go`, `sdk/sdtoken/profile.go`, `sdk/sdtoken/profile_rules.go`, `sdk/sdtoken/structuredfields.go`), JWT adapter `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/access_token.go`, CWT adapter `sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/access_token.go`, server-side generator wiring `sdk/token/sd` + `server/services/token` (harness in `integration/sdat_http_test.go`).

**Status: hardened**

| Draft § | Requirement (short) | Draft statement (vendored text) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §3 | Recursive disclosures MUST NOT be used; every disclosure names a top-level claim | "the … array elements, nested _sd claims and the recursive Disclosures of Section 4.2.6 of [RFC9901] MUST NOT be used. Every Disclosure then names a top-level claim and stands on its own" | `sdk/sdtoken/profile_rules.go:40-127` (`ValidateDisclosableClaims`: `ErrProtectedClaim`, `ErrNestedDisclosable`; `rejectNestedMarkers` walks all depths) | `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/nested_marker_rejected` and `/marker_inside_marker_value_rejected__recursive_disclosure_` — both rejected with `ErrNestedDisclosable` (executed) |
| §3 | Protected claims MUST stay in the clear | "The following claims, with their contents, MUST be in the clear and MUST NOT be selectively disclosable: iss, exp, aud, sub, client_id, iat, jti, cnf, and scope and nbf when present… The same applies to any claim whose absence would widen what the Token permits, such as act [RFC8693] with everything nested in it" | `sdk/sdtoken/profile.go:62-76` (`AccessTokenProfile.ProtectedClaims`: iss, sub, aud, exp, nbf, iat, jti, client_id, cnf, scope, authorization_details, act, may_act); enforcement `sdk/sdtoken/profile_rules.go:40-98` | `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/issuer_protected_claim_marked_disclosable` (sub, scope, cnf, authorization_details, act, may_act all rejected) and `/element_marker_under_protected_array_claim_rejected` (scope/authorization_details array elements rejected — the "widen what the Token permits" clause) (executed) |
| §3 | typ stays `at+jwt` (RFC 9068) | "The header is that of Section 2.1 of [RFC9068], including typ at+jwt… this profile keeps the [RFC9068] value" | `sdk/sdtoken/profile.go:65` (`ExpectedTyp: "at+jwt"`); verifier typ check exact-match (`sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/access_token.go`) | `TestSDATAdversarial/jwt/typ_confusion_vc+sd-jwt` (credential typ rejected by the access-token verifier) and `/cross-profile_typ_confusion` (`at+jwt` rejected by the ID-token profile verifier) (executed) |
| §4 | Token response MUST carry `disclosures` (JSON array), fresh on refresh | "the authorization server MUST include a disclosures parameter whose value is a JSON array of the Disclosure strings issued with, and committed to by digest in, that Token. This holds for a refresh as for the initial issuance" | Harness wires the response parameter: `integration/sdat_http_test.go:83-89` (`tokensd.AccessTokenWithSelectiveDisclosure` with `ResponseParameterDisclosures`, `sdk/sdtoken/profile.go:118`); digest commitment asserted on both mints | `TestSDATHTTPResponseCarriesDisclosures` — response carries exactly email+name disclosures; refreshed response carries **fresh** disclosures whose digests are committed in the refreshed token's `_sd` (`integration/sdat_http_test.go:282-309`) (executed) |
| §4 | access_token value carries no disclosures; payload has `_sd`/`_sd_alg`, no cleartext values | "They are returned apart from access_token so that its value stays a JWT that recipients unaware of this profile can parse" | `integration/sdat_http_test.go:224-244` asserts 3-segment JWT, `typ at+jwt`, `_sd` present, `_sd_alg sha-256`, no cleartext email/name | `TestSDATHTTPResponseCarriesDisclosures` (executed) |
| §5.1 | SD-JWT-Disclosures: Structured Fields List of Strings, order significant, multiple field lines combined in order | "A List of Strings, each one Disclosure in its base64url form. Order is significant. The field MAY be split across several field lines, which recipients combine in order as Section 5.3 of [RFC9110] specifies" | `sdk/sdtoken/structuredfields.go:49-73` (`ParseDisclosuresField` over combined lines; `FormatDisclosuresField` renders `"a", "b"`), grammar `sdk/sdtoken/structuredfields.go:25-41` (RFC 9651 §4.2.2 sf-string) | `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/structured_fields_combination` (split lines combine in order) and `/structured_fields_malformed` (tokens, numbers, unescaped quotes, empty items, leading commas, two-item KB field all rejected) (executed) |
| §5.1 | SD-JWT-Key-Binding: an Item that is one String | "An Item that is a String, one KB-JWT in compact serialization" | `sdk/sdtoken/structuredfields.go:75-86` (`ParseKeyBindingField`: exactly one sf-string item) | `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/structured_fields_malformed` — empty and two-item KB field rejected (executed) |
| §5.3 | Verify reassembled SD-JWT per RFC 9901 §7.3; KB key = DPoP proof key whose thumbprint MUST equal cnf.jkt | "The key that verifies the KB-JWT is the jwk in the header of the DPoP proof, whose thumbprint [RFC7638] MUST equal the Token's cnf.jkt (Section 6.1 of [RFC9449])" | `sdk/sdtoken/access_token.go:63-73` (`AccessTokenVerifier.Verify` takes the DPoP proof JWK; jkt equality enforced in the adapters — `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/access_token.go`), and `sdk/sdtoken/sdjwt/verifier.go:139-148` (`WithKeyBindingKeyProvider` path, "draft-forten section 5.3: the DPoP proof key") | `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/dpop_jkt_mismatch` — presentation with the attacker's DPoP key rejected (jkt ≠ cnf.jkt); `/kb_signed_by_attacker_key` (executed) |
| §6 | Tokens under this profile MUST be DPoP-bound | "Tokens under this profile MUST be DPoP-bound [RFC9449]: only the holder of the key can use one" | `sdk/sdtoken/access_token.go:108-115` (`WithRequiredConfirmation` — `Issue` rejects a claims map without cnf, `ErrConfirmationRequired`); harness mints with it: `integration/sdat_http_test.go:79-82` | `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/sd-at_without_cnf_under_required_confirmation` and `/sd-at_with_jkt-less_cnf_under_required_confirmation` (executed) |
| §5.3 (introspection clause) | Introspection response carries no Disclosure | "the introspection response carries no Disclosure and an authorization server MUST NOT put selectively disclosable values in one" | `integration/sdat_http_test.go:379-396` — the introspection handler's response member set never includes disclosures (iss, aud, iat, exp, client_id, scope, sub, jti, cnf, authorization_details only) | `TestSDATHTTPIntrospectionNoDisclosures` — `require.NotContains(t, body, "disclosures")` (executed) |
| §5.2 (sending clause, positive form) | Client sends the token alone; disclosures in the field; KB over the reassembled SD-JWT | "The client sends the Token in the Authorization header with the DPoP scheme and a DPoP proof… with nothing attached. In SD-JWT-Disclosures it sends any subset of the Disclosures received with that Token… each at most once" | `sdk/sdtoken/access_token.go:50-61` (`Select` by claim name — duplicate selection rejected; `KeyBind` over the exact selected subset); reassembly constants `sdk/sdtoken/profile.go:104-113` | `TestSDATAdversarial/{jwt,cwt}/duplicate_disclosure` (`ErrDuplicateDisclosure`), `/select_unknown_claim_name` (`ErrDigestMismatch`), `/duplicate_selection`, `/cross-token_disclosure_splice` (executed) |

**ID-token generalization (repo extension, `IDTokenProfile`, `sdk/sdtoken/profile.go:85-99`):** the draft-forten posture is generalized to OIDC ID tokens (`typ id+jwt`, protected nonce/auth_time/at_hash/sid/acr/amr/azp/c_hash + act/may_act; email/name/address disclosable). **No RFC mints SD ID tokens** — this surface is a repo extension wired through the opt-in D-12.1 `WithIDTokenGenerator`; default posture unchanged. Proofs: `TestSDIDTokenHTTPResponse` (id_token + id_token_disclosures, `_sd`/`_sd_alg`, pairwise sub, nonce echo, aud=client_id, digest commitment, 2 decoys) and `TestSDIDTokenDefaultOff` (no generator wired → no id_token) — both executed.

**Divergences / hardening notes:**
1. **Required confirmation enforced at issuance in the reference harness** (`WithRequiredConfirmation` in `integration/sdat_http_test.go:81`): the draft's MUST is a profile rule for the RS side; the AS-side factory makes DPoP-binding mandatory at mint — stricter.
2. **CWT serialization adapter also profiled** (`sdk/sdtoken/sdcwt/access_token.go`): the draft is JWT-only; the repo runs the same protected-claim and KB rules over the SD-CWT serialization. Extension, not divergence.

**Unverified:** the actual HTTP *field* carriage on a resource-server request path (`SD-JWT-Disclosures`/`SD-JWT-Key-Binding` parsing is unit- and integration-tested at the codec level, but no integration test drives a real RS HTTP handler reading those headers end-to-end). The parse/format functions themselves are proven (tests above).

### 2.38. Pairwise Subject Identifiers (OIDC Core §8.1 posture; formal model I5–I6) — **hardened** (BLAKE2b keyed construction)

Not a standalone RFC row-set in `docs/rfcs/` (OIDC Core text is not vendored; the repo normative anchor is `docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md` + the Quint model). Implementation: `sdk/pairwise/` (hash-based sectorized encoder), applied at authorization time in the reference assembly; formal properties in `spec/solid.qnt`.

**Status: hardened**

| Property | Requirement (short) | Grounding | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test / Quint property) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pseudonymity per sector | Same user, different clients (sectors) ⇒ different pseudonyms; no cross-client linkage | `docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md` module header ("Pairwise subjects: injective per (sector, user), no cross-client linkage"); RFC 6819 §5.2.1.1-style privacy goal reflected in model comment | `sdk/pairwise/hash.go:45-81` — keyed BLAKE2b-256 over length-prefixed `sectorID || subject`, base64url output; key = server salt ≥16 bytes (`hash.go:29-31,46-49`) | **Quint I5** `inv_pairwiseNoCrossClientLinkage` (`spec/solid.qnt:467-471`: `(t1.client != t2.client) implies (t1.subject != t2.subject)`) + witness `pairwiseDisjointnessTest` (`spec/solid_test.qnt:164-171`) — executed, `quint test solid_test.qnt` 19 passing |
| Injectivity per client | Same subject under one client ⇒ same underlying user (no collisions) | `spec/solid.qnt:473-476` model comment | Deterministic keyed hash: same (sector, subject) → same pseudonym | **Quint I6** `inv_pairwiseSubjectInjective` (`spec/solid.qnt:473-476`) — executed (same run) |
| Mandatory sector identifier | A pairwise client must not collapse sector-less clients into one domain | OIDC Core §8.1 posture encoded in code comment `sdk/pairwise/hash.go:56-58` | `sdk/pairwise/hash.go:59-62` — blank sector identifier rejected | `Test_hashEncoder_Encode` (matrix incl. blank/short-salt/empty-subject rejections) — executed, `./sdk/pairwise/` 2 passing |
| Unambiguous framing | ("ab","c") and ("a","bc") must never collide | Defensive design note (no RFC text requires framing; naive concatenation is the known failure) | `sdk/pairwise/hash.go:70-74` — length-prefix framing `%d:` before sector bytes | `Test_hashEncoder_Encode_sectorFraming` (`sdk/pairwise/hash_test.go:196-217`) — executed |
| End-to-end application | The /authorize handler applies pairwise encoding before issuing the code; the ID token sub is the pairwise subject | `integration/sd_idtoken_http_test.go:160-165` (handler applies pairwise at code issuance), `:378-381` (sub = pairwise, never the bare login subject) | `seedAuthorizationPairwise` `integration/sd_idtoken_http_test.go:160-248`; `registerPairwiseClient` `:414-438` (SubjectType pairwise + sector identifier) | `TestSDIDTokenHTTPResponse` (sub equality + `require.NotEqual(t, "hello", payload["sub"])`) — executed |

**Divergences / hardening notes:** BLAKE2b-256 keyed by a server-side salt (not the OIDC Core example sha-256/sha-384 concatenation recipe) — a stronger KDF-shaped construction; salt minimum 16 bytes enforced. Stricter-direction design choice.

### 2.39. RFC 9200 (ACE-OAuth framework) + RFC 9201 (COSE profile) + RFC 9202 (DTLS profile) + RFC 9430 (TLS extension) — **hardened** (hint-key wire bug fixed — ledger #1 resolved; RFC 9430's TLS option remains a not-exercised MAY — ledger #12)

RFC 9200 (Aug 2022, Standards Track): the ACE-OAuth framework over CoAP/CBOR. Implementation: CBOR wire codec `sdk/ace/` (presentation-agnostic), full in-process triangle over mutual DTLS 1.2 in `examples/coapace/` (AS + RS + client, `pion/dtls` v3 + `plgd-dev/go-coap` v3), adversarial codec proof in `integration/ace_adversarial_test.go` (6 test functions, 13 subtests), unit proof `sdk/ace/codec_test.go` (golden hex).

**Status: hardened** — the D1 wire-constant bug is fixed: `KeyAS = 1` per vendored Table 1 (line 818: `| AS | 1 | text string |`), Figure 2 (`/ AS / 1 :`), Figure 3 (`01 # unsigned(1) (=AS)`), and the §8.1 IANA registry (ledger #1 resolved). Every framework surface is implemented and adversarially tested over real DTLS; divergences (canonical-CBOR-only, fail-closed client_id, deterministic cnf, no Encrypted_COSE_Key) are stricter-direction.

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (vendored text) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9200 §5.8.1 | Token request over CoAP: CBOR map with abbreviated parameters | "If CoAP is used, then these parameters MUST be provided in a CBOR map (see Table 5)" | `sdk/ace/token_request.go:49-72` (`EncodeTokenRequest`/`…WithOptions` — client_id 24, audience 5, scope 9, req_cnf 4, grant_type 33); decoder `sdk/ace/token_request.go:115-171` | `TestACEAdversarialCodecTokenRequest` (type confusion, non-map, absent/empty client_id all rejected; unknown members ignored per §5.8.4 extensibility) + `TestACEAdversarialGoldenHex` (golden vector `a3…` decodes: client_id/audience/grant) — executed |

| 9430 §3/§4 | DTLS profile extended to TLS; SHOULD support DTLS, MAY support TLS | "Clients and Resource Servers SHOULD support DTLS and MAY support TLS" | The example implements the DTLS side only (CoAP over UDP); no TLS-TCP surface in `examples/coapace/`. SHOULD satisfied; MAY not exercised | `TestCoAPACEDemo` (DTLS paths) — executed. TLS path: **not implemented, not tested** — honest gap on a MAY |
| 8705 §2.1 (via coapace) | tls_client_auth: certificate SAN binding for client authentication | RFC 8705 §2.1 (client cert matched against registered binding) | `examples/coapace/server.go:56, 69, 132-162` (`clientauthentication.TLSClientAuth`, SAN-URI-registered clients, `AuthMethodTLSClientAuth`); AS token endpoint requires the cert: `examples/coapace/server.go:245-252` | `TestCoAPACEDemo` (token grant over the SAN-bound cert) + `/wrong client id` (unregistered client → 4.01 invalid_client) — executed |
| 8705 §3 (via coapace) | Certificate-bound tokens: x5t#S256 confirmation | RFC 8705 §3 (cnf carries the cert thumbprint; RS enforces) | AS mints x5t#S256 (`examples/coapace/server.go:245-267`); RS: `examples/coapace/resource.go:125-137` (introspected cnf.KID == presenting cert's `X509ThumbprintS256`), `:200-203` (`sdktoken.CertificateBound`); helper `sdk/token/confirmation_x5t.go:29-51` | `TestCoAPACEDemo` happy path + `/unknown cert gets hints` (a cert with no stored token gets 4.01) — executed |

**Divergence D1 — AS Request Creation Hints `AS` key value (RESOLVED, was a wire-compatibility bug):**
- RFC 9200 Table 1 (vendored, `docs/rfcs/rfc9200.txt:817-824`) and the IANA "ACE Authorization Server Request Creation Hints" registry both assign **AS = CBOR key 1** (Figure 2/Figure 3 hex: `01 # unsigned(1) (=AS)`); kid=2, audience=5, scope=9, cnonce=39. Fixed during this audit: `KeyAS = 1` (`sdk/ace/hints_keys.go`), with the drift test, wire-shape test, and golden hex re-pinned (Figure 3 key order 01/05/09).
- (Historical, pre-fix state): the repo encoded `KeyAS = 16` and pinned it as "registry truth" in the drift test — the demo peers only with itself, so the triangle passed while a spec-conformant third-party client would have missed the AS hint. The fix re-pins `TestACEAdversarialCodecHints` to members {1,5,9}, the drift table to `{ace.KeyAS, 1}`, and the golden hex to the Figure-3-conformant encoding; `sdk/ace`, `TestACEAdversarial*`, and `TestCoAPACEDemo` (including the hints-emitting path) are green.

**Divergences / hardening notes (stricter-direction):**
1. **Canonical CBOR encoding** (`sdk/ace/token_request.go:74-94`): RFC 9200 App. A.2 *suggests* length-first key sorting; the repo makes it the only encoding mode — deterministic wire, stricter.
2. **client_id required, non-empty** (`sdk/ace/token_request.go:119-120`): the RFC's default-grant leniency (grant_type OPTIONAL, defaults client_credentials) is kept (grant always emitted explicitly to remove ambiguity — `sdk/ace/token_request.go:44-48`), but a missing/empty client_id is a decode error — fail-closed.
3. **Ambiguous cnf resolves deterministically to COSE_Key** (`sdk/ace/cnf.go`, test "ambiguous cnf"): RFC 8747 §3.1 says at most one representation SHOULD be present; on violation the codec refuses ambiguity rather than erroring out blindly — deterministic posture.
4. **Encrypted_COSE_Key not carried** (`sdk/ace/cnf.go:56-57`): symmetric PoP keys unsupported (repo-wide no-HSxxx rule) — a narrowing of the RFC 9201 surface, declared in code.

**Unverified:**
- RFC 9200 PSK mode (RFC 9202 PSK cipher suites TLS_PSK_WITH_AES_128_CCM_8): not implemented; the demo uses certificate (x5t) binding only. RPK mode likewise not implemented (cert mode instead). Both are profile options, not framework MUSTs — recorded as scope, not failures.
- RFC 9430's TLS transport option: not implemented (see table row).

### 2.40. RFC 8252 (OAuth 2.0 for Native Apps) — **partial** (server-side §7.3 + PKCE MUST only; client-side guidance out of scope)

RFC 8252 (BCP, Oct 2017) is largely **client-behavior + user-agent guidance** (external user-agents, in-app browser tabs, custom URI schemes); the repo is an AS/SDK, so only the server-side MUSTs apply. Grep evidence for scope honesty: repo-wide `grep -rn "8252" --include="*.go"` returns exactly two hits, both in `server/services/clientregistration/service.go` (§7.3 loopback rule); `grep -rn "8252" docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md` returns nothing (the doc's native-app-relevant posture is carried by D-07 PKCE instead).

**Status: partial (server-side subset only; client-side recommendations out of repo scope)**

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (vendored text) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §7.3 | Loopback redirect URIs: http scheme on loopback IP literal, any port MUST be allowed | "Loopback redirect URIs use the 'http' scheme and are constructed with the loopback IP literal and whatever port the client is listening on… The authorization server MUST allow any port to be specified at the time of the request for loopback IP redirect URIs" | `server/services/clientregistration/service.go:403-418` (`validRedirectURI`: https always; http only for loopback hosts; no fragments) + `:420-434` (`isLoopbackHost`: localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1; port stripped — so any port accepted) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` cases "fragment redirect uri" and "non-https non-loopback redirect uri" both rejected as invalid_client_metadata; https + loopback http registrations succeed throughout the integration harness (`testRedirectURI` fixtures) — executed |
| §4 (via PKCE) | Authorization servers MUST support PKCE for native-app clients | "authorization servers MUST support PKCE for such clients" | SolID exceeds: PKCE mandatory S256-only for **all** clients (`docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md` D-07; `integration/rfc7636_pkce_test.go` — coordinator slice) | `TestDCR_MetadataHostileInputs_2_3` (implicit grant/response-type rejections) executed; PKCE matrix owned by the core-grants slice |

**Out-of-scope (client-side, informational):** external user-agent choice (§4), in-app browser (§5), custom URI schemes (§7.1) — AS-side surfaces only; no code or test claims conformance there. `ui_locales` (§6.5-adjacent surface) exists only as a protobuf passthrough field (`api/oidc/flow/v1/flow.pb.go:108`) with no server logic — no conformance claimed.

### 2.41. RFC 8996 (Deprecating TLS 1.0 and TLS 1.1) — **not-implemented** (no in-repo surface; TLS/DTLS stacks' floors make TLS 1.0/1.1 unreachable — architecture-level posture, ledger #15)

RFC 8996 (BCP 195) formally deprecates TLS 1.0/1.1. Grep evidence: **no Go file in the repo references RFC 8996** (`grep -rn "8996" --include="*.go" .` → no hits outside vendored docs). Conformance is inherited from the TLS stacks:

**Status: not-implemented** (no in-repo code surface; the TLS/DTLS stacks' floors make TLS 1.0/1.1 unreachable, but nothing in the repo implements or tests the deprecation — architecture-level posture only, ledger #15)

| RFC § | Requirement (short) | RFC statement (vendored text) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §4 (BCP) | TLS 1.0/1.1 MUST NOT be negotiated | "This document formally deprecates Transport Layer Security (TLS) versions 1.0… and 1.1" (`docs/rfcs/rfc8996.txt` abstract: "These versions lack support for current and recommended cryptographic algorithms") | No repo code pins TLS versions directly. The HTTP stacks rely on Go's `crypto/tls` defaults (TLS 1.2+ minimum since Go 1.18 — this repo is Go 1.27, `go.mod`); the DTLS stack (`examples/coapace`) uses pion/dtls v3 whose floor is DTLS 1.2 (`examples/coapace/server.go:172-177`, `examples/coapace/client.go:56-60` — no DTLS 1.0/1.1 surface reachable) | No repo test asserts TLS-version floors. **Unverified as a direct claim** — relied on: (a) Go toolchain defaults, (b) the integration suite running exclusively over `httptest` (TLS 1.3/1.2) and pion DTLS 1.2 (`TestCoAPACEDemo`, executed, negotiates DTLS 1.2 only) |

Honest statement: this is an **architecture-level conformance** (transport stacks used cannot speak TLS 1.0/1.1), not a tested repo surface. The coordinator should not represent it as test-proven.

---

### 2.42. RFC 5646 (Tags for Identifying Languages) — **not-implemented** (informational passthrough only; opaque `ui_locales`, no validation — ledger #16)

RFC 5646 (BCP 47) defines language tags. Grep evidence: the only references are protobuf-generated doc comments — `api/oidc/discovery/v1/server.pb.go:190,196` (a `languages_supported`-style field "represented as a JSON array of BCP47 [RFC5646] language tag") and `api/oidc/flow/v1/flow.pb.go:102` (`ui_locales` "space-separated list of BCP47 [RFC5646] language tags"). No validation, normalization, matching, or well-formedness checking of language tags exists anywhere in `server/` or `sdk/` (`grep -rn "5646\|language" server/ sdk/` → only the fuzz-filler `server/services/authorization/fuzzfill_test.go:61` which generates an arbitrary `UiLocales` string).

**Status: not-implemented** (no conformance claimed) — RFC 5646 is vendored as reference text only; the repo accepts `ui_locales` as an opaque string and never parses it. This is an honest gap: no code path validates BCP47 well-formedness (e.g. `und`, case normalization, subtag registry lookup), and no test claims it. The old report should not carry a "compliant" row for it.

---

### 2.43. RFC 6819 (OAuth 2.0 Threat Model and Security Considerations) — **not-implemented** (informational reference)

RFC 6819 (Informational, Jan 2013) is a comprehensive threat-model catalog, not a testable conformance target. Grep evidence: **zero direct references in Go code or `docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md`** (`grep -n "6819" docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md` → empty; the RFC is vendored and pinned in `docs/rfcs/index.jsonl` as `updated_by: RFC9700`). Its role in the repo is inherited posture:

**Status: not-implemented** (Informational threat-model catalog — nothing to implement; the repo's posture linkage is the Quint model I4, which formalizes §5.2.2.3's rotation countermeasure)

| RFC 6819 § | Countermeasure (short) | RFC statement (vendored text) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §5.2.2.3 | Refresh token rotation detects replay and revokes the family | "The basic idea is to change the refresh token value with every refresh request in order to detect attempts to obtain access tokens using old refresh tokens… the valid refresh token and the access authorization associated with it are both revoked" | SolID exceeds: rotation unconditional, replay revokes the whole grant family (`docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md` D-13, OR-2 over draft v2-1 §4.3.3) — code owned by the core-grants slice | **Quint I4** `inv_replayRevokesFamily` (`spec/solid.qnt:461-465`: `replayDetected.forall(g => allTokens.forall(t => (t.grantId == g) implies (t.status == Revoked)))`) — executed, `quint test solid_test.qnt` 19 passing (incl. `failedRedeemBlocksHonestRetryTest`) |
| §5.2.2.5 (device identification), §5.2.5 (token substitution) | Attacker-class framing | RFC 6819 structures "Threats… along the lines of the protocol structure"; §5.2.2.5 device identification etc. | The repo's adversarial suites are explicitly threat-class-shaped: `integration/ace_adversarial_test.go:18-23` ("The attack vectors mirror the RFC 9700 A5 token attacker posture"); `docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md:177` cites "attacker classes A3/A4" for PKCE | `TestACEAdversarial*` (executed) — the A5-posture codec attacks |
| §5.1.2 (access token leakage in storage/logs) | Minimize token exposure surfaces | RFC 6819 catalog entry on token storage | draft-forten §6/§7 privacy posture: values travel only in Disclosures, never in the token; introspection carries no disclosures | `TestSDATHTTPIntrospectionNoDisclosures` (executed) |

The formal linkage the user asked for: **spec/solid.qnt is the executable threat-model response** — `docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md:433` states the invariants "encode the standards' security intents (RFC 9700 §4.5 and §4.14.2, RFC 8693 §4.1.3 and §5, CIBA §10, pairwise injectivity)". RFC 6819's rotation countermeasure (§5.2.2.3) is exactly the property I4 formalizes.

### 2.44. RFC 9101 — JWT-Secured Authorization Requests (JAR) — **hardened** (sole-value aud)

Status: **hardened** — 7 integration tests (`integration/rfc9101_jar_test.go`) + 1 hardening test, all executed and passing on 2026-10-14 (`go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC9101_|TestAdversarial_JARIssMismatch' -count=1` → ok). Multi-valued `aud` is rejected (stricter than the RFC's array allowance; security-topics parity).

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (docs/rfcs/rfc9101.txt) | Code evidence | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2 | Request objects MUST NOT contain nested `request`/`request_uri` | "The Request Object MAY be sent by value, as described in Section 5.1, or by reference, as described in Section 5.2. `request` and `request_uri` parameters MUST NOT be included in Request Objects." (§2) | `sdk/jwsreq/decoder.go:106-113` rejects any claims map carrying `request` or `request_uri` | `TestRFC9101_NestedRequestUri` (integration/rfc9101_jar_test.go:83-93) |
| §5 / §6.2 | Request objects MUST be JWS-signed (or signed+encrypted) and the signature validated against a client-associated key with algorithm verification per RFC 8725 §3.1/3.2 | "The Request Object MUST be one of the following: (a) JWS signed (b) JWS signed and JWE encrypted" (§5); "The authorization server MUST validate the signature of the JWS-signed Request Object… Algorithm verification MUST be performed, as specified in Sections 3.1 and 3.2 of [RFC8725]." (§6.2) | `sdk/jwsreq/decoder.go:54-93` parses via an injected `token.Verifier` carrying a fixed ES384 allowlist (harness: `integration/harness_test.go:1008-1019`); the decoder applies no client-acceptable `alg` — out-of-allowlist algorithms fail at parse | `TestRFC9101_AlgConfusion` (integration/rfc9101_jar_test.go:115-130 — an ES256-signed request object fails against the ES384-only allowlist) |
| §2.1 / §11 | The `aud` claim SHOULD identify the AS issuer; solid enforces exactly-one audience | "If signed, the Authorization Request Object SHOULD contain the Claims `iss` (issuer) and `aud` (audience)… The value of `aud` should be the value of the authorization server (AS) `issuer`" (§2.1) | `sdk/jwsreq/decoder.go:133-141` + `audClaimIsSingle` (`sdk/jwsreq/decoder.go:194-212`): the single-element array form decodes, a multi-valued `aud` is rejected (hardening: RFC 7519 arrays allowed, security-topics 2.1.2 parity rejects them) | `TestRFC9101_AudArraySemantics` (rfc9101_jar_test.go:100-113) — divergent-by-design (stricter), covered below the row as "hardened" |
| §5 (RFC 9396 ride-along) | Request objects may carry `authorization_details` (RFC 9396 §2); the parameter is preserved, not dropped | RFC 9396 §2 defines `authorization_details` as an authorization request parameter; RFC 9101 §5 states request-object parameters are the OAuth request parameters | `sdk/jwsreq/decoder.go` decodes `authorization_details` into `flowv1.AuthorizationRequest.AuthorizationDetails` (verified by test assert on `.Type`/`.Actions`) | `TestRFC9101_AuthorizationDetailsDecoded` (rfc9101_jar_test.go:136-148), `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_JarSmuggledUnknownType` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:253-292) |

Formal model: PAR/JAR request lifecycle is modeled in `spec/solid.qnt` (`registerPAR`, `authorizePAR` actions; source map in `spec/README.md`); the `parSingleUseTest` run (spec/solid_test.qnt:68-75) pins the PAR burn-after-read referenced by the JAR §5.2 request_uri flow.

Unverified: none for the decoder surface. The HTTP-level rejection of `request`+`request_uri` together is additionally covered by `server/httpkit` unit test `TestAuthorizationRejectsRequestAndRequestURI` (server/httpkit/authorization_test.go).

### 2.45. RFC 9126 — Pushed Authorization Requests (PAR) — **hardened** (strict single-use request_uri)

Status: **hardened** — 4 integration tests (`integration/rfc9126_par_test.go`), executed and passing (`go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC9126_' -count=1` → ok). Solid treats `request_uri` as strictly single-use (RFC: SHOULD one-time, MAY allow duplicates for reloads) and enforces client binding at consume time in addition to issuance.

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (docs/rfcs/rfc9126.txt) | Code evidence | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1 | The AS returns a `request_uri` (with `expires_in`) for a pushed request | "The authorization server returns a request URI… The format of the `request_uri` value is at the discretion of the authorization server, but it MUST contain some part generated using a cryptographically strong pseudorandom algorithm" (§2.1) | `sdk/generator/request_uri.go:47-51` mints `urn:solid:` + 32 chars from `random.String` (`sdk/random/string.go:32-71`, crypto/rand with rejection sampling); `server/services/authorization/service.go:228-305` `Register` validates, checks client binding (line 268-272), generates the URI, stores with expiry (`authorizationRequests.Register` line 292) | `TestRFC9126_GeneratedUriRoundTrip` (integration/rfc9126_par_test.go:35-73 — Register → Authorize → code → redeem round-trip) |
| §2.1 | The `request_uri` MUST be bound to the client that posted the request | "The `request_uri` value MUST be bound to the client that posted the authorization request." (§2.1) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:128-131` — at consume time, `ar.ClientId != req.Client.ClientId` → `invalid_request` ("request_uri was not issued to the requesting client"); issuance-side: service.go:268-272 | `TestRFC9126_RequestUriClientBinding_2_2` (rfc9126_par_test.go:118-146 — client B presenting client A's request_uri rejected) |
| §2.2 (via §4) | Expired/unknown `request_uri` MUST be rejected as invalid; single-use SHOULD | "Authorization servers SHOULD treat `request_uri` values as one-time use but MAY allow for duplicate requests due to a user reloading/refreshing their user agent. An expired `request_uri` MUST be rejected as invalid." (§4) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:115-125` — `DeleteAndGet` (atomic burn-after-read, no Get/Delete TOCTOU window); unknown URI → `invalid_request` | `TestRFC9126_RequestUriSingleUse` (rfc9126_par_test.go:77-113 — second use fails), `TestRFC9126_UnknownRequestUri` (rfc9126_par_test.go:151-169). Divergence (stricter): no reload tolerance — strict single-use is the enforced posture |
| §2.1 (client auth at PAR) / RFC 9101 §10.2(d) | The request-object-URI service MUST perform client authentication and bind the client identifier; entropy ≥128 bits, short validity | RFC 9101 §10.2(d): "the authorization server MUST perform client authentication to accept the Request Object and bind the client identifier to the Request Object URI… the lifetime of the Request Object URI MUST be short and preferably one-time use. The entropy… MUST be sufficiently large… a cryptographic random value of 128 bits or more" | `sdk/generator/request_uri.go:36` — 32 alphanumeric chars ≈ 190 bits of entropy over crypto/rand; storage TTL returned as `expires_in` (service.go:299); client authentication at the PAR endpoint is enforced by `httpkit.ClientAuthentication` middleware wired in examples (integration/http_posture_adversarial_test.go:49-60 mirrors the assembly) | Entropy/format pinned by the generator validator in `TestRFC9126_GeneratedUriRoundTrip` (g.Validate on generated URI); auth middleware posture by `TestHTTPPosture_NoTokenValueInResponses` (http_posture_adversarial_test.go:106-161) |

Formal model: `spec/solid.qnt` actions `registerPAR`/`authorizePAR`; `parSingleUseTest` (spec/solid_test.qnt:68-75) — second `authorizePAR(0)` fails (`.fail()`), 19/19 suite runs passing (verified 2026-10-14).

### 2.46. FAPI JARM — JWT Secured Authorization Response Mode (openid-financial-api-jarm-ID1) — **compliant**

Status: **compliant** — 8 integration tests (`integration/fapi_jarm_test.go`) over the real `sdk/jarm` encoder/decoder, executed and passing (`go test ./integration/ -run 'TestJARM_' -count=1` → ok). The server enforces the JWT response mode for the code flow (harness request fixtures carry `response_mode: query.jwt`; `server/services/authorization/service.go:352-361` accepts only JWT-capable modes).

| Spec § | Requirement | Spec statement (docs/rfcs/openid-financial-api-jarm-ID1.txt) | Code evidence | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §5.1 step 2 | Client checks `state` binding to the user agent before anything else | "The client obtains the `state` parameter from the JWT and checks its binding to the user agent. If the check fails, the client MUST abort processing and refuse the response." | `sdk/jarm/encoder.go:81-87` mints `state` into the signed claims; `sdk/jarm/decoder.go:130-135` round-trips it so the client-side check has the value to verify | `TestJARM_SuccessRoundTrip` (integration/fapi_jarm_test.go:116-127 — "state must round-trip so the client can check the user-agent binding") |
| §5.1 step 3 / §7.3 (mix-up) | Client checks `iss` equals the expected issuer (mix-up mitigation) | "The client obtains the `iss` element from the JWT and checks whether its value is well known and identifies the expected issuer… If the check fails, the client MUST abort processing and refuse the response." (§5.1 step 3); §7.3: JARM detects mix-up "by providing an identification of the sender (`iss`) and the intended audience" | `sdk/jarm/decoder.go:98-102` — `SecureCompareString(claims.Issuer, d.issuer)` else `invalid_token` | `TestJARM_WrongIssuerRejected` (fapi_jarm_test.go:146-162) |
| §5.1 step 4 / §7.3 | Client checks `aud` matches its own client_id | "The client obtains the `aud` element from the JWT and checks whether it matches the client id the client used… in the corresponding authorization request. If the check fails, the client MUST abort processing" | `sdk/jarm/decoder.go:104-108` — `SecureCompareString(claims.Audience, audience)` (the audience argument is the requesting client_id); `sdk/jarm/encoder.go:61,84` sets `aud = resp.ClientId` | `TestJARM_WrongAudienceRejected` (fapi_jarm_test.go:133-142) |
| §5.1 step 5 | Client checks `exp` | "The client checks the JWT's `exp` element to determine if the JWT is still valid. If the check fails, the client MUST abort processing and refuse the response." | `sdk/jarm/decoder.go:110-118` — `claims.ExpiresAt + expirationLeeway < now` → `invalid_token` (60s leeway mirrors sdjwt/sdcwt verifiers, documented in-code); encoder side: `sdk/jarm/encoder.go:77-79` refuses non-positive `ExpiresIn` (the only public-API path to an already-expired envelope) | `TestJARM_ExpiredResponseRejected` (fapi_jarm_test.go:166-189 — the encoder must not mint an already-expired envelope; the decoder expiry gate carries the 60s leeway contract) |
| §5.1 step 6 | Client verifies the signature with the key resolved from `iss`/`kid` | "The client obtains the key needed to check the signature based on the JWT's `iss` element and the `kid` header element and checks its signature. If the check fails, the client MUST abort processing and refuse the response." | `sdk/jarm/decoder.go:74-77` — `d.verifier.Parse` (signature+alg allowlist) before any claim processing; fail-closed: no response object is returned on signature failure | `TestJARM_ForeignSignatureRejected` (fapi_jarm_test.go:194-211), `TestJARM_TamperedPayloadRejected` (fapi_jarm_test.go:215-229 — payload segment flip invalidates the signature) |
| §4 | Error responses are conveyed as JWTs with the same binding | "In case of an error response, the JWT contains the error response parameters in the same manner as with the response type `code`." (§4) | `sdk/jarm/encoder.go:52-65` — error responses carry `iss`/`aud`/`state`/`exp` (2-minute envelope) exactly like success responses; decoder validates the envelope before dispatching on the error claim (`sdk/jarm/decoder.go:95-97` "an error response must still be a properly issued, unexpired response addressed to this audience") | `TestJARM_ErrorResponseStillBound` (fapi_jarm_test.go:249-270 — the bound error decodes; the same JWT addressed to another client is `invalid_token`) |
| §4.3 | Response modes `query.jwt`, `fragment.jwt`, `form_post.jwt`, `jwt` | "This draft defines the following response mode values: `query.jwt` `fragment.jwt` `form_post.jwt` `jwt`" (§4.3) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:352-361` — the supported set is exactly `query`, `fragment`, `form_post`, `jwt`, `query.jwt`, `fragment.jwt`, `form_post.jwt`; anything else `invalid_request`; client-registered response_modes enforced at service.go:489-494 | `TestJARM_SuccessRoundTrip` uses `response_mode: query.jwt` (fapi_jarm_test.go:52, harness jarClaims integration/rfc9101_jar_test.go:52); enforcement of the mode allowlist exercised across every harness flow (`TestRFC9101_*` fixtures) |
| §5.1 (order) | The client MUST NOT process grant-type-specific parameters before all checks succeed | "The client MUST NOT process the grant type specific authorization response parameters before all checks succeed." | `sdk/jarm/decoder.go:88-108` — signature parse (74-77), `typ` header check (80-86), then iss/aud/exp checks all precede any `Code` extraction (line 131) | `TestJARM_GarbageRejected` (fapi_jarm_test.go:273-293 — malformed inputs fail closed before any envelope is built), `TestJARM_ForeignSignatureRejected` (nothing returned to act on) |

Unverified: the browser-side leg (state→user-agent binding at step 2) is a client-side countermeasure; the AS-side tests pin that `state` is present and signed so the check is possible.

### 2.47. RFC 9396 — Rich Authorization Requests (RAR) — **hardened** (fail-closed validation)

Status: **hardened** — 13 integration tests across `integration/rfc9396_authorization_details_test.go` (5), `integration/rfc9396_token_exchange_details_test.go` (1), `integration/rfc9396_adversarial_test.go` (7), plus 1 escalation test in `integration/hardening_adversarial_test.go`; executed and passing (`go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC9396_' -count=1` → ok). Metadata advertisement verified (`examples/authorizationserver/metadata_content_test.go`). Hardening: token-endpoint narrowing is exact proto-equality (no field-subset heuristics), and a narrowed refresh family can never widen back.

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (docs/rfcs/rfc9396.txt) | Code evidence | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2 | `authorization_details` is an array of objects with a REQUIRED `type` field | "The request parameter authorization_details contains, in JSON notation, an array of objects… type: An identifier for the authorization details type as a string… This field is REQUIRED." (§2) | `sdk/authzdetails/static.go:44-57` — `StaticValidator.Validate` rejects null entries and unregistered types; `sdk/authzdetails/api.go:37-44` defines the `Validator` contract (nil validator = reject every non-empty set, fail-closed default) | `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_NilValidatorFailsClosed` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:73-108), `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_UnknownTypeRejected_5` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:41-68) |
| §5 | The AS MUST refuse unknown types or non-conforming details with `invalid_authorization_details` | "The AS MUST refuse to process any unknown authorization details type or authorization details not conforming to the respective type definition. The AS MUST abort processing and respond with an error invalid_authorization_details…" (§5) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:378-384` — details validated at the authorization endpoint, error `invalid_authorization_details`; the JAR decoder preserves details so the chain fails closed at the service layer (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:249-292) | `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_UnknownTypeRejected_5`, `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_JarSmuggledUnknownType`, `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_DeviceEndpointTampering` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:299-342) |
| §6 | The token-endpoint `authorization_details` must be allowed by the grant (subset); otherwise `invalid_authorization_details` | "The AS checks whether the underlying grant… allows the issuance of an access token with the requested authorization details. Otherwise, the AS refuses the request with the error code invalid_authorization_details" (§6); §6.1 defines comparison semantics per type | `server/services/token/authorization_details.go:33-46` `validateAuthorizationDetailsSubset` — exact proto-equality match against the consented set (solid hardening: no additive-field subset heuristics §6.1 warns about); wired at grant_authorization_code.go:200-206, grant_refresh_token.go:196-206, grant_token_exchange.go:360-368 | `TestRFC9396_TokenEndpointNarrowing_6` (rfc9396_authorization_details_test.go:94-134), `TestRFC9396_NarrowingViolationRejected_6` (rfc9396_authorization_details_test.go:139-170), `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_EntryTamperingEscalation` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:114-161), `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_CrossGrantReuse` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:166-202) |
| §6 (refresh) | Refresh narrowing only; the narrowed set persists | §6.1: "upon refreshing a token, the client can ask for a new access token with 'fewer permissions'… the resource owner's previous authorization is unchanged" | `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:186-215` — subset check, then `narrowedMeta.AuthorizationDetails = grantedDetails` and the rotated tokens carry the narrowed set | `TestRFC9396_TokenEndpointNarrowing_6` (narrowed refresh keeps the narrowed set, line 120-124), `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_RefreshReplayEscalation` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:207-247 — a narrowed family never widens back; pre-rotation replay revoked) |
| §7 | The token response MUST return the granted `authorization_details` | "In addition to the token response parameters as defined in [RFC6749], the AS MUST also return the authorization_details as granted by the resource owner and assigned to the respective access token." (§7) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go` (`res.AuthorizationDetails` set in mintAuthorizationCodeTokens), `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:388-390` ("RFC 9396 section 7: the granted authorization_details are returned in the token response") | `TestRFC9396_AuthorizationDetailsEndToEnd` (rfc9396_authorization_details_test.go:60-89 — response + token metadata + refresh carry the granted set), `TestRFC9396_TokenExchangeDetailsCarriedVerbatim` (rfc9396_token_exchange_details_test.go:36-102 — exchange carries details verbatim into token, response, and introspection) |
| §10 | `authorization_details_types_supported` server metadata | "authorization_details_types_supported, which is a JSON array." (§10, metadata name registered §14) | `sdk/authzdetails/api.go:46-48` `TypesSupported`; `sdk/authzdetails/static.go:61-70`; example assembly advertises the registered types in its discovery document | `TestMetadataDocumentAdvertisesAuthorizationDetailsTypes` (examples/authorizationserver/metadata_content_test.go:38-45, executed and passing 2026-10-14) |
| §6 (client_credentials) | client_credentials details are policy-bound; solid fails closed | "The AS checks whether… the client's policy (in case of grant type client_credentials) allows the issuance" (§6) | No consent authority exists for client_credentials in this profile: any details request on that grant → `invalid_authorization_details` (integration-verified behavior) | `TestRFC9396_ClientCredentialsFailsClosed` (rfc9396_authorization_details_test.go:175-193) — divergent-by-design (stricter): details are consent-bound, and this grant has none |
| §6 (token exchange) | Exchange details must be a subset of the subject token's consented set | "The AS checks whether the underlying grant… allows the issuance" (§6, applied to the exchange subject context) | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:360-368` — subset check then verbatim assignment | `TestAdversarial_TokenExchangeDetailsEscalation` (integration/hardening_adversarial_test.go:125-162 — an entry the subject never consented to is `invalid_authorization_details`), positive: `TestRFC9396_TokenExchangeDetailsCarriedVerbatim` |
| §2 (device flow) | Details ride the authorization request (device authorization) into the session and token | §2: the parameter is part of the authorization request; §6 token-endpoint processing applies to the grant's consent | `server/services/token/grant_device_code.go` — session-consented details mint into the device token; token-endpoint details on the device grant rejected | `TestRFC9396_DeviceFlowCarriesDetails` (rfc9396_authorization_details_test.go:199-243), `TestRFC9396_Adversarial_DeviceEndpointTampering` (rfc9396_adversarial_test.go:299-342) |

Formal model: `authorizationDetailsWideningRejectedTest` (spec/xaa_test.qnt:222) pins the widening-rejection guard in the XAA model (J8 guard set); XAA suite 26/26 passing (verified 2026-10-14).

### 2.48. RFC 8707 — Resource Indicators — **hardened** (single resource per token)

Status: **hardened** — 7 integration tests (`integration/rfc8707_adversarial_test.go`), executed and passing (`go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC8707_' -count=1` → ok). Hardening: multiple `resource` parameters are rejected outright (RFC §3 only encourages a single resource) — a deliberate stricter posture against multi-audience bearer tokens.

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (docs/rfcs/rfc8707.txt) | Code evidence | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2 | The AS SHOULD audience-restrict issued access tokens to the resource(s) indicated by `resource`; unknown resource → `invalid_target` | "The authorization server SHOULD audience-restrict issued access tokens to the resource(s) indicated by the `resource` parameter." (§2); "If the authorization server is unwilling or unable to issue a token for any target service indicated by the 'resource' or 'audience' parameters, the 'invalid_target' error code SHOULD be used" (error semantics defined per RFC 8693 §2.2.2; RFC 8707 §2 uses the registered code for unknown/malformed resource indicators) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:218-226` — every requested resource URI must resolve in the registry (`s.resources.GetByURI`) else `invalid_target`; `server/services/token/grant_client_credentials.go:109-128`, `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:323-349` same rule | `TestRFC8707_UnknownResourceAtAuthorization_2_1` (rfc8707_adversarial_test.go:48-61 — unknown resource fails redemption with `invalid_target`), `TestRFC8707_UnknownAudienceRejected_2` (rfc8707_adversarial_test.go:145-167, with positive control) |
| §2 (aud binding) | The validated resource indicator becomes the token audience — attacker-controlled `audience`/`resource` strings never land verbatim | "The authorization server may use the exact `resource` value as the audience or it may map from that value to a more general URI or abstract identifier" (§2) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:379-387` `effectiveAudienceOf` — resource indicator first, audience parameter only after registry validation (lines 233-243); `server/services/token/grant_jwt_bearer.go:105-128` (ID-JAG audience/resource validated before minting) | `TestRFC8707_ResourceBindsTokenAudience_2` (rfc8707_adversarial_test.go:67-85 — with both `audience` and `resource` present, aud = the validated resource, not the request audience parameter) |
| §2.2 | Token-request resources limited to those originally granted or a subset | "In the case of a `refresh_token` or `authorization_code` grant type request, such policy may limit the acceptable resources to those that were originally granted by the resource owner or a subset thereof." (§2.2) | `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go:181-192` — refresh resource must equal the granted audience (single-resource posture: the only subset of one is itself), else `invalid_target` | `TestRFC8707_TokenRequestResourceMismatch_2_2` (rfc8707_adversarial_test.go:91-117), `TestRFC8707_RefreshAudienceInvariant_2_2` (rfc8707_adversarial_test.go:174-212 — no-resource keeps grant-level aud; granted-resource refresh succeeds; different registered resource fails) |
| §3 (solid posture) | Multi-audience expansion refused | §3: "using only a single 'resource' parameter is encouraged" (Recommendations section); §2.2 notes the requested access rights are "the cartesian product of all the scopes at all the target services" | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:209-216` — `len(ar.Request.Resource) > 1` → `invalid_target` ("multiple resource indicators are not supported (RFC 8707 section 3)") | `TestRFC8707_MultiResourceRejected_3` (rfc8707_adversarial_test.go:125-138) — divergent-by-design (stricter): a bearer token valid at multiple resources can be used by any of them against all others; solid refuses |
| §2.2 (client_credentials) | Audience validated against the registry on all grant types | "When the `resource` parameter is used on an access token request… for all grant types, it indicates the target service" (§2.2) | `server/services/token/grant_client_credentials.go:109-128` — registered audience minted into `aud`; unknown → `invalid_target` | `TestRFC8707_ClientCredentialsAudienceValidated_2_2` (rfc8707_adversarial_test.go:218-238) |

Formal model: `unregisteredResourceRejectedTest` and `resourceIndicatorWinsAudienceTest` (spec/xaa_test.qnt:188, 204) pin the resource-registry validation and indicator-wins-audience guards in the XAA model (J8); 26/26 passing.

### 2.49. draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 — ID-JAG (Cross-App Access) — **hardened** (10-min lifetime cap; pairwise subjects)

Status: **hardened** — 6 integration tests in `integration/idjag_adversarial_test.go` (including the full cross-domain loop, the 10-case adversarial matrix, RFC 7521/7523 profile matrix, and identity-chaining laundering), executed and passing (`go test ./integration/ -run 'TestIDJAG|TestIdentityChaining|TestRFC7521AssertionProfile' -count=1` → ok). The XAA formal model (spec/xaa.qnt) proves J1–J8; suite 26/26 passing (verified 2026-10-14). Hardening: assertion lifetime capped at 10 minutes; authorization_details rejected at ID-JAG issuance (no consent authority).

| §4.3 | Token exchange with `requested_token_type=id-jag` issues the grant via the `access_token` parameter with `issued_token_type` | §4.3 Token Exchange; §4.3.4 Response | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange_idjag.go:46-197` — audience REQUIRED (line 57-60), audience resolver trust (64-68), subject token refresh/id_token dispatch (72-87), pairwise subject resolution (92-96), scope narrowing only (100-110), cnf.jkt binding (122-125), target client-id mapping required (136-139), response shape (177-193) | `TestIDJAGCrossAppAccessLoop` step 1 (issued_token_type == id-jag), negative matrix in `TestIDJAGAdversarial` subtests "issuance rejects unknown audience" (invalid_target), "issuance rejects scope exceeding subject context" (invalid_scope), "issuance rejects unmapped client" (invalid_request) — idjag_adversarial_test.go:379-404 |
| §4.4.1 (typ) | The JWT typ MUST be `oauth-id-jag+jwt` | "Validate the JWT typ is oauth-id-jag+jwt (per Section 3.11 of [RFC8725])" (§4.4.1) | `sdk/idjag/verifier.go:77-80` — typ must equal `token.HeaderType(token.TypeIDJAG, format)`; misused access tokens (typ `at`) rejected | `TestIDJAGAdversarial` "redemption rejects wrong typ" (idjag_adversarial_test.go:406-417), `TestRFC7521AssertionProfile` "rejects an assertion with a mismatched typ" (667-672) |
| §4.4.1 (aud) | aud MUST be the redeeming AS's issuer identifier — string or single-element array; mismatch → invalid_grant | "The Resource Authorization Server MUST validate the aud… The aud claim MAY be a string… or an array containing a single issuer identifier. If the aud claim is an array, it MUST contain exactly one element… MUST reject the JWT with an invalid_grant error" (§4.4.1) | `sdk/idjag/verifier.go:113-117` + `audienceMatchesRaw` (229-246): single-element array normalized (145-149); multi-element array rejected; wrong aud rejected | `TestIDJAGAdversarial` "redemption rejects wrong aud" (419-427), "redemption rejects multi-element aud array" (429-437); XAA model J2 `inv_noGrantLaundering` |
| §4.4.1 (client continuity) | client_id claim MUST match the authenticated client | "The client_id claim MUST identify the same client as the client authentication in the request… MUST reject the request with an invalid_grant error" (§4.4.1) | `server/services/token/grant_jwt_bearer.go:230-232` — `SecureCompareString(claims.ClientId, client.ClientId)` | `TestIDJAGAdversarial` "redemption rejects client_id mismatch" (450-458); XAA J3 `inv_clientContinuity` |
| §4.4.1 (signature trust) | All of RFC 7521 §5.2 applies (signature verification against the trusted issuer's keys) | "All of Section 5.2 of [RFC7521] applies, in addition to the following processing rules" (§4.4.1) | `sdk/idjag/verifier.go:69-111` — algorithm-allowlisted parse, issuer resolution (fail-closed on unknown issuer), cryptographic verification honoring `kid` | `TestIDJAGAdversarial` "redemption rejects foreign-key signature" (470-479), "redemption rejects untrusted issuer" (481-490); `TestRFC7521AssertionProfile` "rejects an assertion from an untrusted issuer" (656-664); XAA J1 `inv_signatureTrust` + runs `forgedSignatureRejectedTest`, `untrustedIssuerRejectedTest` |

| §4.4.3 (replay until exp) / §5.5 chain | The ID-JAG is re-submittable until exp; no refresh token from the assertion grant | §4.4.3: "When the access token has expired, clients MAY re-submit the original Identity Assertion JWT Authorization Grant"; "The Resource Authorization Server SHOULD NOT return a Refresh Token when an Identity Assertion JWT Authorization is exchanged" (§4.4.3); chain §5.5 recommends short-lived grants | `server/services/token/grant_jwt_bearer.go:213-219` (documented multi-use posture with the 10-minute lifetime cap at 211), `server/services/token/grant_jwt_bearer.go:144-145` ("no refresh token is issued for an ID-JAG exchange"); issuance lifetime `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange_idjag.go:39` `idjagLifetime = 5 * time.Minute` (chain §5.5 short-lived) | `TestIDJAGAdversarial` "valid ID-JAG remains replayable until expiry" (507-514), `TestIDJAGCrossAppAccessLoop` asserts `res.RefreshToken == nil` (362); XAA J7 `inv_noRefreshFromBearer` + run `jagReplayableUntilExpiryTest`, `lifetimeCapRejectsLongLivedJagTest` |

All passing (verified 2026-10-14).

### 2.50. draft-ietf-oauth-identity-chaining-17 — Identity Chaining (act/may_act delegation) — **hardened** (depth cap 3, fail-closed)

Status: **hardened** — 4 integration tests (`integration/identity_chaining_act_test.go`) + 5 MCP delegation tests (`integration/mcp_delegation_test.go`), executed and passing (`go test ./integration/ -run 'TestIdentityChaining|TestMCPDelegation_' -count=1` → ok). Hardening: act-chain depth capped at 3 (fail-closed, no silent truncation); may_act gates the immediate actor (a prior-chain entry cannot smuggle authorization); re-delegation requires a DPoP proof and re-binds the confirmation to the re-delegator's key.

| Draft § / RFC 8693 § | Requirement | RFC statement | Code evidence | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| chain §2.5 / RFC 8693 §4.4 | The act chain must survive delegation: immediate actor first, then the prior chain, no dropped hops | chain §2.5 Claims Transcription: "propagate user and client identifiers, authorization context… across trust boundaries" — transcription must not drop hops; RFC 8693 §4.4 defines the act claim chain | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:294-315` — chain = [immediate actor] + subject token's chain + actor token's prior chain, duplicates collapsed by subject, capped at `maxActChainDepth` (3) | `TestIdentityChainingActChainPreserved_4_4` (identity_chaining_act_test.go:71-99 — [admin, alice] preserved in order, duplicates collapsed) |
| RFC 8693 §5 / chain §2.5 | may_act on the subject token restricts allowed actors | "may_act… restricts the allowed actors; an unlisted actor is an [error]" (code: grant_token_exchange.go:174-191) | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:174-191` — immediate actor subject must appear in the subject token's may_act; `mayActContains` (407-418) | `TestIdentityChainingMayActGatesChainedActor_5` (identity_chaining_act_test.go:106-136 — immediate actor "service" not in may_act is rejected even though prior chain lists "admin"), positive control `TestIdentityChainingMayActListedChainedActorSucceeds_5` (174-201) |
| chain §5.5 (hardening) | Deep delegation chains are a privilege-laundering vector; bounded chains | chain §5.5: mitigations "include, but are not limited to: Issuing short-lived authorization grants to minimize the window of exposure. Limiting authorization grants to a single use to prevent repeated replay…" (bullet list condensed) | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:37-45` `maxActChainDepth = 3` with fail-closed rejection (line 310-312) — no silent truncation, which would misrepresent the authorization history | `TestIdentityChainingActChainDepthCapped` (identity_chaining_act_test.go:144-169 — a 5-entry chain is rejected, no token minted) — divergent-by-design (stricter): the drafts set no cap; solid bounds delegation depth |
| RFC 8693 §5 (audience) / chain §2.3.1 | The new token's audience MUST NOT exceed the subject token's; retargeting requires delegation | chain §2.3.1: "One of resource or audience is REQUIRED to indicate the intended authorization server in trust domain B"; RFC 8693 §5 audience rules | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:318-323` — a bearer subject-token exchange cannot retarget its audience (`invalid_target`); audience registry-validated (325-336) | `TestRFC9700_TokenExchangeAudienceRestriction_4_10_2` (rfc9700_token_exchange_test.go:60-83), `TestRFC9700_TokenExchangeUnknownAudience_4_10_2` (87-100) |
| chain §2.5 (delegation policy) / RFC 8693 §5 | may_act minting is AS policy; re-delegation re-binds proof-of-possession | chain §2.5: authorization context propagates; the delegation policy defines who may re-delegate | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:338-357` — `delegationPolicy.MayRedelegate` gates may_act issuance (fail-closed on policy error, no policy → no may_act); re-bind path at 193-218 requires the may_act-listed client to present its own DPoP proof (218: "may_act re-delegation requires a DPoP proof") | `TestMCPDelegation_PolicyMintsMayAct` (mcp_delegation_test.go:97-117), `TestMCPDelegation_TwoHopChainAndReBind` (124-166 — act chain [mcp, agent], confirmation re-bound to the re-delegator's key, subject preserved), `TestMCPDelegation_ThiefFailsClosed` (172-198), `TestMCPDelegation_ReDelegationWithoutProofFailsClosed` (204-243), `TestMCPDelegation_NoPolicyNoMayAct` (248-266) |
| chain §2.4.2 | The AS in trust domain B MUST deny the request if it cannot identify the subject | "The authorization server in trust domain B MUST deny the request if it is not able to identify the subject." (chain §2.4.2) | Denial is a local-policy concern of the Resource AS (subject resolution wired per assembly); solid's reference assembly resolves pairwise subjects at issuance (`server/services/token/grant_token_exchange_idjag.go:92-96` — unresolvable subject → invalid_grant) and transcribes the grant subject at redemption (`server/services/token/grant_jwt_bearer.go:121`) | `TestIdentityChainingUnresolvableSubject` (idjag_adversarial_test.go:563-585 — documents the posture: verification succeeds, the transcribed pairwise subject rides the token; local denial policy remains assembly-defined). Marked **partial** for the RS-local policy leg: no wired test denies on unresolvable local subject because the reference assembly always resolves |

All passing.
