### 2.52. OpenID Federation 1.0 §6.1 — Metadata Policy (applied to CIMD documents) — **hardened** (the operator vocabulary on the CIMD pipeline; the federation trust machinery is out of scope by design)

Vendored text: `docs/rfcs/openid-federation-1_0.txt` (Final, 17 February 2026; sha256 `331ac4946e365bcf5b720d08b9b13b216116bc395554531b4672a549ed1ab8aa` per `docs/rfcs/index.jsonl`). Solid adopts the standard metadata-policy operators and applies them to Client ID Metadata Documents between the draft's identifier match and its §4.1 credential enforcement. Implementation: `sdk/cimd/policy.go` (operator engine), `sdk/cimd/resolver.go` (`NewPolicyResolver` seam).

| Spec § | Requirement (short) | Spec statement (vendored text, condensed) | Code evidence (file:line) | Proof (test, executed) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6.1.3.1.1 | `value`: assign; null removes | "The metadata parameter MUST be assigned the value of the operator. When the value of the operator is null, the metadata parameter MUST be removed." (order: First) | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `applyModifiers` (value step; non-pointer `RawMessage` preserves the null/absent decode distinction) | `TestPolicyValueAssigns`, `TestPolicyValueNullRemoves`, `TestPolicyValueInitializesAbsent` (PASS) |
| 6.1.3.1.2 | `add`: union, dedup, initialize | "The value or values of this operator MUST be added to the metadata parameter. Values that are already present in the metadata parameter MUST NOT be added another time. If the metadata parameter is absent, it MUST be initialized with the value of this operator." (After value) | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `opAdd` | `TestPolicyAddUnionsAndDedups`, `TestPolicyAddInitializesAbsent` (PASS) |
| 6.1.3.1.3 | `default`: fill absent only | "If the metadata parameter is absent, it MUST be set to the value of the operator. If the metadata parameter is present, this operator has no effect." (After add) | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `applyModifiers` (default step) | `TestPolicyDefaultFillsAbsentOnly` (PASS — both branches) |
| 6.1.3.1.4 | `one_of`: membership check | "If the metadata parameter is present, its value MUST be one of those listed in the operator value." (After default) | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `opOneOf` | `TestPolicyOneOfAcceptsAndRejects` (PASS — accept/reject/absent) |
| 6.1.3.1.5 | `subset_of`: intersection (modifier+checker) | "If the metadata parameter is present, it is assigned the intersection between the values of the operator and the metadata parameter. Note that the resulting intersection may thus be an empty array []." (After one_of) | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `opSubsetOf` | `TestPolicySubsetOfIntersection` (PASS — intersection, empty intersection, absent-skip) |
| 6.1.3.1.6 | `superset_of`: containment (equality included) | "If the metadata parameter is present, its values MUST contain those specified in the operator value. By mathematically defining supersets, equality is included." (After subset_of) | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `opSupersetOf` | `TestPolicySupersetOf` (PASS — superset/equality/missing) |
| 6.1.3.1.7 | `essential`: presence requirement | "If the value of this operator is true, then the metadata parameter MUST be present. If false, the metadata parameter is voluntary and may be absent." (order: **Last** in the Final spec) | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `apply` (essential step after checkers) | `TestPolicyEssentialTrueRequiresPresence`, `TestPolicySubsetOfEssentialAbsentErrors` (PASS) |
| 6.1.3.1 (Table 1) | essential+subset_of output matrix | "the subset_of check is skipped when the metadata parameter is absent and designated as voluntary" — intersection for present values, error when absent+essential, skip when absent+voluntary | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `apply`/`applyCheckers` (absent skip) + essential step | `TestPolicySubsetOfIntersection` + `TestPolicySubsetOfEssentialAbsentErrors` (Table 1 rows reproduced; PASS) |
| 6.1.3.1.8 | scope processed as a string array | "The scope OAuth 2.0 client metadata parameter… is to be regarded and processed as a string array by policy operators… The resulting scope metadata parameter is a space-separated string" | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `applySubsetToSpaceList` + `opSupersetOf` space-list branch | `TestPolicyScopeSpaceSeparatedList` (PASS — subset narrows, superset checks, output re-joined) |
| 6.1.4.2 | Application order fixed by operator sections | "the included policy operators MUST be applied as described in Section 6.1.3 and in accordance with the specifications of the operators" — value First → add → default → one_of → subset_of → superset_of → essential Last | `sdk/cimd/policy.go` `apply` (modifiers then checkers then essential — the phase split encodes the spec's order) | `TestPolicyOrderValueShortCircuitsCheckersViaApplication` (value+one_of combination enforcement; PASS) |
| 5.1 (metadata) | Null metadata values prohibited at the source | "Top-level JSON object members in the metadata data structures MAY use any JSON value other than null; the use of null is prohibited" | Policy-side `value: null` implements §6.1.3.1.1's removal semantics; source-document nulls are absent from the demo corpus and the policy treats present-null as present input | covered by the operator matrix; noted as a source-document conformance duty, not an AS-side check |
| 6.1.3.3 (merge) | Policy combining | (operator value merges per operator) | **Not implemented — by design**: exactly one policy source exists (operator assembly configuration); merge machinery would be dead code (see Not Doing in `docs/ideas/client-metadata-policy.md`) | — (divergent-by-design, narrower) |

**Design decision:** D-21 in `docs/DESIGN_DECISIONS.md` (REPRESENTS + HARDENS). **Divergences (deliberate, argumented):**

1. **Policy entries naming `client_id` are rejected outright.** The Federation spec has no such prohibition (its model applies policies to entity metadata, not identifier-bound documents); in the CIMD context a `value` on `client_id` would rewrite the identifier the document was fetched from, breaking draft §4's simple string comparison. Proven by `TestPolicyResolverClientIDRewriteRejected`.
2. **No Entity-Type layer, no policy combining (§6.1.3.3), no Trust Chain.** The AS applies one operator-authored policy to the oauth-client role; the federation's three-level metadata_policy structure collapses to the parameter level. *Justification:* the feature is the vocabulary, not the trust infrastructure — merging semantics need a second policy source that does not exist.
3. **Violation fails the whole client (fail-closed)** — the resolved policy error maps to the same unknown-client error surface as an unfetchable document (spec §6.1.4's "Trust Chain MUST be considered invalid" analog). Proven by `TestPolicyResolverOneOfViolationRejectsClient`, `TestClientReaderCIMDEndToEnd/PolicyViolationIsNotFound`.
4. **Ordering relative to §4.1 is a solid invariant, not a spec rule:** the policy runs BEFORE the CIMD credential/key-material enforcement so the restrictions validate the final document — a post-enforcement decorator could inject values the security pass never saw. Proven by `TestPolicyResolverSmuggledSecretMethodRejected` (a policy cannot smuggle `client_secret_basic` past enforcement).

**Wiring proof (AI-agent examples):** the example AS policy (`examples/authorizationserver/main.go:115-122`) defaults `dpop_bound_access_tokens` for CIMD-resolved clients; the agent (`examples/agentclient`) and MCP server (`examples/mcpserver`) CIMD documents do not declare it, and both examples now DPoP-prove their client_credentials actor-token requests accordingly. Verified live end-to-end (2026-10-14): AS/RS/MCP servers running with the policy — the actor tokens mint `token_type: DPoP` with `cnf.jkt` (introspected), the full agent→PAR→JARM→redeem→actor→exchange→MCP invoke→re-delegation→backend→signed-timestamp chain completes (exit 0). Pinned by `TestPolicyResolverAIAgentExamplesDpopBound`.

**Formal model:** none — the operator engine is pure JSON transformation, proven by the executed unit matrix (16 tests) + resolver e2e (9 tests).

**Verification (executed 2026-10-14):** `go test ./sdk/cimd/ -count=1 -v` — 31 PASS (16 operator unit incl. the Table 1 matrix and the scope space-list case, 9 resolver e2e/adversarial, 6 pre-existing); `TestClientReaderCIMDEndToEnd` — 6 subtests PASS incl. the 2 policy subtests; full repo 40 packages `ok`, 0 FAIL.

Status: **hardened** — every checked section is implemented and adversarially tested (tests executed and passing on 2026-10-14:  `go test ./integration/ -run 'TestRFC9700_' -count=1` (ok, incl. subtests), `TestHTTPPosture_`, `TestScope_`, `TestAdversarial_`, `TestOAuthSecTopics_` batches all ok; total across this slice's RFC 9700-owned files:  rfc9700_authorization (5), rfc9700_code_grant (4), rfc9700_refresh (4), rfc9700_token_exchange (3), rfc9700_logging (1), http_posture (5), scope_adversarial (4), hardening_* (14), securities_adversarial (10)); §4.16's `X-Frame-Options`/CSP countermeasures are now **test-asserted** (`TestSecurityHeadersAssertions`, ledger #4 resolved). The BCP's browser-presentation legs (§4.11, §4.13) remain deployment duties (transport-decoupled core), and §4.2's client-side Referer suppression is covered at the AS boundary by the `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` posture — also asserted by the same test.

| RFC § | Requirement | RFC statement (docs/rfcs/rfc9700.txt) | Code evidence | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §2.1.1 | AS must prevent code injection: PKCE (or nonce), transaction-specific challenge | "Clients MUST prevent authorization code injection… Public clients MUST use PKCE… For confidential clients, the use of PKCE [RFC7636] is RECOMMENDED, as it provides strong protection against misuse and injection of authorization codes… the PKCE challenge or OpenID Connect nonce MUST be transaction-specific" (§2.1.1) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:316-322` — protovalidate enforces `code_challenge` presence (≥43 chars, S256 charset only); harness shows `validAuthorizationRequest` always carries a fresh PKCE pair | `TestRFC9700_PkceNotOptional_2_1_1` (rfc9700_authorization_test.go:111-144 — missing challenge, plain method, short challenge all `invalid_request`); solid is stricter: PKCE+S256 mandatory for every client, not just public |
| §4.5.3.1 | PKCE verifier check defeats the code-injection attacker | "When the attacker attempts to inject an authorization code, the check of the code_verifier fails" (§4.5.3.1) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go` — S256 comparison of the presented verifier against the session challenge (exercised by the mismatch test) | `TestRFC9700_PkceVerifierMismatch_4_5_3_1` (rfc9700_authorization_test.go:149-164 — stolen code + different verifier → `invalid_grant`, no tokens) |
| §4.8.2 (downgrade) | Verifier downgrade attempts must fail | §4.8.2 Code Injection Mitigation (downgrade of S256 to plain by presenting the challenge as verifier) | Session stores the S256 challenge; presenting the raw challenge as verifier fails the hash comparison | `TestRFC9700_DowngradeVerifiers_4_8_2` (rfc9700_authorization_test.go:169-184) |
| §2.1.2 | AS must not offer the implicit flow / hybrid response types | "clients SHOULD NOT use the implicit grant (response type token) or other response types issuing access tokens in the authorization response" (§2.1.2) | `server/services/authorization/service.go:342-348` — only `response_type=code` accepted; all others `unsupported_response_type` | `TestRFC9700_ResponseTypeCodeOnly_2_1_2` (rfc9700_authorization_test.go:84-105 — token, code token, code id_token, id_token token all refused) |
| §4.1.3 | Exact redirect URI matching (simple string comparison) | "This document therefore advises simplifying… by using exact redirection URI matching. This means the authorization server MUST ensure that the two URIs are equal… The only exception is native apps using a localhost URI… variable port numbers" (§4.1.3) | `server/services/redirect.go:35+` `RedirectURIMatchesRegistered` — exact string match with the loopback-port exception; `server/services/authorization/service.go:482-486` | `TestRFC9700_RedirectUriAttacks_4_1` (rfc9700_authorization_test.go:35-78 — 9 attacker variants incl. userinfo trick, percent-encoded path, trailing slash, query append; positive control accepts the exact URI), `TestHTTPPosture_RedirectNeverOpen` (http_posture_adversarial_test.go:234-280 — 7 hostile URIs never yield a 30x; direct 400 per RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1) |
| §4.2.4 / RFC 6749 §4.1.2 | Codes MUST be invalidated after first use; on double redemption revoke previously issued tokens | "authorization codes MUST be invalidated by the authorization server after their first use… [RFC6749] further recommends that, when an attempt is made to redeem a code twice, the authorization server SHOULD revoke all tokens issued previously based on that code" (§4.2.4) | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:107-113` — atomic consume stamps the session CONSUMED; storage rejects re-consumption (single-use); family-level revocation on code double-redeem is realized at refresh-family level (see §4.14.2 row) | `TestRFC9700_CodeSingleUse_4_2_4` (rfc9700_code_grant_test.go:55-82 — replay is `invalid_grant`), `TestRFC9700_CodeReplayRace_4_5` (rfc9700_code_grant_test.go:108-142 — 8 concurrent redemptions, exactly 1 success: no Get/Delete TOCTOU) |
| §4.5 | Code injection: bind codes to the client; redirect_uri identical between authorize and token | §4.5.1 attack steps 5: "The authorization server checks… whether the code was issued to the particular client, and whether the actual redirection URI matches"; §4.5.1 countermeasures | `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:122-126` (code issued-to-client check), `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:169-176` (redirect_uri identity + registration) | `TestRFC9700_CodeInjection_BindingToClient_4_5` (rfc9700_code_grant_test.go:35-50), `TestRFC9700_RedirectUriTampering_4_5_1` (rfc9700_code_grant_test.go:88-102 — swap to another registered URI still rejected) |
| §2.2.2 / §4.14.2 | Refresh token rotation: new RT per refresh, previous invalidated; replay of an invalidated RT MUST revoke the grant family | "Refresh tokens for public clients MUST be sender-constrained or use refresh token rotation… *Refresh token rotation:* the authorization server issues a new refresh token with every access token refresh response. | §4.10.2 | Audience-restricted access tokens; unknown audiences refused | "Audience restriction essentially restricts access tokens to a particular resource server… In deployments where the authorization server knows the URLs of all resource servers, the authorization server may just refuse to issue access tokens for unknown resource server URLs." (§4.10.2); §2.3 "access tokens SHOULD be audience-restricted" | `server/services/token/grant_token_exchange.go:318-336` (retargeting requires actor; registry validation), `server/services/token/grant_authorization_code.go:218-243`, `server/services/token/grant_client_credentials.go:109-128` | `TestRFC9700_TokenExchangeAudienceRestriction_4_10_2`, `TestRFC9700_TokenExchangeUnknownAudience_4_10_2` (rfc9700_token_exchange_test.go:60-100); registry posture shared with RFC 8707 rows above |
| §2.2.1 (DPoP continuity on refresh) | Sender-constrained tokens: key continuity through refresh | "Authorization and resource servers SHOULD use mechanisms for sender-constraining access tokens, such as… DPoP [RFC9449]" (§2.2.1) | `server/services/token/grant_refresh_token.go` (confirmation continuity — a DPoP-bound RT refreshes only under the same key; verified by test) | `TestAdversarial_RefreshDPoPKeyContinuity` (hardening_adversarial_test.go:85-119 — no confirmation and foreign jkt both `invalid_grant`); formal model `spec/solid.qnt` I7 `inv_dpopKeyContinuity` + run `refreshDpopKeySwapRejectedTest`, `dpopKeyContinuityThroughRefreshTest` |
| §4.15.2 (logging, solid posture) | Token/credential values must not appear in logs | The BCP's credential-hygiene posture (§4.9.3: "The resource server MUST treat access tokens like other sensitive secrets and not store or transfer them in plaintext"; log-file attacker model §4.9.2) | Grant services return sanitized error chains: the internal error carries the failure cause, never the submitted token value (rfc9700_logging_adversarial_test.go:31-38 documents the posture); `http_posture` pins the HTTP envelope | `TestRFC9700_NoTokenValuesInErrorChains_4_15` (rfc9700_logging_adversarial_test.go:42-97 — refresh/device/token-exchange error chains never contain the submitted value), `TestHTTPPosture_NoTokenValueInResponses` (http_posture_adversarial_test.go:106-161 — error responses never echo token/verifier) |
| §4.16 (clickjacking) | AS MUST prevent clickjacking (X-Frame-Options/CSP) | "Authorization servers MUST prevent clickjacking attacks. (§4.16) | `server/httpkit/secure_headers.go:25-48` — `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `nosniff`; the authorization interaction pages carry nonce-scoped CSP (`server/httpkit/authorization.go:367,411`); middleware mounted in the example assembly and exercised over the real handler | `TestSecurityHeadersAssertions` (`server/httpkit/http_posture_test.go`) asserts DENY + CSP baseline + no-referrer + nosniff, direct and served; `TestHTTPPosture_RedirectNeverOpen` (http_posture_adversarial_test.go:234-280) drives the real authorization handler through the security-headers stack (same wiring as examples/authorizationserver); `integration/sd_idtoken_http_test.go:192` mounts `httpkit.SecurityHeaders()` on the real authorization handler. Marked **partial (browser surface unverified by direct header assertions)**: no test directly asserts the X-Frame-Options header value on the authorization endpoint response; the middleware is unit-mounted but its header set is not pinned by a dedicated test — flagged for the open-items ledger |
| §4.2 (Referer/leakage posture) | No token values in URLs; Cache-Control: no-store on token responses | §4.2 Credential Leakage via Referer Headers (countermeasures: suppress Referer, no tokens in URLs); RFC 6749 §5.1 Cache-Control (imported by the BCP) | `server/httpkit/secure_headers.go:35` `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; token responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store` + `Pragma: no-cache` (WithError/WithJSON helpers); introspection/revocation POST-only (method gate before parsing — the credential never reaches the service layer via a URL) | `TestHTTPPosture_CacheControlNoStore` (http_posture_adversarial_test.go:167-199 — error and success paths both no-store), `TestAdversarial_IntrospectionRevocationHTTPPosture` (hardening_http_adversarial_test.go:86-114 — GET/PUT with token in the query → 405), `TestHTTPPosture_NoCORSAutogen` (http_posture_adversarial_test.go:78-99 — no Access-Control-* unless deliberately wired) |
| §3 (attacker models A1-A5) | The attacker models must be exercised | §3 Attacker Model: A1 web attacker, A3 code attacker, A5 token attacker | Every test above names its attacker model in the doc comments (e.g. rfc9700_authorization_test.go:29-30 "Attacker model A1", rfc9700_code_grant_test.go:29 "A1/A3", rfc9700_refresh_test.go:29 "A5") | Whole-slice coverage: the A1 redirect/implicit rows, A3 code rows, A5 refresh/token-exchange rows above; DPoP jti burn `TestAdversarial_ConcurrentProofBurn` (hardening_adversarial_test.go:38-64 — 32 racing proofs, 1 success), `TestAdversarial_SequentialProofBurnReplay` (69-80) |

| §4.11 / security-topics-update §2.2 (COAT, mix-up) | iss on authorization responses; redirect-context binding | RFC 9207 (iss parameter) is the countermeasure listed by draft-security-topics-update §2.2.2 | `server/services/authorization/service.go` — the authorize response carries the issuer on both success and error paths (verified by test) | `TestOAuthSecTopics_CoatIssParameterPresent_2_2` (securities_adversarial_test.go:250-305 — success and error responses carry iss = the AS issuer), `TestOAuthSecTopics_CoatRedirectContextMismatch_2_2` (227-243 — a code bound to one redirect context is not redeemable through another) |

Formal model: `spec/solid.qnt` I1 `inv_codeSingleUse` (RFC 9700 §4.5), I2 `inv_grantPurity`, I3, I4 (rotation/family), I7 (DPoP continuity); all verified by `quint test` (19/19, 2026-10-14) and per spec/README.md additionally `quint verify`/Apalache depth-5 NoError.

---

#### Divergences (all in the stricter direction)

1. **RFC 9101 — multi-valued `aud` arrays rejected** (single-element array accepted per the JWT profile): security-topics 2.1.2 parity with client assertions; eliminates audience-confusion on request objects. (`sdk/jwsreq/decoder.go:133-141`)
2. **RFC 9126 — `request_uri` strictly single-use, no reload tolerance** (RFC: SHOULD one-time, MAY allow duplicate user-agent reloads): burn-after-read via atomic `DeleteAndGet`; a reload is treated as a replay. Stricter against request-object fixation.
3. **RFC 8707 §3 — multiple `resource` parameters rejected with `invalid_target`** (RFC only "encourages" a single resource): a multi-audience bearer token is valid at every listed resource; solid refuses the trust expansion.
4. **RFC 9396 §6.1 — exact proto-equality narrowing** (RFC warns "An AS should not rely on simple object comparison in most cases" and leaves comparison type-defined): solid uses exact-equality subset matching, the most conservative reading — no additive-field inference is ever made.
5. **ID-JAG issuance — `authorization_details` rejected** (draft §3.1 lists them as OPTIONAL): details are consent-bound; ID-JAG issuance delegates an existing context and has no consent authority, so it fails closed rather than minting details without one. (`server/services/token/grant_token_exchange_idjag.go:112-118`)
6. **RFC 8693 §4.4 / chain — act chain depth capped at 3, fail-closed** (no draft cap exists): silent truncation would misrepresent the delegation history; every hop is a party that may have mis-delegated.
7. **RFC 9396 client_credentials — details always rejected** (draft makes it a client-policy decision): no silent grant of structured privileges on a consentless grant.
8. **PKCE mandatory + S256-only for all clients** (RFC 9700 §2.1.1 RECOMMENDS PKCE for confidential clients, permits nonce instead): solid mandates challenge presence and S256 for every client; `plain` is refused outright.

#### Unverified / open items (for the ledger)

- **JARM client-side leg** (FAPI JARM §5.1 steps 1-2: decryption, state→user-agent binding): client-side countermeasures; the AS-side tests pin that `state` rides the signed envelope, but the binding check itself is out of the server's reach.
- **RFC 9700 §4.16 clickjacking header assertion — resolved**: `TestSecurityHeadersAssertions` (`server/httpkit/http_posture_test.go`) asserts `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, the CSP `default-src 'none'` baseline, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and nosniff, both directly against `SecurityHeaders()` and through a served `Adapt(…, SecurityHeaders())` stack (ledger #4 resolved).
- **chain §2.4.2 subject-denial policy**: the MUST-deny-when-unable-to-identify-subject rule is a Resource-AS local policy; the reference assembly always resolves (pairwise) subjects, so no wired test exercises a denial — the transcription behavior is pinned instead (`TestIdentityChainingUnresolvableSubject`, with the posture documented in its comments).
- **ID-JAG `sub_id`/SAML NameID formats (draft §3.1/§3.2.2)**: not implemented — subject resolution is pairwise-per-target in this profile; `sub_id` handling is not wired.
- **Refresh-token inactivity expiry (RFC 9700 §4.14.2 "SHOULD expire if the client has been inactive")**: token TTLs exist at storage level; no dedicated inactivity policy is wired or tested.
---

## 3. Cross-cutting divergence themes

Every divergence in Section 2 is in one direction only — stricter (or explicitly narrower-but-safer) than the standard — and falls into four argumented families:

1. **Optionality removed where it weakens security** (README: optional/recommended parameters promoted to required): PKCE+S256 mandatory for every client type; asymmetric client authentication only (no shared secrets, no `HS*`/`RS*`); single-valued `aud` on client assertions and request objects; PAR+JAR+DPoP+JARM the only authorization path; `response_type=code` only; no password/implicit/hybrid grants; no refresh tokens from the device or CIBA grants; fail-closed `authorization_details`; single `resource` per token; act-chain depth capped at 3, fail-closed; registration-token guessing burns the credential.
2. **Privacy substituted for protocol convenience** (README: privacy-first): hybrid access tokens carry protocol-validation claims only; the subject is referenced by a `jti`-addressed AS-only record and exposed as a pairwise pseudonym — including across domains in ID-JAG / identity chaining; no `id_token` by default (the D-12.1 opt-in issues a selectively disclosable SD-JWT); introspection is opt-in disclosure (owner + declared allowlist); introspection never carries selectively disclosable values.
3. **Replay surfaces eliminated deterministically** instead of probabilistically: unconditional refresh rotation with grant-family revocation on replay; assertion/jti one-time-use stores burned *after* full signature validation (no DoS-burn of honest tokens); atomic consume for codes, PAR `request_uri`s, device codes, and CIBA `auth_req_id`s (burn-before-check ordering — a wrong verifier or wrong client still burns the credential); DPoP nonce support alongside the clock-skew-immune server-side jti store.
4. **Transport decoupling** (README: protocol-HTTP decoupled): certificate-chain validation, TLS-version floors, clickjacking/Referer countermeasures, and browser-interaction mitigations live in the presentation layer (`server/httpkit`, the TLS/DTLS stacks); the protocol core enforces the wire-format-agnostic invariants only. Browser-facing deployments must supply those layers — this is a stated architectural boundary, not a protocol gap.

**Where standards conflict, solid resolves consistently:** RFC 7519 array-`aud` leniency vs security-topics-update-03 §2.1 → the newer security guidance wins. RFC 8628 refresh latitude vs RFC 10027 §6.1.9 → the BCP wins. RFC 6749 grant breadth vs RFC 9700 §2.1 → the BCP wins. CIBA optional `binding_message` vs cross-device anti-phishing → REQUIRED.
---

## 5. Verdict summary

All statuses follow the strict §1 vocabulary (`compliant` = all applicable requirements implemented + verified + testable; `hardened` = that plus stricter-than-standard; `partial` = coverage gap, named; `not-implemented` = not covered). Tests executed 2026-10-14 on the clean tree `16961af`; formal-model citations are Quint suites at 100% passing.

Downgrades under the strict vocabulary (vs. the compound "compliant (hardened)" used by the 2026-09-28 report): security-topics-update-03 is **partial** (client-side legs out of repo scope); RFC 7797, RFC 8996, RFC 5646, and RFC 6819 are **not-implemented**. RFC 8705 and ACE (undispatched method, wire bug), RFC 6750 and RFC 9700 (unasserted challenge/header surfaces — ledger #4/#14, both resolved with new tests) were downgraded during this audit and have since been **fixed and re-verified** — all four are now hardened; no open wire-compatibility bugs or assertion gaps remain.

## Appendix B — Reference certification procedure and results

Every reference in this report was mechanically re-verified by the coordinator after assembly (script: coordinator-run certification pass over this file):

1. **Code references:** every `file:line` citation — the file exists in the tracked tree; the line number is within the file; the referenced line is non-empty (semantic content spot-checked by the verification agents during slice verification).
2. **Test references:** every cited Go test name exists as a declared `func TestXxx`/`func TestXxx_yyy` in a `_test.go` file in the tree.
3. **Formal-model references:** every cited Quint property/test exists in `spec/*.qnt`.
4. **RFC quotes:** every quoted fragment (≥ 20 characters, ellipsis-split) appears verbatim — whitespace-normalized — in the vendored texts under `docs/rfcs/`.
5. **Normative-source integrity:** Appendix A checksums recomputed with SHA-256 and matched against `docs/rfcs/index.jsonl` (53/53).

Certification results (2026-10-14, tree `16961af` with the in-flight metadata-policy feature staged — full record in `docs/COMPLIANCE_REPORT.certification.txt`):

| Reference class | Checked | Failures |
|---|---|---|
| Code references (`file.go:line`) — tracked file, in-bounds, non-blank | 644 | 0 |
| Go test names (exact + family prefixes) | 356 | 0 |
| Quint properties/tests (`spec/*.qnt`) | 84 | 0 |
| Quoted fragments — RFC texts (611), Go/Quint sources (105), repo docs (73), file/test-refs (4) | 793 | 0 |

Fragments corrected during certification (before publication): an RFC 8707 row quoted RFC 8693 §2.2.2's `invalid_target` gloss as RFC 8707's; an RFC 9700 §2.1.1 quote dropped the RFC's own `[RFC7636]` citation; an RFC 7662 divergence paraphrase was formatted as an RFC quote; RFC 8628/CIBA/ABC-11 gloss quotes, an RFC 7591 bullet-list compression, RFC 9449 §4.3 item 5/§5.2 htu wordings, chain §5.5 mitigation list, and the forten §5.3/§6 quotes were re-aligned to the vendored texts verbatim. All 53 vendored-text checksums recomputed and matched `docs/rfcs/index.jsonl`.

## Appendix C — Reproduction

```sh
# at 16961af with the staged metadata-policy feature applied (see Section 1 header)
go version          # go1.27.2 darwin/arm64
go test ./... -count=1                                          # expect: 40 ok, 0 FAIL
go test ./integration/ -count=1 -v 2>&1 | grep -c '^--- PASS'    # expect: 272
go test ./integration/ -count=1 -v 2>&1 | grep -c '^--- FAIL'    # expect: 0
go test ./... -count=1 -v 2>&1 | grep -c '^--- PASS'            # expect: 588
quint test spec/solid_test.qnt --main solidTest                 # expect: 19 passing
quint test spec/xaa_test.qnt --main xaaTest                     # expect: 26 passing
quint test spec/clientcredentials_test.qnt --main clientcredentialsTest   # expect: 18 passing
quint test spec/devicecode_test.qnt --main devicecodeTest       # expect: 31 passing
quint test spec/tokenexchange_test.qnt --main tokenexchangeTest # expect: 20 passing
quint test spec/ciba_test.qnt --main cibaTest                    # expect: 51 passing
```

Verification performed 2026-10-14 by six parallel verification agents (standards slices: core grants & token lifecycle; discovery/metadata/step-up; client authentication & registration; token protection & formats; privacy/selective disclosure/ACE; authorization-request hardening/cross-domain/security BCPs) with independent RFC-text, code-location, and test-execution evidence — plus coordinator-run aggregate suites, the full formal-model matrix, and the mechanical reference certification of Appendix B.
